- For the next opt-in authority trial, [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `bhkRigidBody` API](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.h) identifies distinct native position and rotation setters and a linear-velocity setter; [Precision's pinned hook](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ersh1/Precision/9ef45e0d8d9e3cf8b2df444f05142c1e7bf41cd4/src/Hooks.cpp) places pre-physics work immediately ahead of the solver. Adopt a single-selected-reference, default-off velocity application at the verified native step first, with a timestamped host body target and hard bounds. This tests whether phase-correct authority can drive a constrained body through the game's own solver and subsequent body-to-scene transfer. Reject immediate full-transform teleport as the first trial: the prior post-step body-position test detached the reference/mesh from its body, and the combined position/rotation setter has not yet been validated on this runtime. A successful velocity trial would still require orientation, constraints, late packets, cell transitions, and five-player performance before any parity claim.
- [CommonLib's `GridCellArray`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/G/GridCellArray.h) and [base `GridArray`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/G/GridArray.h) place attached exterior cells in a bounded `length × length` array at +0x18, matching this project's `TES::cells` and `GridCellArray::arr` layout. The old host physics stream scanned only `PlayerCharacter::parentCell`; this is not enough when a moving body remains active in a different attached tile. Adopt a same-worldspace attached-grid scan with distance and dimension caps, deduplicating references, rather than special-casing intro carts. It does not yet solve followers in wholly separate worldspaces or server-side cell-interest routing.
- The first opt-in native-step velocity trial (`authority-20260923-130917.json` through `-130943.json`) accepted hundreds of corrections but the selected cart remained 144–184 game units apart. Packet publication stopped with the cart still moving, and the target aged to 4.1 seconds; this was not evidence that velocity correction alone fundamentally cannot help. With the attached-grid scan in build `537A2788...`, the same trial published 390 targets with zero stale-step skips by `authority-20260923-131503.json`; cart error hovered near 130 units. After disabling it in the *same* session, error grew to 148 then 182 units (`-131531.json`, `-131544.json`). This suggests phase-correct velocity authority helps but does not achieve parity. [CommonLib's `hkTransform`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkTransform.h), [`hkMatrix3`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/H/hkMatrix3.h), and [`bhkRigidBody` setters](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.h) support a next bounded, opt-in position-and-rotation trial at the same pre-step seam; validate body-to-reference separation and visual jumping, not just cross-PC position error.

- [Precision's pinned pre-physics hook](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ersh1/Precision/9ef45e0d8d9e3cf8b2df444f05142c1e7bf41cd4/src/Hooks.cpp) identifies AE relocation ID 77851 and the pre-step point at offset `+0x407`. The installed 1.7.104 Address Library maps that ID to RVA `0x104AC30`; disassembly shows `bhkWorld::Update` at that RVA and the referenced point at `0x104B037`. A transparent entry detour was built/deployed only for cadence/thread/order observation. In paired `authority-20260923-124712.json`, it ran 365/325 times, while selected cart body-to-scene callbacks ran 56/34 times on *different* threads, all outside the update, about 2.2 ms after its last end in the sampled call. This rejects the scene callback as a same-thread in-step authority surface.
- The installed `bhkWorld::Update` calls virtual slot `0x27` (`GetWorld1`) and places its return directly in `r13`; [CommonLib's pinned `bhkWorld`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/B/bhkWorld.h) names that method as returning `hkpWorld*`. The 1.7.104 disassembly then passes `r13` into a wrapper at RVA `0xB5B0B0` (Address Library ID 61410), which invokes its native physics step. An initial diagnostic incorrectly dereferenced that argument as a wrapper at `+0x10`, causing zero selected-body matches despite valid body cache refreshes; this was corrected to compare the direct `hkpWorld*`. Do not infer no body motion from that invalid sample.
- With the corrected read-only identity check in paired build `7BBE47E2...`, `authority-20260923-125837.json` measured 384/384 selected host steps and 249/249 selected follower steps changing cart `0x000B9DF3`'s Havok body. The last selected body step moved 6.37 game units on the host and 16.41 on the follower. The native step ran on the same thread as the enclosing world update; selected body-to-scene callbacks ran on a different thread, 2.32/4.81 ms after the last step end in that sample. The cart reference positions still differed by 235.75 game units. This is direct evidence of *independently divergent physics integration* before the native body-to-scene transfer, not merely a reference/mesh update bug. The selected probe was disabled on both PCs after capture. It does not yet identify every upstream force, authored constraint, or scene/Papyrus input, nor prove that direct body replacement at pre-step is safe. Future host-authoritative playback must validate world identity, body generation, source tick/epoch, angular state, constraint response, and the subsequent scene transfer without hitches.

- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `NiCollisionObject`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/N/NiCollisionObject.h) places `sceneObject` at +0x10; its [pinned `NiAVObject`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/N/NiAVObject.h) places `world` at +0x7C, `world.translate` at +0xA0, and `userData` at +0xF8. On the installed 1.7.104 executable, disassembly of RVA `0x2F4360` loads `r13=[collisionObject+0x10]` and calls native reference setter `0x2F0EF0` with `rdx=r13+0xA0`. Thus the position setter receives the scene node's world translation. This is a stronger causal interpretation than the previous phase trace: the reference position is copied **from the node** by the collision/scene synchronization path. It does not prove what drove that node's world transform or how to override Havok safely.
- Six host crash dumps from 07:44 through 12:00, including builds before this turn's selected setter trace, share `SkyrimTogether.exe+0x7237D2`, a null `rcx` read at `[rcx+8]`. The actual caller is `0x6AA288` (return address at crash stack +0x98, accounting for the callee prologue), which loads `rcx=[actor+0xF8]` without a null check. **Correction:** An initial inference called this `NiAVObject::userData`; that was wrong. The preceding instructions read position at `actor+0x54`, the project's live Actor layout places `currentProcess` at +0xF8, and [CommonLibSSE-NG names Address Library ID 39895 as `AIProcess::KnockExplosion(Actor*, const NiPoint3&, float)`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/A/AIProcess.cpp). The installed Address Library v5 maps `0x7237C0` to ID 39895 and `0x2F4360` to ID 19826. The game calls explosion knockback on an actor without an AI process; a null-process guard at that native entry is a candidate universal crash fix, but only a paired unattended run past the previously recurring failure window can validate it. CommonLib's generic Actor offset differs on this 1.7.104 build, so use the installed disassembly and this project's validated +0xF8 layout for the guard.
- Build `4F086225...` hooked the source-backed `AIProcess::KnockExplosion` ID 39895 and returns only when its process `this` is null; normal calls still forward to the original function. In the paired unattended campaign launched 12:09:18, the host and follower were responsive through MQ101 stage 75 at 12:15:06. The host's bridge counter reported **one actual null call skipped**; follower reported zero. This is direct in-game confirmation that the known null-process crash condition occurred and was guarded, beyond the previous 4m28s crash interval. The same run still failed cart/camera parity, and one successful campaign window is not proof that all crash paths or a full playthrough are fixed. Evidence: `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-121506.json`.
- Build `6278FD76...` added an optional selected-reference pointer-identity trace to the already installed native setter hook. In the paired 12:17:20 sample, both machines were actively updating cart `0x000B9DF3` (217 host and 165 follower setter calls). On each, the setter input pointer was exactly `&watchedNode->world.translate` and the caller was RVA `0x2F4754`; yet the cross-PC cart reference separation was 214.83 game units. This directly validates the disassembled node-to-reference copy path on both runtimes and locates the parity error *before* that copy. The selected trace was disabled after capture. Evidence: `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-121720.json`.
- Build `3D27BF89...` installed a transparent, selected-reference trace on collision-sync relocation ID 19826. In the paired 12:21:33 sample, this callback ran 59 times host and 35 times follower for the watched cart. The last sampled callback moved the reference 28.34/35.27 game units while node-world and Havok body positions did not change during that callback; afterward reference-to-node error was zero on both. The cart remained 155.73 units apart across PCs. This identifies ID 19826 as a **post-node copy/notification phase** for the watched cart, not the place where divergent node physics is generated. Evidence: `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-122133.json`.
- The next paired callsite trace (`authority-20260923-122431.json`) found the same caller RVA `0x106B531` on both PCs. Installed 1.7.104 disassembly shows function RVA `0x106B160` accepts a collision object and a `NiTransform*`, copies its translation and rotation into `NiAVObject::world` at +0x7C through +0xA8, then invokes the callback pointer that reaches collision-sync ID 19826. Address Library v5 maps this transform-entry function to ID 78382. Thus the runtime chain is **input transform → node world → reference position**, with a native callback at the middle-to-last edge. A selected, read-only hook on ID 78382 can determine whether that input matches the body and whether its caller is a stable authority seam; until paired evidence arrives, do not write through it.
- Build `0C0D9BB1...` added that default-off ID 78382 trace. At `authority-20260923-122845.json`, both PCs selected the same cart and saw 140/92 calls. In the last sampled call, the input transform was ~11.66 game units from the Havok body on both PCs (a consistent body-to-node pivot), but 24.97/39.06 units from the *previous* node world; after the call both node world and reference position matched the input translation exactly. The carts still differed by 137.76 units across PCs. Both sides reported caller RVA `0x105D086`. Installed disassembly at this caller reads a native body quaternion and position through virtual slots +0x1A0/+0x198, converts the body position by the game's 70:1 Havok-to-game scale, builds a `NiTransform`, then calls ID 78382. This establishes a concrete body → transform → node → reference chain. A host-authoritative patch must update body state and the corresponding transform coherently at a verified physics phase; node-only or reference-only writes cannot satisfy collision and passenger parity. The selected probe was disabled after capture.
# Follower render-pose trial (2026-09-23)

- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `NiAVObject` layout and virtuals](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/N/NiAVObject.h) distinguish local (+0x48), world (+0x7C), and previous-world (+0xB0) transforms and expose `UpdateDownwardPass`, `UpdateWorldData`, and `UpdateTransformAndBounds` as separate native stages. [Precision's published hook definitions](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ersh1/Precision/9ef45e0d8d9e3cf8b2df444f05142c1e7bf41cd4/src/Hooks.h) identify additional per-actor animation update seams. A post-graph local transform write cannot be assumed to reach the rendered world transform; the current implementation keeps this experiment opt-in until a safe, verified pre-render propagation phase is identified. No proprietary engine code was imported.
- In paired new-campaign capture `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-162636.json`, the follower-only opt-in regular-update presenter ran for about eight seconds and reported 13,479 applied frames / 1,048,554 bone writes, no graph rejections or write failures, and 12,833 skips of frames older than the 200 ms receipt limit. It was switched off immediately after capture. Of 121 matched sampled **render-local** bones across 11 actors, 112 were within the comparator's 0.001 tolerance, whereas zero full skeleton checksums matched; exact full checksums are therefore an overly strict proxy for the effect of this writer. Render-root separations for most actors were about 58–64 game units, with one 1,445-unit outlier, and the campaign parity gate still failed. This is evidence of an active local-pose path, **not** proof of visually smooth or 1:1 animation. The sample was 11 ms apart, but presentation delay and animation/render phase may differ. Native mount relation remained absent for the follower's rider despite a valid server rider-to-horse relation. Next work must measure host and follower at the same presentation tick, trace local-to-world propagation and mount interaction prerequisites, then test a bounded native-stage correction; do not enable the current writer by default.
- A root-relative world-space comparison was added to the paired diagnostic and rerun against that frozen pose capture (`authority-20260923-162935.json`). For the 11 overlapping actors, root-relative maximum sampled bone offsets were about 2.7–22.1 game units even when absolute bone error was 61–1,448 units. Therefore the bulk of the absolute separation is actor/root placement, though remaining relative errors show world-pose parity is incomplete. Do not infer a universal missing world-propagation call from absolute bone positions alone. The comparator change is diagnostic-only and requires no client redeploy.

## Native vehicle binding audit

- [The published vanilla `Actor.psc` declaration](https://github.com/Amelinyaa/SKSE/blob/master/Data/Scripts/Source/Actor.psc) defines `SetVehicle(ObjectReference)` for horses, carts, and other vehicles, including detach with `None`. It describes an engine-native vehicle relation, but does not establish whether it is the same relation returned by `Actor::GetMount`, how mounted animation is triggered, or the exact 1.7.104 implementation. The existing client records every native Papyrus registration in `PapyrusService`, so first expose the installed game's `Actor::SetVehicle` registration and module-relative function address in the read-only game snapshot. Inspect that installed binding before any follower-side call. Reject a blind package retry or direct memory write as a mount solution: the paired capture already showed valid network relation plus repeated package attempts but no native seat.
- The built read-only probe (`8F51389D...`) was deployed to both clients and a new paired campaign confirmed that the installed SkyrimSE.exe registers `Actor::SetVehicle` at RVA `0xA02D50`. Installed 1.7.104 disassembly at that RVA receives actor in `r8`, vehicle in `r9`, handles `None`/self as detach, resolves a vehicle handle, and calls native RVA `0x6ABC00`. That function stores the handle at actor `+0x1F0` and invokes further actor state/AI-process operations; it is **not** merely a coordinate attach. The disassembly at RVA `0x6E1BD0` explicitly branches on actor `currentProcess` at `+0xF8`, so a follower actor without normal AI processing may still not seat or animate after this call. This supports a single-selected-rider, opt-in game-thread trial with immediate native-mount and crash diagnostics, not default-on general use. The Papyrus wrapper ABI matches the client project's existing `PapyrusFunction` convention (VM, stack, actor, argument).
- **Correction after inspecting the branch:** the actor-vehicle path at `0xA02D50` calls `0x172010` on the rider's `ExtraDataList` (`actor+0x70`), not `0x6ABC00`. The latter is the non-actor vehicle branch and its `actor+0x1F0` field is irrelevant for a horse. `0x172010` creates/updates extra-data type `0x7B`, which [CommonLib identifies as `ExtraHorse`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/E/ExtraDataTypes.h); its [published layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/E/ExtraHorse.h) contains a horse handle at +0x10. Native `GetMount` (installed Address Library ID 38702 / RVA `0x6D32C0`) checks extra-data type `0xA9`, [named `ExtraInteraction`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/E/ExtraInteraction.h), then resolves a [RefrInteraction actor/target pair](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/R/RefrInteraction.h). Thus `SetVehicle` and mounted state are **different engine domains**. A one-rider trial called `SetVehicle` once with no immediate or subsequent `GetMount` success (`authority-20260923-163915.json`, `-164056.json`), so do not use it as the mount fix. Next inspect the host/follower `ExtraHorse` and `ExtraInteraction` records read-only, then identify a native operation that creates the correct interaction and animation state. No direct extra-data writes are justified.
- The paired fresh-intro snapshot `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/authority-20260923-165108.json` resolves that question. Both rider pairs had the same `ExtraHorse` handle on both PCs. The host had `ExtraInteraction` on the rider **and** horse and native `GetMount` succeeded; the follower had neither interaction record and native `GetMount` failed. A later capture (`authority-20260923-165334.json`) found the missing follower interaction on six host-interacting actors, so the parity comparator now explicitly fails on this mismatch. One follower rider briefly reported `wasSeated` but did not retain the native mount. The evidence rejects horse-handle copying, a successful package start, and the `SetVehicle` trial as sufficient fixes; it does not yet identify the creation path.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `ExtraDataList::Add`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/src/RE/E/ExtraDataList.cpp) is a thin relocation call, and its [declaration](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/E/ExtraDataList.h) accepts a `BSExtraData*`. Its [published `ExtraInteraction` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/E/ExtraInteraction.h) points to a ref-counted `RefrInteraction`. Installed 1.7.104 Address Library ID 12315 maps to native add RVA `0x17E0E0`; disassembly shows that operation locks the list and calls the underlying insertion routine. A bounded, transparent trace of only `ExtraInteraction` additions during a connected game should reveal the caller RVA and paired actor/target handles without editing the extra data. Reject default-on writes or re-creating these records by hand before locating the native mount transition and its cleanup/lifetime rules.
- Build `D582E360...` traced that add path during a new paired intro. **Both** clients created three pairs of `ExtraInteraction` records through caller RVA `0x173A77`, with identical actor/target handles; the follower did so around 17:01:58 and the host around 17:02:03. At 17:03:02 the follower no longer had either rider/horse interaction while the host did (`authority-20260923-170302.json`). This revises the diagnosis from "follower never creates interaction" to "follower loses it after creation." The installed binary disassembly identifies caller `0x173A77` as part of function RVA `0x1739A0`: with a non-null `RefrInteraction` smart pointer it allocates/adds `ExtraInteraction`; with a null one and an existing record it calls native removal RVA `0x17FA30`. A signature-guarded, read-only trace around `0x1739A0` can distinguish explicit native clearing from later actor destruction or extra-list replacement. The existing add hook should remain diagnostic only.
- Build `99E9487C...` traced the setter itself during another paired intro. The host recorded five target-link installs and three clears; the follower recorded three installs and **42 rider-side plus 45 partner-side clear calls**, including repeated null calls after the records were already absent. The true transitions (`before=true after=false`) came from native caller RVAs `0x78D676` and `0x73D2B5` on both PCs, but only the host re-established durable links. Disassembly shows `0x78D676` is inside a `RefrInteraction` cleanup path (RVA `0x78D610`) which resolves the paired actor and calls the `0x1739A0` null branch; `0x73D2B5` mirrors that clear on the other reference. [CommonLib's `RefrInteraction` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/R/RefrInteraction.h) confirms the actor/target handles and a `synced` flag; it does not expose the mount-specific transition implementation. Snapshot `authority-20260923-171009.json` still fails native mount and scene/pose parity. Do not suppress this cleanup blindly: the interaction object can be released, leaving dangling references. A bounded one-rider/one-horse native-process trial is warranted because the current client hook returns immediately for *all* remote actors; it can test whether the engine lifecycle needs continued remote actor processing without enabling follower AI globally.
- Build `BDC3CAE1...` added a default-off two-form native-process trial. On the follower it was armed for intro rider `0x000198BA` and horse `0x000A9DE7` before starting a fresh campaign; the cached snapshot reported **5,632 allowed native actor-process ticks**, yet the follower still cleared that pair's interactions at 17:13:13.873 and still had no native mount in paired captures `authority-20260923-171331.json`, `-171402.json`, and `-171453.json`. At `-171453.json`, host/follower native mount and actor-pose parity still failed. The trial was disabled in the running follower immediately afterward. This rejects "allow full remote Actor::Process for the pair" as a sufficient mount fix, and it is **not** a production policy because independent remote AI could diverge. The root mechanism may involve the mount interaction's queue/state-machine prerequisites or a scene phase offset; trace that transition rather than retaining a soon-to-be-freed interaction pointer.
- In the same run the host cart stopped at a **closed Helgen gate** and the follower reproduced that same closed-gate scene. Local and remote screenshots at about 17:21 show the obstruction; paired captures `authority-20260923-171800.json` and `-172032.json` held scene `0x000BECD4` at phase 20 on both PCs for more than two minutes. This is shared *stalled* state, not success: the carts still differed by about 136 and 30 game units, and the camera/pose/native mount gates remained red. [The author of "Helgen first gate intro Fix"](https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition/mods/126760) reports that vanilla opening depends on a trigger detecting Tullius's horse and changed it to Tullius while repositioning the volume. That is relevant prior art and a hypothesis for this run because mounted/horse state is already unstable; it does **not** prove that our particular trigger failed for that reason. Do not import the mod's protected assets or add a one-off forced gate open. Determine whether the source-side trigger sees the horse and whether its door reference state changes, then use the same host-authoritative trigger/door-event mechanism for doors game-wide.
- [SKSE's published vanilla `ObjectReference.psc`](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/ObjectReference.psc) documents `GetOpenState()` values 0–4. The client already wraps that native call; use it only for bounded read-only snapshots of nearby loaded door references. Scan the player cell for nearby door and activator references by base `FormType`, select nearest candidates rather than relying on the existing first-256 non-actor reference cap, and compare door open/disabled state with scene phase and horse/cart position. An activator's proximity alone does not prove it is the gate trigger; identify script/record linkage before implementing any generic trigger authority relay.
- The fresh read-only snapshot after adding that generic probe exposed an independent ownership split: on the leader PC, rider forms `0x000198BA` and `0x0002BFA2` were **locally owned**, but their horses `0x000A9DE7` and `0x000654E1` were **remotely owned**. The server's existing `OnMountRequest` transfers a mount to the rider owner only *after* a native mount event, while the client and server explicitly exclude mounts from leader ownership claims. This creates a circular prerequisite: native mount interaction requires one coherent local simulation, but the ownership co-location waits for the interaction. The project source itself is the directly inspected prior implementation; an additional search of public Skyrim Together source did not surface a safer mount-affinity policy. Adapt the existing leader-in-range, epoch-validated claim path to include mounts for the leader only, while retaining cell-lease handoff when the leader is absent. This is a game-wide entity-authority correction, not an MQ101 gate script patch. Validate owner alignment and mount/door outcomes separately.
- Paired build `6C3763E08E5998E4F65B08E12B3B4311D41D6FB43A98C341BC83B2CD9A14344E` added leader-only mount claims through the existing range/party/epoch validation. The new `mount-leader-affinity` protocol scenario passed: a follower in a separated cell leased a mount, the leader claimed it on entering range, and the follower's fresh-epoch reclaim was rejected. All protocol scenarios and TPTests (135 assertions) passed. The first fast campaign launch hit a transient game-window-thread timeout during startup; the second succeeded. On the host live snapshot, both tested rider/horse pairs were now local epoch 2, and both riders reported the expected `nativeMountFormId`, `horseExtra`, and `interactionExtra`. Unlike the preceding stalled run, paired captures `authority-20260923-173524.json` through `-173927.json` showed MQ101 advance from stage 15/scene phase 3 to stage 70, with both peers at the same stage. The host screenshot `feedback-20260923-173940-45232.bmp` shows the execution-area sequence, beyond the earlier blocked cart approach. This is evidence that the ownership correction removed the observed *host progression stall in this run*, not proof that the gate's exact reference open state was measured: the generic nearby-door probe did not identify a door at the later capture point, and the gate may use an activator/animated reference. The parity gate still failed because camera roots and loaded references differ, actor pose coverage is incomplete, and the follower lacks native mounted/interaction state despite receiving the server relation. Do not call this a 1:1 fix; next isolate follower-side native mount recreation and visual pose/root parity without regressing host progression.
- At 17:41–17:42, both screenshots showed character creation on the same default Nord, so the previous gate failure did not recur through that milestone. The host `feedback-20260923-174217-45232.bmp` and follower `feedback-20260923-174148-23876.bmp` nevertheless show different camera framing/background alignment while displaying the same menu. These are not frame-synchronized screenshots, so use the paired camera-root snapshot rather than pixel comparison to quantify this remaining presentation gap.
- [TiltedEvolution's original mount-control commit](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/commit/400c12e) and [mount-trigger commit](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/commit/6fe7d4a) confirm two different paths: local owner handoff for horses and a native mount-package call on the remote peer. The current branch still uses that native call, now deferred until both actors have 3D, but the follower only briefly seats some riders before native interaction cleanup and accumulates repeated rejected package attempts. Do not infer that replaying the mount event or setting `ExtraHorse` is enough. Add a bounded read-only trace of native package return, rider/mount AI-process presence, caller RVA, and remote status, plus a snapshot `hasAIProcess` flag, then compare the earliest successful and first rejected follower calls against the host before changing engine lifecycle behavior.
- The first mount-package hook trace did not capture the deferred follower call because `Actor::InitiateMountPackage` invokes the saved trampoline directly. The caller-site trace in paired build `4D543065ED6F6FC4A9CDEF7C1184238C5F40545F482757C2E4C48B9C946E656A` did: at 17:51:04 both follower rider/horse pairs had a non-null AI process and returned `started=true`, but immediate native mount and `ExtraInteraction` were absent. Five seconds later both returned false for six attempts, then sometimes true again, still without immediate mount or interaction. Thus a missing `currentProcess` pointer and a simply rejected first package are disproven; the async native completion/cleanup path is at issue. The trace's Windows thread IDs vary between callbacks; this alone does not prove unsafe concurrency or wrong-thread calls, because `World::Update` is invoked through Skyrim's VM update hook. Before changing scheduling or native package behavior, measure whether calls overlap and which native callback completes or cancels a mount request. Keep the trace bounded and read-only.
- The assignment-boundary trace in build `4DB403C6...` isolated a **specific universal lifecycle bug**. On the follower, mounted riders `0x2BFA2` and `0x198BA` retained native `GetMount` and `ExtraInteraction` through `ReconcileActorData`, then immediately lost both after the unconditional `MoveActor`/native `MoveTo`, even where the target differed by less than one game unit. [CommonLib's reference wrapper](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) confirms `MoveTo` is an engine-native relocation, and its [Actor implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp) confirms native `GetMount` depends on the interaction domain. The causal conclusion comes from our paired boundary trace, not those generic declarations. A first trial that skipped same-cell `MoveTo` for riders only preserved rider `0x2BFA2` through its assignment, but the later unconditional `MoveTo` of horse `0x654E1` cleared both links. A relationship-aware same-cell guard must protect **either participant**, while cross-cell relocation still needs an explicit group transaction rather than silently skipping necessary movement.
- Paired build `5BF4673AC25231C3B748B05CD0158124C6964789F64DCD8A5C760DE2F5CDFF86` guarded same-cell assignment `MoveTo` whenever the actor already has `ExtraInteraction`; ordinary host interpolation remains active. Follower assignment logs at 18:08:38 showed both tested rider/horse pairs retained interaction after reconciliation **and** after movement handling. Paired capture `authority-20260923-180911.json` at MQ101 stage15/phase3 had both rider pairs natively seated with matching server mount relation and no mount parity failure; `-181107.json` at stage25 retained those two pairs. The third relation, Elenwen `0x198BC` on horse `0x527F1`, still failed native seating: at 18:09:39.253 the follower engine created and cleared its interaction in the same thread before actor-assignment response, and later native package attempts returned false. This is a different lifecycle path; do not claim all mounted actors fixed. Camera and loaded-reference parity still fail. Unit suite: 147 assertions/13 cases; protocol scenarios including mount-leader-affinity passed. A follower screenshot at 18:09:21 showed the intro cart occupants/body meshes present, but not the third rider/horse relationship in view.

## Follower body-attachment probe (2026-09-23)

- **Correction:** paired artifact `authority-20260923-175508.json` appeared to show transient `0xFF000DB5` and `0xFF000DB3` with eight root 3D children on the host but four on the follower, and equal worn-armor counts. The comparator was pairing by **local form ID**, however, and temporary FF IDs are independently allocated per client. Those two rows cannot be treated as same-actor evidence until matched by server network ID. The comparator now pairs network entities by network ID and records both local form IDs. [CommonLibSSE-NG's `NiNode` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/N/NiNode.h) places child pointers in the root array at +0x110, and its [`NiObjectNET` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/N/NiObjectNET.h) places the node name at +0x10. A guarded, bounded read-only child-name/hidden/scale snapshot can identify missing geometry in fresh paired samples. `WaitingFor3D` currently clears on the first non-null root node, which may be premature, but a stronger gate needs valid same-actor evidence and whether children arrive later. The read-only probe is not a visual fix.
- Corrected same-network-ID capture `authority-20260923-180708.json` matched 39 unique server actors, including four temporary FF actors whose **local form IDs differed** across PCs. The previously suspicious FF actors actually had matching root child counts and worn counts (8/2 or 8/4) in that sample. Three static actors differed in root child count, but without child identity and a screenshot this is not proof of floating heads; rider/mount scene geometry can legitimately differ while native mount is broken. The comparator correction also affects pose/visual packet comparisons, previously keyed by form ID for FF actors. An attempted later capture hit a remote window-thread timeout during a coordinated game restart and produced no evidence.
- A read-only Reviewer A review of this checkout proposed an inventory/3D lifecycle hypothesis: `Actor::Create` marks a remote actor before native spawn, `EquipHook` suppresses un-overridden native equips, and `WaitingFor3D` calls `SetActorInventory` as soon as a root node exists. That setter removes and re-adds items using `Equip(..., abApplyNow=false)`; worn flags can be true without attached armor geometry. This is source-consistent, but the corrected FF capture does **not** currently corroborate missing geometry, and CommonLib's [`ActorEquipManager::EquipObject`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/ActorEquipManager.cpp) only forwards the `applyNow` flag—it does not document what the engine does with it. Do not switch the flag or trigger a 3D rebuild until same-actor child identities/timing prove the failure.
- Fresh paired capture `authority-20260923-181014.json` resolved the static root-count discrepancy too. Actor `0x000654FA` has eight present named root children on both PCs, but the follower's `NiTObjectArray::length` is 12 because four detached child slots are null. Across all matched actors with root-child data, the present **name multisets** matched even when raw array lengths did not. Therefore compare present children, not the array length; this capture does not reproduce a missing top-level body mesh. [CommonLib's `NiObject` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/N/NiObject.h) identifies virtual slot `0x03` as `AsNode`. Use a guarded read-only one-level nested child probe to check whether same-named armor/body nodes differ internally; cap arrays and names so snapshot cost remains bounded. A matching shallow tree still cannot prove skinned geometry, fade, texture, or final on-screen parity without paired screenshots.
- The first compact actor audit (`authority-20260923-181733.json`) falsely flagged four worn-armor mismatches because `GameTestService` only samples inventory and root child names for the first **48** enumerated network entities; unsampled actors were emitted with zero placeholders. The comparator now marks the per-PC sample window and withholds armor/geometry judgments when either actor was outside it. In `authority-20260923-181907.json`, 10 of 28 actor rows were unsampled, and only one interaction mismatch remained. A missing observation is not a matching mesh. An explicit per-entity sample-validity field from the client would be safer than this index inference if enumeration rules change.
- Build `5BF4673A...` added explicit visual/inventory sample flags and the one-level nested-node probe, plus native actor state/pose/render checksums. Paired `authority-20260923-182328.json` sampled 38 server-ID-matched actors at two synchronized host/follower points roughly 6.7 seconds apart (a requested 3-second delay plus remote-command/snapshot overhead). Two stationary NPCs, server IDs 21 (`0x000AAF97`) and 31 (`0x000AAF96`), had changing 99-bone native poses and 96-bone render checksums on the host but unchanged checksums on the follower, despite matching native state IDs of 0. This is a strong *specific* stagnant-animation signal, not proof of every on-screen pose or a general fix. Actor 31 also had a differing combat target: none on host versus server actor 36 on follower. A horse, server ID 28 (`0x000527F1`), lacked the host's root saddle/bridle node on the follower; the nested probe identified `ImperialBridle` and `ImperialSaddle` under that node. This is a real same-actor geometry mismatch and may explain some mount visuals, though not the reported floating NPC heads. The per-actor audit now separates root/nested geometry, native graph state, native/render pose progression, package, position, and normalized targets; graph-state ID differences alone remain diagnostic because time/phase can differ.
- With follower visual-pose apply enabled on a later run, paired `authority-20260923-182827.json` again found host-changing/follower-stagnant native **and render** pose for the same forms (`0x000AAF97` server ID 21 and `0x000AAF96` server ID 32). Their graph-post callbacks advanced on both machines, and the host visual packets reached the follower with matching source ticks/checksums at both sample points. The global mailbox showed tens of thousands of applied frames and zero write failures, but this does not establish these two holders were written. The user's live visual report says global apply caused NPCs to snap between host and local animations, so it was disabled. Code inspection of `VisualPoseMailbox::InspectPostGraph` found per-frame writes require both receipt age <=200 ms and a presentation-history bracket; a global applied count cannot distinguish per-holder missing bracket, stale receipt, slot collision, or later native overwrite. Add bounded per-holder read-only diagnostics for slot/frame identity, receipt age, history/bracket, last skip reason, eligible/written bones, and applied source tick before changing the writer's hold semantics. This is a diagnostic instrument, not a parity fix.

## Hitch-to-physics timeline probe (2026-09-23)

- Reviewed [Skyrim Together Reborn's open-source client](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/tree/dev/Code/client) and [CommonLibSSE-NG's SKSE API/event implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/SKSE/API.cpp) for existing game-loop and node-update seams. Neither source establishes that a world-update gap is a Havok catch-up step. This fork already hooks `GameVM::Update` and samples the two loaded cart references, so the smallest read-only instrument is at those known seams; no new engine hook or physics write was added.
- `authority-20260923-195429.json` measured follower maximum world-entry gap 3.844746 s (host 2.963984 s) and follower cart peak *short-frame* steps 202.05/201.30 game units versus host 17.82/10.54. The existing step sampler explicitly excludes intervals above 100 ms, so those peaks do **not** prove a single 3.84-second catch-up transform or temporal coincidence. Dynamic-body servo and selected body playback were both off. Added VM-entry and original-VM maximum timings plus a bounded 64-event hitch/motion trace with wall-clock tick, frame gap, per-cart sample interval, step, and position. This will distinguish a stall inside the mod/VM from one outside it and establish event ordering on the next paired capture. It is diagnostic only, not a cart-parity fix.
- A later capture from the same still-running pair, `authority-20260923-200510.json`, found cart reference gaps down to 6.79 and 5.33 game units while cumulative follower peak steps remained 202.05 and 201.30. Follower physics packets received rose to 5,112, but both pre-step and per-frame body playback remained disabled; receipt is not body application. This supports transient divergence and recovery, not a sustained 200-unit gap. The new binary (`7C8DC8B5...`) was staged and SHA-256 verified on the follower without interrupting either running game; it has not been installed or exercised yet.

## Dynamic-body stream change detection (2026-09-23)

- [SSE Display Tweaks' Havok source](https://github.com/SlavicPotato/SSEDisplayTweaks/blob/master/SSETweaks/havok.cpp) reads Skyrim's `fMaxTime`, `fMaxTimeComplex`, and physics step limits, and can adjust maximum physics time from the engine frame timer. Its [published configuration](https://github.com/SlavicPotato/SSEDisplayTweaks/blob/master/SSETweaks/SSEDisplayTweaks.ini) warns that frame times below `fMaxTime` can cause physics issues. Neither PC has that plugin installed. The host had `iVSyncPresentInterval=0` and the follower `=1`, so a same-setting timing trial was warranted, but this source does not imply that VSync alone creates host-authoritative physics.
- On build `8F62319A...`, both PCs were restarted into a fresh shared campaign with host VSync temporarily set to 1 (matching the follower); the host preference was backed up and restored to 0 after the trial. The lightweight 20-sample cart trace still recorded follower `0xBB970` peak short-frame step 115.71 game units (three large steps), versus host peak 7.82, and steady aligned cart errors around 53–68 units on both carts after initial loading. Physics packets rose host 102→756 and follower 118→773, with no body-only host updates during the sampled interval. This does not show cart parity or eliminate the observed short-frame jumps; differing VSync is not a sufficient explanation. Do not adopt a global VSync preference change as the multiplayer fix. Both games were stopped after the trial; no active game settings remain changed.
- Repeated the fresh campaign after byte-for-byte restoring the host's original preferences (`SHA-256 A63D3642...`). The follower recorded a 2.899627-second world-entry gap at shared tick 186174158, with only 6 microseconds of preceding `GameTestService` work. Cart `0xBB970` then moved 116.10 and 100.38 game units on two 16-ms frames at ticks 186174990 and 186175007; the host had a 3.067221-second load gap but peak cart step 7.82 for this cart. The follower was still around 58–67 units from the host over a subsequent 20-sample interval. This places those two jumps **after** the loading gap, not in one large elapsed-time integration call, and again rejects the deep diagnostic as their immediate cause. A scene/constraint reinitialization remains possible; no specific native force has yet been identified. The existing bounded event already captures process time and cart position, but `hitch_snapshot` omitted them, so the light bridge now exposes those existing fields for the next paired run without a deep actor audit.
- Build `42D16581...` deployed to both PCs and tested in a third fresh campaign. The light bridge returned its new position/timing fields correctly. The follower's 2.961601-second gap was at shared tick 186395740; at tick 186396133, roughly 393 ms later, cart `0xBB970` stepped 117.71 units during a 16-ms world frame with 2-us prior `GameTestService` work. Two subsequent 100.39/77.25-unit moves were also recorded on short frames, whereas this host run's peak for that cart was 7.86 units. The aligned cross-PC error at the sample was 63.40 units. This repeat argues against VSync mismatch and against the cached diagnostic causing the jump; it does **not** establish whether the source is a local cart script, a Havok constraint/impulse, or a scene initialization write. The 166-assertion/16-case unit suite passed before deployment. No physics gameplay logic changed in this build.
- Following the already-validated [Precision pre-physics seam](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) and the existing selected-body native-step probe (see earlier cart-body findings), added only a peak/count accumulator for one opt-in Havok body. Build `D3D9E55D...` was deployed to both PCs, and the selected read-only probe was enabled for cart `0xBB970` before a fourth fresh campaign. Follower peak native body displacement was 50.927 game units per step; its peak reference displacement was 50.924, while the host native body peak was 7.881. The follower did not repeat a >75-unit jump in this run, so the peak cannot be used to explain the earlier 117-unit event. It does confirm substantial movement discrepancy already inside the selected native physics step, before reference/scene presentation. The body probe was disabled on both PCs after measurement; no corrective body writes were enabled. Tests again passed 166 assertions/16 cases. The next high-value observation is time-aligned host/follower input/constraint state at the beginning of the selected native step, not another reference teleport.
- For the next read-only probe, [Precision's published API](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) confirms the meaningful before/after boundary is `hkpWorld::stepDeltaTime`, and [CommonLibSSE-NG's `bhkRigidBody` header](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.h) distinguishes velocity and pose access. This fork's installed-runtime `RigidBody` view already verifies the transform, motion-type, linear-velocity, and angular-velocity offsets. Capture those values only when a selected body's native-step displacement sets a new peak; compare local millisecond time to the lightweight cart-reference events. Pre-step high velocity would implicate upstream motion inputs, while a post-step-only spike or large displacement with modest velocity would suggest impulse/constraint resolution. These are hypotheses to test, not conclusions or playback changes.
- Build `5F4512C5...` deployed to both PCs with that opt-in peak record and tested in a fifth fresh campaign. The follower cart `0xBB970` native-body peak was **117.715** game units in a `0.016667`-second step at local time 53058500 ms; its velocity was already **58.401 Havok units/s before** the step and **104.4 after**, motion type 3 (dynamic). The reference's 117.698-unit short-frame jump was observed at local time 53058515 ms, about 15 ms later. The host's selected-body peak was 7.890 units in the same nominal step duration, with pre/post speeds 4.769/8.626. This is strong evidence that an excessive follower body velocity/step precedes the visible snap and is not caused by a large simulation delta or a render-only transform copy. It does not yet identify whether the source of that velocity is cart script input, collision impulse, constraint solver, or another game-native writer. The preceding follower load gap ended about 399 ms before the reference event; the immediately preceding game-test work was 3 microseconds. Build/tests passed 166 assertions/16 cases. Do not infer a game-wide physics fix from this one cart.
- A sixth fresh trial enabled the existing selected pre-step *velocity* controller for follower cart `0xBB970` before campaign start. The follower still peaked at 110.745 game units (host 9.116), with native pre/post speeds 58.959/104.400 Havok units/s; late snapshot counters confirmed 5,547 applied velocity corrections, 1,100 published selected targets, and 622 stale skips. Those cumulative counts do not establish whether a correction was applied on the initial burst, so build `AECD0D4A...` captured that condition at the exact peak. The seventh fresh trial found a **target was applied** at the 110.862-unit burst with source age 166 ms. Thus an absent target is not a sufficient explanation. Source tick, authority, and exact write readback still needed checking.
- Build `97E73D86...` then sampled the selected body **after the velocity setter and before the original native step**, still in opt-in mode. On a fresh campaign's 110.667-unit follower peak, the host target was applied at age **18 ms** and the body's immediate velocity readback was **25 Havok units/s**, matching the controller's speed cap. The native `0.016667`-second step ended at **104.4 Havok units/s** and the visible reference stepped 110.658 units on the next frame; host peak was 7.890. This rules out a failed setter, stale/missing packet, large frame delta, or presentation-only copy as the cause of this event. It identifies a large velocity/position change generated *within the follower's native step* despite an accepted pre-step correction; the specific contact, constraint, tether, or other solver input remains unproven. The velocity controller and selected phase probes were disabled on both PCs after the test. Tests passed 166 assertions/16 cases before deployment. Do not make velocity-only control the default.
- [SKSE's published vanilla `ObjectReference.psc`](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/ObjectReference.psc) declares `TetherToHorse` as a distinct native cart operation. A secondary mirror of Creation Kit documentation describes a horse-to-chassis constraint and cart tip-recovery impulse, but those mechanics have not yet been verified against this runtime. This makes the cart/horse physical island and tether lifecycle a concrete hypothesis to inspect next, not a basis to call every high-speed step a tether bug. A universal solution must identify and synchronize the authoritative physical island/constraint inputs for moving objects, not hardcode these two MQ101 cart IDs as gameplay exceptions.
- The source tree itself registers runtime RTTI for `bhkCartTether` (Address Library ID 393247), and [libSkyrim's earlier public RTTI catalog](https://github.com/himika/libSkyrim/blob/master/Skyrim/include/Skyrim/RTTI.h) independently names that class. This gives a concrete native class to inspect rather than guessing from a cart script. It does **not** yet prove the selected cart's loaded collision object is of that type, that its tether instance is present on both PCs, or that it caused the measured 25→104.4 velocity jump. Verify those identities and the horse/chassis constraint relationship read-only before altering tether state.
- [libSKSE's NiRTTI declaration](https://github.com/himika/libSKSE/blob/master/skse/skse/NiRTTI.h) shows the native RTTI begins with name and parent pointers, and [CommonLibSSE-NG's `NiTimeController::GetRTTI`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/N/NiTimeController.cpp) demonstrates the virtual RTTI path. This fork's `NiObject` already declares `GetRTTI()`. Add only a bounded, guarded type-name observation to the on-demand two-cart snapshot; do not dereference unknown tether internals or change constraints until the loaded types are confirmed on the installed executable.
- Paired build `7BD711BA...` exercised that RTTI observation in a fresh shared campaign. Both cart root collision objects were readable as **`bhkCollisionObject`**, parent `bhkNiCollisionObject`, on both PCs; flags were 173 and native bodies dynamic. Thus `bhkCartTether` is **not** the loaded cart root collision-object type in this run. This does not rule out a separate tether object or child/horse relationship. [Mutagen's generated placed-NPC keys](https://github.com/Mutagen-Modding/Mutagen.Bethesda.FormKeys/blob/release/Mutagen.Bethesda.FormKeys.SkyrimLE/Skyrim/PlacedNpc.cs) identify cart1's horse as `0xB9DF2` and cart2's as `0xBB971`. An on-demand same-PC snapshot found the expected horse actors and matching host/follower package IDs, but those snapshots were seconds apart and are not a synchronized tether-distance comparison. Extend the lightweight shared-tick history to include the paired horse positions before judging a constraint mismatch.

- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `bhkRigidBody` interface](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/B/bhkRigidBody.h) exposes body transform and velocity independently of `TESObjectREFR` state; [Precision's pre-physics interface](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/PrecisionAPI.h) likewise treats the Havok body as the physics update object. In our source, the host's 50-ms physics stream previously decided to send from `TESObjectREFR::position`/Euler changes alone, even though it serialized `body.State.transform` and linear velocity afterward. A moving or rotating body whose reference had not yet updated could therefore be dropped before serialization.
- The host now compares native body translation, orientation basis, and velocity against the **last transmitted** body state; kind transitions from passive to dynamic or back also trigger an update. Bounded thresholds are 0.015 Havok units of translation, 0.0004 squared basis error, or 0.15 Havok units/s velocity delta. A five-assertion unit case verifies body-only translation, rotation, and velocity changes; the full test suite passed 166 assertions/16 cases. This improves packet completeness, **not** follower physical authority: dynamic-body playback remains default-off after failed cart trials. The revised build still needs paired deployment and packet-rate/performance measurements before calling it a live improvement.
- The user confirmed both games were idle and authorized restart. Build `2822F2B9...` was deployed and a fresh campaign launched. `authority-20260923-201801.json` measured a 1.0373-second `GameTestService` snapshot on the host followed by a 1.0441-second VM/world-entry gap; this is direct evidence that our deep diagnostic can itself hitch the game. A profiled repeat in build `89C6D530...` (`authority-20260923-202148.json`) attributed 1.282 seconds host / 1.930 seconds follower to the network-actor audit, with far smaller actor-pose, reference/scene, cart, nearby-actor, and quest sections. The follower also recorded 100+ game-unit short-frame cart steps, but the causal relation of those steps to a specific deep snapshot still needs an aligned lightweight run.
- To avoid self-induced stalls during hitch investigation, added a cached `hitch_snapshot` bridge response refreshed every 100 ms on the game thread and read from the window thread. It contains frame-gap maxima, host/follower physics packet counts, cart motion stats, and the bounded hitch/motion events keyed by shared world tick; it does **not** walk native graph/bone state. `Capture-TwoPcHitch.ps1` reads both caches without scheduling a deep scan. This is a diagnostic separation, not a physics fix; its measured per-frame cost and paired output require deployment validation.

## Diagnostic hitch isolation (2026-09-23)

- During the paired intro trial, a requested full `game_snapshot` consumed 2.010 seconds on the host and 2.302 seconds on the follower inside `GameTestService`, and the follower's next short frame showed ~363 game-unit cart steps. The cached profile attributed 1.684 seconds of the host scan to `networkActors` and 154 ms to `nearbyActors`. This directly implicates the deep diagnostic in one major hitch, but does not establish that every hitch or cart jump shares that cause.
- The full audit also ran implicitly once per launch because `OnGameThread` skipped its request gate when `m_gameSnapshotTimeMs` was zero. It now requires an explicit `request_game_snapshot` even at startup. The cheap `hitch_snapshot` remains available without triggering native graph/bone scans. After build `1C90C88F...` was deployed to both PCs and a fresh campaign started, five cached paired samples showed host/follower maximum GameTest durations of 954/322 microseconds, rather than the previous ~2-second scans. This validates removal of the automatic deep audit; it does **not** validate hitch-free play. The host recorded a ~20.9-second load/entry gap and follower a ~2.93-second gap with negligible prior GameTest work. The follower's second cart still showed three short-frame large steps, peak 116 game units, while the two cart-to-horse distances at aligned ticks were roughly similar; neither cart parity nor loading parity is fixed.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently found a disabled-playback callback that still read remote visual bones. `VisualPoseMailbox::InspectPostGraph` now returns before the graph lock and per-bone reads unless a selected pose-apply or selected root diagnostic is active. This is a source-verified hot-path correction; paired performance impact remains to be measured after deployment.
- Reviewer B and Reviewer A independently inspected the recurring 50-ms paths. `CharacterService::RunLocalUpdates` can serialize up to four evaluated poses per update, with up to ~99 per-node same-process `ReadProcessMemory` calls each under a graph lock; `ObjectService` performs a recurring host physics scan. Reviewer B observed host `lastScanUs` ranging 5.1–31.6 ms in 12 lightweight samples. These are plausible contributors to smaller stalls, not a proven attribution; phase-specific lightweight timings and paired A/B tests are needed before changing capture or discovery behavior.

## Humanoid look-at graph inputs (2026-09-23)

- [SKSE's published vanilla `Actor.psc`](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/Actor.psc) exposes `SetLookAt` and `SetHeadTracking` as distinct actor operations. This supports treating a native target/AI head-tracking path separately from animation-graph variables; graph variables alone cannot guarantee identical gaze targets.
- The project's humanoid `Master_Behavior` descriptor already sent `kbHeadTracking` and `kbHeadTrackSpine`, but not four other head-tracking booleans (`kbHeadTrackingOn`, `kbCanHeadTrack`, `kbDisableHeadTrack`, `kbNoHeadTrack`) or four look-at gain floats. Because `TESObjectREFR::SaveAnimationVariables` serializes *only* indices in this descriptor, that omitted input state could make host and follower evaluate different head/neck poses even when action events match. Added these eight entries while respecting the protocol's hard 64-boolean limit; a focused descriptor test passes 12 assertions. This is a transport correction, **not** proof that visible gaze now matches: target identity, graph phase, rest skeleton, and final render-node writes still require paired evidence.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's `Actor` implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp) demonstrates graph-variable reads for aim, while this fork's `AnimationGraphUpdateTrace` and `VisualPoseMailbox` sample the active graph and visual nodes at separate phases. Reviewer B and Reviewer A were asked for a read-only review; both suggested distinguishing behavior-pose, render-local, and render-world phase checksums before default-enabling bone writes. the implementer additionally hypothesized skeleton/rest-pose or 3D-generation mismatch for persistent floating heads, but did not inspect source or a paired capture. Treat those as diagnostic hypotheses, **not** engine facts or verified cause.

## Per-actor pose authority and visual snap trial (2026-09-23)

- The network-entity snapshot now records every registered actor's cell, position, package, combat target, latest action/event target, native graph state and evaluated-pose checksum, render-local and render-world bone checksums, plus bounded root/nested geometry. A separate capped scan inventories all loaded actors within 8000 units of the player's current cell, including actors absent from the network registry. The paired comparator matches registered actors by server ID, excludes unsampled geometry, and compares two captures to distinguish a static follower pose from a static host. In `authority-20260923-183428.json`, 16/16 nearby loaded actors were registered on both PCs; this is coverage for that cell and moment, not the entire game.
- With global `VisualPoseMailbox` apply enabled, the player observed alternating host/local NPC poses and hitches. `authority-20260923-183640.json` measured 339,383 applied frames and 26.83 million bone writes over 8,656 world calls; follower dispatcher cumulative time was about 29.98 s versus 13.66 s on host. A two-capture audit at `authority-20260923-182827.json` showed two 99-bone actors receiving matching host pose packets while their follower native and rendered checksums remained unchanged, despite graph-post callbacks advancing. This falsifies an explanation based solely on absent graph callbacks.
- The presenter formerly required a packet receipt age <=200 ms and newest pose within 100 ms of presentation; the producer serializes at most four actors per 50 ms. Reviewer B and Reviewer A independently pointed out that with dozens of actors this creates a deterministic host/local duty cycle. A bounded hold-latest trial raised the age window to 1500 ms and removed the 100 ms newest cap. In `authority-20260923-183520.json`, two previously frozen actors (`0xAAF97`, `0xAAF96`) gained changing follower render-local and render-world checksums, with per-holder mailbox writes confirmed. Their native pose remained static, other actors still diverged, and the player still reported visible snapping and hitches. Global apply was disabled and verified off in `authority-20260923-183640.json`. The hold change alone is **not** a parity fix and is not enabled by default.
- Per-holder mailbox diagnostics now expose slot/frame identity, receipt age, history bracket, last skip reason, eligible/written bones and applied source tick. A fresh selected-actor experiment should use one final bone writer, trace graph update versus world-transform update phase, and avoid all-actor game-loop writes. [CommonLibSSE-NG graph holder implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.cpp) distinguishes graph updates from [NiAVObject world updates](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/N/NiAVObject.cpp); the source does not prove their runtime ordering on this build. The horse `0x527F1` missing saddle/bridle children remains a separate 3D attachment fault, not something bone pose playback can repair.
- A follow-up selected-actor-only writer build `A4B9D1D1...` removed the regular game-loop bone write, required an explicit form ID, and limited the post-graph writer to one actor. `authority-20260923-184409.json` showed exactly one selected form (`0xAAF97`) with 425 apply visits / 40,800 bone writes, not the earlier all-actor workload. The user then proposed the more causal direction: compare how AI targets, packages, scene/dialogue decisions, graph inputs, and animation events are chosen before matching bones. Playback was disabled. Reviewer B and Reviewer A found that the four-frame visual history spans only about 150 ms at 20 Hz, while default presentation delay is 300 ms; a valid delayed interpolation bracket often cannot exist. A future pose fallback requires at least eight samples and a collision-free selected history, but is not the primary diagnostic direction now.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG's pinned `AIProcess` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/include/RE/A/AIProcess.h) identifies follow and target handles at +0x110/+0x114, and [its implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/A/AIProcess.cpp) exposes `GetHeadtrackTarget` through AE ID 39484. The decision audit added guarded reads of the two raw handles, dialogue/combat/action targets, package/process level, and processed/replayed event IDs/ticks. However, calling ID 39484 during this game's loading caused an access violation at `SkyrimTogether.exe+0x6FF05A` in the host on 2026-09-23 18:49:44, with dump `SkyrimTogether.exe.60788.dmp`. This may be an ABI/receiver-state mismatch; it is **not** a valid head-tracking observation. The call was removed, and head-track fields are explicitly marked unreadable until a runtime-safe path is established. No native target writes were attempted.

## Temporary-NPC identity and native mount parity (2026-09-23)

- Headless character creation uses Skyrim's existing UI transition, not a raw player-name write. [CommonLibSSE-NG exposes `RaceSexMenu::ChangeName`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/R/RaceSexMenu.cpp), but that alone does not prove the confirmation, name-entry, or MQ101 continuation callbacks ran. In the live host session, `R` opened the native confirmation, a mouse click on OK opened the native name field, typing `Host` displayed it, and Enter closed RaceSex Menu and advanced the scene; this is one host runtime observation, not a party-wide creator queue. [SKSE's input source](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/skse64/GameInput.h) distinguishes button and character events, consistent with testing name-entry separately from menu button selection. The bridge's helper lookup previously used `GetModuleFileNameW(nullptr)` as if it returned the launcher directory, but this fork's `TP_GetModuleFileNameW` spoofs that call to Skyrim's root. The corrected bridge looks under `Data/SkyrimTogetherReborn` and validates the visible native menu before invoking a same-desktop helper. Follower and multi-player validation remain pending.

- The reported duplicates are **NPCs, not player characters**. Source inspection of this fork shows static actor references are deduplicated by server `FormIdComponent`, but temporary `0xFF` references are custom assignments: the server skips that dedupe and a receiving client creates a native actor for each distinct server ID. Two peers independently creating the same scripted scene role can therefore produce two network identities, or one networked replica can coexist with a local unregistered actor. This is a hypothesis for the observed duplicates, not yet a paired classification. Do not merge by base NPC ID or proximity: legitimate actors can share both. The next low-cost provenance is authenticated source player ID, source local FF reference ID, base, cell/initial position, server ID, and spawn/remove time. The existing capped nearby-actor audit can classify a selected duplicate, but its full game-thread snapshot is too expensive to poll during play. [Skyrim Together Reborn's open character synchronization source](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/server/Services/CharacterService.cpp) is the upstream comparison for this fork's assignment path; [CommonLibSSE-NG's reference implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) distinguishes the live reference from its base object.
- For the Helgen rider (`0x198BC` in earlier paired captures), host `HorseEnterInstant` was delivered and replayed on the follower, yet its native mount/interaction remained absent. The current code already polls owner native mount every 200 ms, stores the server's desired rider-to-horse relation, and checks pending follower mounts about every 100 ms. `InitiateMountPackage` returning success only queues native work; it is not a seated-state acknowledgment. The local mount-package hook reports package start before native seating; if seating never commits, the server relation may remain set without a native mount because the owner's poll has not recorded a nonzero native mount to later clear. A 300-ms native-zero dismount is possible only after the poll first observed a nonzero mount. A lightweight catch should compare accepted server relation, host-native relation, follower-native relation and `ExtraInteraction` only for known riders, keyed by server identity/ownership epoch, and record divergence transitions before applying any correction. Repeated full-skeleton checks or blind remote-AI enabling are not warranted. [CommonLibSSE-NG's Actor mount accessors](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp) inform native-state inspection, but this executable's hook/ABI must still be validated at runtime.

## Animation-decision audit, not bone matching (2026-09-23)

- Combat target is one low-bandwidth decision absent from the active synchronization path: `CombatService::RunTargetUpdates` is under `#if 0`, while the installed client exposes `CombatController::targetHandle` and a native `SetTarget` wrapper. A new owner-validated `ReferenceUpdate::CombatTargetServerId` carries zero for no target, a server actor ID for a resolved target, and `UINT32_MAX` for an unresolved host handle. The server rejects IDs that do not name a registered character; the follower maps a server ID to its own actor handle before applying it. This is an engine-level target choice, not a bone pose write. The first paired trial (`authority-20260923-193718.json`) proved the positive target ID reached the follower, but exposed a timing bug: the newest packet source tick was always ahead of the 300-ms delayed presentation tick, so the target was never applied (`lastApplyTick=0`). The correction stores target *changes* in a bounded timestamped queue and promotes each at the presentation tick. Encoding tests passed 161 assertions/15 cases; protocol scenarios passed. A fresh paired positive-target observation is still required before claiming target parity.

- The new `movementGraphInputs` snapshot and paired received-vs-native audit compare selected packed movement variables against the follower's live graph values. In `authority-20260923-191445.json`, all selected received values matched follower native values for sampled moving NPCs, even when a same-wall-clock host sample differed in speed and turn variables. The movement point was sampled at tick 180522696 against presentation tick 180522727; the host's later live value is not an equivalent-time comparison. Do not classify those differences as transport loss without matching source ticks and graph phases. [CommonLibSSE-NG's graph-holder implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/b93280e832f263dbef44e44cbe2936622a02f91a/src/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.cpp) distinguishes graph variable access from the animation graph update call; it does not promise simultaneous host/follower evaluation.
- The initial `authority-20260923-191445.json` capture showed rider `0x198BC` (server ID 34) with host `HorseEnterInstant`, no follower `LatestAnimation`, no native mount, and 76 rejected pending mount attempts. The absence of follower `LatestAnimation` **did not prove a missing replay**: that field is populated by the native local-action hook, which suppresses remote actors. A new stage-by-stage diagnostic in `authority-20260923-192300.json` showed the host **sent** `HorseEnterInstant` targeting horse server ID 28, the follower **received and replayed** it at the same source tick, and native `ForceAction` returned success; the rider still lacked a native mount and interaction. The first divergence is therefore after action replay, in native mount/package/interaction commitment, not transport. A selected remote-process trial through `authority-20260923-192425.json` did not seat the rider; it was disabled. A subsequent Papyrus vehicle trial missed the rider after it left the tracked scene, so it is inconclusive and was disabled. Different dialogue targets for other NPCs require separate scene/AI decision provenance. [Skyrim Together Reborn's public animation system](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/client/Systems/AnimationSystem.cpp) is the open-source baseline for the action-event pipeline in this fork; event replay alone does not establish native package or mount parity.
- Reviewer A CLI and Reviewer B independently reviewed cheap decision sync. Direct source checks confirmed `AI_SYNC` is `0` in `Code/client/Games/References.cpp`, so native package capture is compiled out; the existing package request lacks target and owner/epoch validation, and the combat target update body in `Code/client/Services/Generic/CombatService.cpp` is disabled. Existing movement/action relay and mount relation protocols are live, so adding duplicate relays would not address the measured mount failure. Inspect [CommonLibSSE-NG's Actor mount/interaction accessors](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp) for native state semantics, but verify every hook ABI on the installed executable before enabling package control. Reviewer B's CLI sandbox limited its direct source access; the cited findings were checked in this worktree.

- The paired audit now records graph variable raw values (up to the first 256 of at most 512) under the active graph manager lock, with total count/checksum, and names known `Master_Behavior` (301 entries) and `HorseRootBehavior` (76 entries) indices from this repo's descriptor enums. It also compares AI follow/target handles, combat/dialogue targets, running package/process level, latest local animation action, and follower replayed action. These are separate decision layers: matching an event does not imply matching its target, graph inputs, native evaluated state, or final render pose. The `nearbyActorAudit` scan still has a current-cell/8000-unit/256-actor cap; it does not claim to read every actor in the whole game.
- In `authority-20260923-185645.json`, most 301-variable humanoid graphs differed only at index 53 (`kTimeDelta`, expected to vary slightly by machine). Two moving humanoids (`0x2BFA2`, `0x198BA`) additionally differed in speed/turn inputs; `kTurnDeltaDamped` (index 13) and `kturnSpeedMult` (155) were **not** in the synchronized humanoid descriptor and sometimes read zero on the follower. Horse graphs differed in speed and turn inputs even though the horse descriptor already includes those fields, indicating a separate phase/physical-motion problem rather than a missing index. One NPC had the same AI package and follower-replayed `IdleCartPrisonerDSway` but different resolved dialogue targets (`network:10` versus `network:3`) in `authority-20260923-185300.json`.
- Added the two missing humanoid turn floats to the descriptor. The paired `authority-20260923-185959.json` capture reduced `0x2BFA2`'s graph-input differences from six to three; its previously zero follower `kTurnDeltaDamped` became a small nonzero value, and `kturnSpeedMult` matched. This is evidence of a specific input transport correction, **not** scene/pose parity. Later `authority-20260923-190124.json` exposed other missing choice inputs: `kLookAtOutOfRange` (index 178) for a rider, `kweaponSpeedMult` (47) for several NPCs, body morph weight/muscular (127/184) for two NPCs, and mounted-weapon boolean/int (221/229) for rider `0x198BC`. That rider still lacked a native mount and targeted horse `network:28` on the follower while host AI target was empty; its graph differences are downstream of the unresolved mount lifecycle.
- The `AnimationVariables` wire format bit-packs a VarInt-sized boolean vector, not a fixed uint64. The descriptor's 64-boolean static limit was therefore increased to 128 and a 66-bit differential roundtrip test added. Added only observed missing choice inputs: look-at-out-of-range and mounted-weapon booleans, mounted-weapon integer, weapon-speed and two body-morph floats. Tests passed 160 assertions/15 cases; protocol scenarios passed. In paired `authority-20260923-190624.json` and `-190640.json`, the two body-morph NPCs and `0xAAF97` had either no graph-input difference or only machine-local `kTimeDelta`; `authority-20260923-190701.json` still showed six locomotion differences for unseated rider `0x198BC`. This improves source decision alignment but does not fix that native mount, cart physics, dialogue target, or camera/world transforms. The visual bone writer remains disabled by default and was off in all of these decision-audit captures.
- The current client source order is `CharacterService::RunRemoteUpdates` → `InterpolationSystem::Update` (loads latest/discrete movement graph variables) → `AnimationSystem::Update` (for queued actions, loads historical action variables then calls `ForceAction`). Native graph evaluation later computes additional values; this sequence can overwrite newer movement variables with an older action snapshot before native animation. A phase-specific read-only trace is needed before changing that order. The user’s pivot toward host-authoritative *decisions* rather than repeated bone writes is consistent with these measured gaps; no one-step target-write patch is justified yet.
# 2026-09-24 shared gameplay handoff

- [SKSE messaging API](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/skse64/PluginAPI.h) distinguishes `NewGame`, `PostLoadGame`, and `SaveGame` events. Adopt the lesson that entering a new game, finishing load, and having a durable checkpoint are separate boundaries; do not infer a post-dragon checkpoint from `TESLoadGameEvent` or copy the host's `.ess` to followers. The current gameplay barrier still needs a paired native save test before it can be called a checkpoint system.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `BGSSaveLoadManager`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/B/BGSSaveLoadManager.cpp) exposes the native singleton and `Save_Impl` entry points. A paired test of an explicitly requested mid-cinematic save on 2026-09-24 hung both game window threads and left `SSMP_Cinematic_Host_E1_T206205195.ess.tmp` at zero bytes. The direct save bridge path was disabled; this does not prove whether the cause is the cinematic state, the call context, or a native save guard. Test Skyrim's normal F5 path only after the confirmed stage-160 gameplay handoff, and require files plus reload before claiming a checkpoint.
