- [CommonLibSSE-NG `MessageBoxMenu::SelectOption`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/M/MessageBoxMenu.cpp) describes the native queue/callback path. At MQ101 stage 75 on both PCs, the test helper opened “Finish and name your character?” but Enter, Left+Enter, Space and click left it open, despite DInput key counters increasing and native polling active. Reviewer A noted the counter increments before hook suppression and that `SetCursorPos` does not move Skyrim's DInput cursor; Reviewer B noted helper exit 0 only means input sent. We therefore added an opt-in test-only native selection guarded by the exact visible English dialog and a single queue entry, using Address Library IDs 406362, 52284, 400445, 13631. The exact 1.7.104 Address Library maps 52284 and 13631 to `.pdata` function starts. This still requires a paired native test; do not generalize to arbitrary message boxes or ship as a production UI path until the callback ABI and lifecycle are confirmed in-game.
- Paired native test with client SHA-256 `6D875220684F24E47A7720E90C65A41A9F187ED643C41C495C7CB6DB9859A717`: both clients reached MQ101 stage 75, `race_menu_key done` opened the confirmation, `confirm_character_native` returned `nativeSelected=true` on each, and subsequent native state showed MessageBoxMenu closed while RaceSex remained open for name entry. The existing test helper typed `Host` and `Follower 1` and accepted the name; both RaceSex menus closed and each player-name readback matched. This confirms the narrow creator confirmation/typing path, not a full character customization queue or post-dragon playability. An earlier retry crashed on the host at MQ101 stage 70 before this command was invoked: dump `SkyrimTogether.exe.47920.dmp` reported a null `this` read at executable RVA `0x11CC0B2` (inside a 66-byte native function); causal attribution to any mod hook is not established.
- The installed `Skyrim.esm`/PEX MQ101 stage binding already audited in [INTRO_MQ101_MULTIPLAYER.md](reverse-engineering/INTRO_MQ101_MULTIPLAYER.md) maps stage 160 to the first outdoor-control handoff. Adopt it only as the vanilla New Game readiness guard, together with loaded cell and native movement/look handlers; reject a timer or merely seeing the RaceSex menu close as proof that the host can play. This is a narrow onboarding rule, not a general quest/scene synchronization solution.
- Independent source review: local Reviewer A found `SessionState` never leaves 2, leaving follower camera/input and essential/heal protection active forever. Local Reviewer B independently found `DestroyParty` leaks the world-ready `numPausesGame` increment and blocked input on disconnect. The changes introduce an all-member gameplay-ready state 3 and release the intro-only gates there, while cleaning the loading gate on leave. Agreement is not in-game validation: protocol and paired native measurements are required.
- [Skyrim Platform frame event documentation](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/new_events.md) offers a frame callback after a new game/save load. We did not import its runtime; the existing native `UpdateEvent` and party authority are already the integration point, avoiding an additional scripting scheduler in the intro.
- In paired build `6D875220...`, after both names were accepted, the host advanced naturally to MQ101 stage 100 but remained there for more than eight minutes with the executioner axe overhead. The follower screenshot instead showed an open Helgen street: visual 1:1 is still false. The installed `Skyrim.esm` stage map says stage 100 runs `Fragment_15` (`Scene4.Stop(); AlduinAttackScene.Start()`); `AlduinAttackScene` is `0xAA7DF`, reported playing with raw phase word `UINT32_MAX`, and `TorriLedgeScene` `0xE6D69` remained at phase 8. Stage 99's `Fragment_309` (Alduin landing-event registration) arrived about two seconds after stage 100. The raw phase word is not yet a validated blocked-state proof. Local Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviewed the evidence; both initially suspected Torolf ownership churn, then independently cautioned that scene actor availability/event registration or start conditions need direct measurement. No `SetStage` bypass was applied.
- The next diagnostic build `A560ADCE...` confirmed Torolf `0xAAF97` is repeatedly removed from discovery because its parent cell `0x13A67` is **unattached** and it has **no 3D** on the outside Helgen host (`dead=false`, `remote=false`, reconciliation stage 0). It is reacquired roughly six seconds later with a new server ID. That makes a generic attached-cell retention patch inappropriate for this actor and does not prove that Torolf blocks Alduin's scene. A targeted actor probe was added to the cached snapshot for scene aliases outside the player's nearby-cell scan; it has not yet been exercised at the stage-100 stall because the paired cinematic-save experiment hung first.
- The installed-master scene audit was extended to print actor/action bindings. `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/mq101-scene-bindings.json` shows `MQ101TorriLedgeScene` `0xE6D69` has only aliases 51 (`CivilianTorolf`) and 52 (`CivilianTorri`); its phase-8 actions target both. `MQ101AlduinAttackScene1` `0xAA7DF` also uses aliases 51/52 in phase 0 (actions 132/136) among 22 total actors. This is an exact static overlap, not proof that Skyrim disallows the concurrent scenes. A solo vanilla baseline and live alias/scene readiness probe are needed before altering scene scheduling.
- In paired build `A118EA00...`, MQ101 stage 100 advanced naturally to 120 after roughly 7.4 seconds, then to 130/140/145/150/160. Both clients entered session state 3 and had native movement/look controls unblocked. This disproves a deterministic stage-100 scene deadlock, though the earlier long stall remains unexplained. Both player actors were still `networkEntities` authority `unassigned`, network ID 0, with 3D and valid cell after the handoff; no `playerId != 0` remote entity existed on either client, and the screenshots showed unrelated views. `DiscoveryService` had first seen form `0x14` before its parent cell existed; `RequestServerAssignment` deferred, but discovery emits `ActorAddedEvent` only once and never retried. A first retry implementation scanned all unassigned actors, but Reviewer A review found that could re-register intentionally removed NPCs. The deployed revision instead marks only actors explicitly deferred for a missing parent cell, retries marked actors once per second, and clears the marker on removal or disconnect.
- Both clients accepted Skyrim's normal F5 quicksave input in state 3. The host wrote a 2,928,871-byte `Quicksave0...Host...ess`; the follower wrote a 2,915,687-byte `Quicksave0...Follower 1...ess`. Copies are retained at `Tools/InGameTests/artifacts/checkpoint-stage160-20260924-024952/{Host,Follower}.ess`. No matching `.skse` was observed on the host. These are candidate local checkpoints, not yet validated by reload, synchronized save transaction, or restore across machines.
- Paired reload with client SHA-256 `C8AA16E2...` used each machine's own F5 save through shared Continue. Both returned to MQ101 stage 160, party session state 3, and native movement/look controls unblocked. The earlier broad 1-second retry then registered host form `0x14` as player ID 1/network ID 25 and follower form `0x14` as player ID 2/network ID 27. On the opposite PC each became a distinct remote actor with 3D, seven root children, a ready animation graph, 96 rendered bones, and three worn armor pieces. The original save filenames and SHA-256 hashes are retained in the versioned checkpoint artifact's `host/` and `follower/` directories. This proves the narrow two-PC reload/render-entity handoff; it does not prove visual pose parity.
- The safer deferred-marker build `AB1B6116...` passed 166 unit assertions/16 cases and all protocol scenarios, then loaded the same paired stage-160 saves. Both players again registered (IDs 25/27), each had a remote 3D/render counterpart, and movement/look controls were unblocked. A native W-key test moved the host from roughly `(15584,-81252,8205)` to `(15583,-81338,8208)` and the follower's remote-host transform converged to `(15582,-81337,8207)`. This validates movement replication after reload, not combat, camera, ragdoll, or seamless visual parity.
- A fresh New Game on that same safer build registered the two native player actors without any reload (IDs 17/18); the host log recorded `Retrying deferred cell assignment for actor 14` at 03:14:59. Both MQ101 instances reached stage 41 with their game loops still running. At stage 41 the host's MQ101 scene list showed no playing scene while the follower still had scene `0x4679B` (decimal 288667) at raw phase 8. The host cart then remained near `(14584,-80735,8237)` and the follower near `(14633,-80907,8206)`, with visibly different camera tilt, NPC gaze, and poses in paired 03:28 captures. This run has not reached character creation; the earlier paired creator and stage-160 checkpoint tests remain separate results. Do not infer a fixed fresh-intro path from them.
- Reviewer A recommended resolving each active MQ101 scene action's alias to its live actor and package before blaming a scene wait on ownership. A read-only snapshot field now reports that mapping only for MQ101. The next probe build `E29A09FF...` compiled and deployed on both PCs. Its first fresh run remained at MQ101 stage 12 with the host black and follower in a loading menu; a clean restart/launch reached stage 41, but both clients' tracked `MQ101Scene3` had already ended before the requested capture, so the new alias fields did not yet identify a blocker. Follower logs from the prior run show it received `hostPlaying=false` for scene `0x4679B` at 03:17:46 while localPlaying remained true; this establishes the local scene can outlive the host's notification, not that this alone caused the stage-41 stall.
- In the second probe run at MQ101 stage 41, host cart `0xBB970` (decimal 768368) was `(16366.6,-80768.0,8372.0)` while follower cart was `(16674.0,-80776.4,8374.0)`. The follower snapshot had `hasHostPhysics=true`, `hostPhysicsPosition=(16366.6,-80768.0,8371.9)`, and `hostPhysicsBodyDriven=true`: transport delivered the host pose but default playback did not apply it. This is expected from `ObjectService::ApplyRemotePhysics`: the dynamic-body servo is default-off, and only the one selected diagnostic body receives the bounded per-frame trial. Prior pre-step velocity/pose/kinematic trials in this document failed to control Skyrim's constrained cart solver and showed large jumps; do not enable the generic servo solely from this gap. A replacement needs a whole-island/scene ownership design and a paired smoothness test, not a single-body position warp.
- After that failed intro, the same deployed probe build used shared Continue to reload both original stage-160 F5 saves. Each returned to MQ101 stage 160/session state 3 with a registered local player (server IDs 25/27), one remote player entity, HUD only, and native controls unblocked. A 160-ms native W input on the follower moved it from approximately `(15586.6,-81427.6,8205.2)` to `(15660.1,-81399.3,8202.6)`; the host's remote-follower transform converged to `(15660,-81399,8202)` on the next snapshot. Thus both directions of basic player movement replication are tested after checkpoint reload. This is still not fresh-intro completion or full visual/gameplay parity.

## Temporary NPC identity, targeting, and ragdoll triage (2026-09-24)

- [TiltedEvolution's open leveled-actor identity issue](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/issues/639) documents that independent local picks can render different actors for one network entity. Its proposed owner-pick reconciliation is relevant, but does not identify script-created FF references across clients. [The project's combat-target issue](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/issues/741) separately shows that local AI pursuit can disagree with a network target. These are upstream issue reports, not verification of this fork's behavior.
- Source inspection here found that `OnAssignCharacterRequest` previously skipped the existing-entity lookup for all FF temporary references, so every client's discovery of the same scripted NPC could create a separate server entity. `OnCharacterSpawn` also synthesizes a temporary actor before the native copy's assignment reply can arrive. A provisional party-scoped reconciliation now requires matching base/pick, cell/worldspace, a recent placement within 64 game units, and exactly one candidate; it never merges the same player's two discoveries, player characters, summons, or mounts. If the spawn wins the race, the assignment path retains the native quest-bound actor and retires its synthetic proxy. This is a conservative heuristic, **not** a stable cross-process actor GUID and **not** an in-game-verified fix.
- Reviewer A recommended placement provenance plus per-player ordinal binding and a wider radius; Reviewer B recommended a content/appearance fingerprint and narrow spatial/time bounds. The current patch uses per-player bindings and unique narrow spatial matching, but not ordinal matching or appearance hashing. It intentionally leaves ambiguous groups separate; logs and paired NPC counts must establish whether that is too conservative before widening it. Both reviewers found that duplicate ownership confounds target and ragdoll diagnosis. Reviewer B additionally found that NPC death currently syncs as a boolean, not a physics pose/impulse stream, while combat-target override is still selected-actor trial-gated. Neither review is live validation.

### Naked outfits and duplicated Headsman follow-up (2026-09-24)

- A live stage-160 checkpoint capture (`authority-20260924-085826.json` and host screenshot `feedback-20260924-090722-47972.bmp`) shows two separate Headsman actors, `FF000DB3` and `FF000DE7`, with the same base `000AA7D6`, distinct server IDs, and about 150 game units separation. The initial 64-unit temporary-reference matcher did not reconcile them; both were registered, so the old “untracked actor count” check missed the duplicate. In a fresh new-game run at MQ101 stage 41 (`authority-20260924-091812.json`), only one Headsman identity is observed, but this does not validate later stages.
- Several placed actors in the stage-160 checkpoint had zero worn armor on *both* PCs after inventory assignment. That could be a saved-state artifact or a current destructive update; the capture alone does not distinguish them. Source inspection confirms that `TESObjectREFR::SetInventory` calls `RemoveAllItems`, while the owner's assignment response echoes an earlier inventory snapshot that may precede native outfit initialization. [CommonLibSSE-NG's reference inventory implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) supports distinguishing live reference inventory from base/container state; [TiltedEvolution's inventory-related crash report](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/issues/787) is a reminder to validate native inventory mutations in-game rather than inferring safety from serialization tests.
- Reviewer A recommended skipping the owner's old inventory echo, preserving native outfits against an empty assignment snapshot, and matching temporary actors against creation as well as current position. Reviewer B agreed on inventory risk but cautioned against broad temporary-actor merging; it preferred retaining the ambiguous-candidate guard and a narrower match. The implementation now skips owner-echo inventory writes, preserves nonempty native inventory when the assignment snapshot is empty, stores temporary actor creation position, and tests a 150-unit cross-client offset under a 192-unit single-candidate gate. This is still a heuristic, not a stable scene-spawn GUID. The new protocol test passes. If a fresh campaign still shows a duplicate or naked actor, capture the two actors' server IDs, base IDs, spawn times and both inventories before widening the matcher again.
- After deploying SHA-256 client `31344AE0...` and server `28C00534...` to both PCs, paired Continue of the stage-160 checkpoint produced `authority-20260924-092807.json`: exactly one registered Headsman (`FF000DB3`, one server ID) in the compared scene, no untracked current-cell actors, and armor counts matching on both PCs for the five previously naked sampled actors (`D5693` 4/4, `F94B6` 4/4, `F94B4` 3/3, `F94B7` 4/4, `2BF9E` 2/2). The host screenshot `feedback-20260924-092706-53088.bmp` also shows the background soldier clothed. An earlier capture at `09:21` showed zero armor immediately after load; the snapshot had not settled. This confirms the checkpoint regression is absent in this settled paired run, **not** that all spawn timings or every actor throughout a fresh playthrough are fixed. Scene phase, camera, loaded reference, geometry, and pose parity still fail in the same report.
- That “one Headsman” conclusion was incomplete. The follower `nearbyActorAudit` at `09:38` showed **two local Headsman references bound to the same server ID** `1048586`: native `FF000DB3` and synthetic `FF000DE5`. `networkEntities` contained both, so the old “untracked” and server-identity-pair checks missed them. Follower logs show the native assignment completing at `09:25:00.326`, while the synthetic `ActorAdded`/3D setup completed milliseconds later. The assignment cleanup filtered on `FormIdComponent` and missed a proxy with only `RemoteComponent::CachedRefId`. The cleanup now checks both identities, and `Compare-TwoPcAuthority.ps1` fails when any two nearby references share a network ID. The old build fails that new check (`authority-20260924-094613.json`); the deployed race patch logs `Retired early temporary proxy FF000DE5 for native FF000DB3` and the paired capture `authority-20260924-094752.json` has zero duplicate network IDs. Keep testing other spawn races and cells; this is one concrete race fix.
- [Improved Camera's open head-bob profile](https://github.com/qudix/skse-improved-camera/blob/main/assets/ImprovedCamera/assets/Profiles/Default.ini) distinguishes first-person head-bob position from camera-state switching. The follower camera complaint is **not yet attributed to the host cinematic camera service**: in `authority-20260924-095024.json`, that service reports `inputGated=false` and no retained host snapshot on either PC. Over 2.5 seconds with zero move/look input and a stationary follower player root, her native camera root moved roughly `(−0.47,+1.32,−0.36)` game units while the host camera root was fixed. This supports native first-person/camera animation motion, but does not yet identify the engine state or prove the reason it persists.
- [BethesdaLibrary's Havok animation format analysis](https://github.com/BadDogSkyrim/BethesdaLibrary/blob/main/docs/file-formats/animations.md) explains that skeleton pose and constrained ragdoll rigid bodies are distinct runtime data. Our paired `authority-20260924-095024.json` confirms the network pose packets for three dead actors had matching source ticks and checksums, but their native/render pose checksums differed. A selected 18-body corpse probe in `authority-20260924-095223.json` measured no ragdoll checksum match and a maximum root-relative render-world bone error around 42 game units. Therefore the current bone transport is not sufficient to claim ragdoll parity; host rigid-body state, constraints, physics-step ownership, and render application need a separate bounded trial before any general playback patch.
- The latest paired audit (`authority-20260924-095911.json`) exposes each player's health, magicka, stamina, action state, controls, capped inventory contents, watched quest stages/objectives, and duplicate-network-ID count. The sampled MQ101 stage/objectives, three inventory entries, and 100/100 health match; this is a point-in-time observability result, not proof that quest or loot mutations synchronize across all scenarios. The comparator now explicitly fails on divergent dead-actor native/render pose checksums and on a stationary follower camera moving while the player root and host camera remain still.
- A source-only Reviewer A review independently concluded that `CameraService::ApplyAfterNativeUpdate` cannot be writing the camera while its reported input gate and host snapshot are both false. It hypothesized that the first-person behavior graph may be re-entering a state, and that matching transmitted graph pose cannot control a corpse whose render bones are driven by peer-local Havok rigid bodies. Neither hypothesis is a demonstrated native causal chain. [Improved Camera's first-person profile](https://github.com/qudix/skse-improved-camera/blob/main/assets/ImprovedCamera/assets/Profiles/Default.ini) confirms head bob can be configured independently of camera mode; [CommonLibSSE-NG](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG) is the source-backed native-layout baseline. A bounded selected-actor diagnostic now records at most 32 rigid-body transforms, velocities, world attachment flags, and motion types to classify the first actual physics divergence before attempting any writer or network protocol. No ragdoll-body or camera override was enabled by this instrumentation.
- The selected corpse `0x000F94B6` live probe after stage-160 Continue found 18/18 bodies present in both Havok worlds, with identical type sequence (twelve type-2 sphere-inertia, six type-3 box-inertia), but different body transforms (first eight translation deltas approximately 1.17–2.05 Havok units) and different body checksums. [CommonLibSSE's motion-type enum](https://github.com/Ryan-rsm-McKenzie/CommonLibSSE/blob/master/include/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.h) identifies types 2 and 3 as simulated inertia modes; the initial diagnostic counted only type 1 as dynamic and was corrected to include simulated types 2, 3 and 6. This rejects the hypothesis that one peer simply lacks ragdoll bodies or has them all fixed/keyframed. It does **not** prove the first divergence occurs in physics; death impulse, initial pose, and collision contacts remain unmeasured.
- Rebuilt and deployed client `50245227...` to both PCs; 168 assertions/17 native tests passed. In the live selected-corpse probe, both peers reported 18 simulated bodies and zero keyframed/fixed bodies, but distinct body checksums. The paired `authority-20260924-101926.json` capture shows MQ101 stage 160 on both, no duplicate server identity in nearby actors, three dead actors with differing native/render pose, and 1.26 game units of follower-camera travel over 2.5 seconds with a fixed player root and fixed host camera. This verifies the new diagnostic and duplicate-race regression in this checkpoint, while explicitly leaving ragdoll and camera parity unresolved. The second Reviewer B review command did not return and was stopped; its earlier independent duplicate/inventory review is recorded above, but it did not validate this ragdoll/camera diagnosis.

### Camera and corpse update ownership follow-up (2026-09-24)

- [Improved Camera's open first-person configuration](https://github.com/qudix/skse-improved-camera/blob/main/assets/ImprovedCamera/assets/Profiles/Default.ini) separates idle/walk head-bob from native camera state. Its specific settings are not a patch for our mod, but motivate measuring the native first-person camera update before overriding it. A bounded 64-sample, 50-ms read-only trace now records camera-root translation immediately before and after Skyrim's original camera-state update. Neither this trace nor the selected reference probe writes game state.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently found that our cinematic camera playback is inert at gameplay stage 160 (`inputGated=false`, no host snapshot) and that `InterpolationSystem::Update` has no dead-actor guard before `ForcePosition`, rotation, and graph-variable writes. Reviewer A proposed stopping dead interpolation and checking the suppressed native SetPosition path; Reviewer B suspected the many calls were mostly Skyrim-native. A new selected-reference counter distinguishes remote calls suppressed by `HookSetPosition` from calls made under our `ScopedReferencesOverride`. In `authority-20260924-103428.json`, dead NPC `0x000F94B6` on the follower had 191 suppressed native calls and 192 permitted override calls, while the host had 834 non-remote calls. Thus both native and mod-forced position attempts are occurring; the old total/last-caller counters could not classify this. The near-equal follower counts justify a bounded dead-only interpolation skip trial, not a blanket removal of the native hook.
- The dead-only interpolation skip was deployed as client `E6017141...`. `authority-20260924-103721.json` then reported 192 suppressed native calls and **zero** permitted override calls for the same follower corpse; host/follower bodies remained 18/18 simulated, with mean translation disagreement around 1.67 Havok units and three dead actors still failing native/render pose parity. This is a verified removal of one local ownership fight, not a ragdoll parity fix. It also means future corpse placement must be validated at death and cell-load transitions; the current checkpoint alone cannot establish those cases.
- A new read-only 64-sample native camera trace observes the original first-person state update at roughly 50-ms intervals. In the stage-160 paired capture after the dead-only build, all 64 follower samples had nonzero before/after camera translation (maximum about 0.54 game units per sampled update), whereas all 64 host samples had zero. This pins the observed bob to Skyrim's original first-person camera update rather than `CameraService::Apply`, but not yet to a specific animation variable or skeleton node. Do not treat moving the camera to a fixed coordinate as a causal fix.
- [SkyrimSouls' source](https://github.com/kassent/SkyrimSouls/blob/master/Skyrim/src/Events/ScriptEvent.cpp) confirms native trigger-enter/leave event sources, matching this fork's `EventDispatcherManager` layout and existing quest-sink pattern. A capped 64-event, form-ID-only trigger trace is now registered by the test bridge and exposed in paired reports. Client `08C0E713...` loaded on both PCs without a startup crash; `authority-20260924-104515.json` captured 28 host and 25 follower trigger events alongside 64/64 camera samples and MQ101 stage160 on both. Different counts alone do not prove a bug because the two players occupy different positions and temporary reference IDs differ; correlate by shared actor/trigger identity and scene phase before asserting parity.
- Reviewer A reviewed the patch and found no definite crash in the new probes, but noted that `Actor::IsDead()` invokes Papyrus by name on every call and is too expensive in the per-frame interpolation loop. [CommonLibSSE-NG's `ActorState1::lifeState` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/A/ActorState.h) identifies bits 21–24 and value 2 as native dead state. The interpolation guard now reads those bits directly; client `3E4602FE...` still showed zero follower override calls for selected dead actor `0xF94B6` in `authority-20260924-105720.json`. Resurrection and death-transition cases remain untested. Reviewer B's post-patch review could not access the latest source due its shell sandbox and is not treated as a code review; its earlier source-accessible diagnosis is recorded above.
- Reviewer A also noted that raw trigger events use per-machine uptime and local FF form IDs. The paired report now maps currently registered actor form IDs to server IDs where possible, marks temporary trigger IDs as unstable, and converts times to each capture's local sample time. Unmapped historical actors remain explicit nulls; this is not a full cross-peer event-correlation engine.
- `SceneTimelineService` in this fork currently **observes and relays** leader scene phase changes but does not apply them on followers. In the live stage-160 run, the active `MQ101DragonAttack` scene `0xD0594` was phase 3 on the host and phase 2 on the follower, while both MQ101 and `MQ101DragonAttack` quest stages matched. Phase 2 had additional eligible actions 6, 7 and 10 that were absent from host phase 3. This is a concrete scene divergence and a plausible contributor to the follower's ongoing first-person animation, **not yet proof** that it drives camera bob. The watched-quest diagnostic was generalized to resolve active action aliases beyond MQ101 so the held actor can be identified before any phase write.
- [SKSE's published vanilla `Scene.psc`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ianpatt/skse64/master/scripts/vanilla/Scene.psc) exposes `IsActionComplete(int)` as a read-only native query, but no phase setter. [CommonLibSSE-NG's `BGSSceneAction` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/B/BGSSceneAction.h) identifies its action index at +0x18. The watched-scene snapshot now includes that ID and its +0x14 status byte and, only when the registered Papyrus native is available, queries completion for at most 32 phase-eligible actions across the entire snapshot. This is diagnostic only; a direct write to the scene's raw phase word would skip native transition side effects and is not justified. The initial query build and offline tests passed; the final global-cap/status-byte edit has not yet completed a rebuild or paired runtime test because the local command environment stalled.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently reviewed the read-only query. Both found the wrapper ABI and guards reasonable. Reviewer A caught the original per-scene rather than global 32-call cap and suggested logging the scene action's native status byte; both changes were made. Reviewer B could not independently verify the +0x18 layout from this repository alone; the CommonLib source above provides the external layout, but a live value check is still needed. Neither review establishes scene parity or a safe phase-advance patch.

### Live scene action and corpse-cell diagnosis (2026-09-24)

- Paired `authority-20260924-174458.json` validated the action query: `MQ101DragonAttackScene1` `0xD0594` is phase 3 on the host and phase 2 on the follower. On the follower, action ID 12 has status byte 1 and `Scene.IsActionComplete(12)=false`; alias 12 resolves to remote Ralof `0x2BF9E`. The host has already left that phase. A read-only export of the installed `Skyrim.esm` through the [Mutagen record model](https://github.com/Mutagen-Modding/Mutagen) identified action 12 as a package action with package `0xD76CA`; the live host and follower report that same package ID. Package assignment is therefore not evidence of package execution/completion. Reviewer A found this fork's `HookActorProcess` returns before native AI processing for remote actors, a strong causal hypothesis for the stuck package action. Reviewer B agreed that the remote scene action is incomplete, but its claim that this causes camera bob is unproven. The native camera trace still shows follower-only motion; it does not identify the upstream animation or shake state.
- The user reported missing dead bodies. A live bridge snapshot shows dead actor server ID 23 / `0xF94B3` exists with 3D on both PCs, but host parent cell `38986` and position `(14854.9,-81927.7,8295.9)` differ from follower parent cell `38955` and position `(14691.3,-81635.4,8233.2)`. This is a concrete corpse cell/position split, not proof that every visually missing body has the same cause. The three dead actors in the nearby parity audit remain present with 3D on both PCs but have divergent ragdoll poses.
- Source inspection found `Movement` already serializes `CellId` and `WorldSpaceId`, yet `Code/server/Services/CharacterService.cpp` stored incoming owner cell fields without copying them into outgoing reference updates, and `Code/client/Services/Generic/CharacterService.cpp` discarded them. The server relay now fills both fields; the client records the latest authoritative cell/worldspace/position/tick in `InterpolationComponent` and exposes them in the read-only game snapshot. This is an observability and transport fix, **not yet a corpse placement fix**. We explicitly do not call `MoveTo` on ragdolls until the paired capture verifies the relayed cell and a safe transition policy. [CommonLibSSE-NG's reference movement implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) confirms native movement is a cell/worldspace operation, but this fork's `MoveTo` wrapper warns it can fail and delete the reference. Reviewer A recommended a settled, rate-limited corpse correction after validating relay, not per-frame teleport.
- Paired reload with client `75B30190...` and server `13687617...` did not complete: the follower crashed just after its gameplay barrier released. Its 1.13 GB dump (`crash_UTC_2026-09-24_22-55-36.dmp`) faults on a null read in `d3d11.dll+0x20398`; matching-image symbol inspection places `FaceGenSystem::Update` and `CharacterService::RunRemoteUpdates` on the candidate stack, during native face-tint resource creation. This does **not** prove the cell relay caused the crash (the changed fields are not used to move or render actors), but launch stability must be restored before claiming that relay tested. The source-backed guard added to `FaceGenSystem` checks the Skyrim renderer's device/context and native texture holder before invoking its D3D11 resource helper, leaving face-tint generation pending if they are unavailable. [CommonLibSSE-NG's renderer and shader/geometry code](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG) provides reference layouts, but the exact helper's internal null receiver remains unverified; paired retry and reviewer checks are required.
- Both Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviewed the renderer guard. Both found its early-return reference count balanced, but Reviewer A noted that a null renderer device is unlikely *after* a rendered load screen; the guard might not address the D3D11 crash. They also flagged unchecked `CreateTexture` / `CreateResourceView` results and possible resource/context lifetime races. The guard must remain a trial until repeated paired loads establish whether it helps. A subsequent paired Continue with client `0BE71E4F...` reached active gameplay on both PCs and `authority-20260924-180218.json` again showed the same scene-phase and ragdoll divergences. One successful retry does not prove the crash fixed.
- That successful retry confirmed the transport fix end-to-end: follower `game_snapshot` for dead actor `0xF94B3` reported `authorityCellId=38986`, `authorityWorldSpaceId=60`, `authorityPosition=(14854,-81927,8295)` matching the host's current cell/position, while its local corpse remained in cell `38955` at `(14691.254,-81635.36,8233.152)`. A bounded remote-corpse correction now waits for 1 s of owner-position stability, requires dead state, loaded 3D, valid host cell/worldspace, same local worldspace and target inside the loaded exterior grid (or current interior), no active mount interaction, and a >128-unit or cell mismatch. It caps moves to one per 250 ms globally and one per target position/cell per 5 s, and reports attempts in the snapshot. It uses the existing native `MoveActor` only for this cross-cell/large-drift case. This build has compiled and passed offline tests, but paired visual/cell stability is **not yet verified**.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviewed that first corpse-correction build. Reviewer A found a blocking exterior-loop bug: native exterior `MoveActor` chooses a cell from position, while the host's parent-cell ID can lag a ragdoll crossing a grid border, causing a correction every 5 s. It also found the absence of a per-target retry cap and a potential entity-view invalidation when moving inside the interpolation loop. The patch now uses owner-position error rather than parent-cell mismatch as the exterior trigger, caps a stable target at two attempts, rejects authority data older than 2 s, handles transport-clock resets, and defers one selected `MoveActor` until after the entity loop. Reviewer B warned the loaded-grid/worldspace gate can skip some legitimate divergent-player cases and that `MoveTo` can still visibly disturb a ragdoll; those limits remain for paired evaluation. The changed correction build has not yet been paired-tested.

### Controlled corpse recovery and scene/camera separation (2026-09-24)

- A subsequent paired Continue with client `C5F79D...` showed four sampled dead actor reference cells/positions already matching within about 1–2 game units, but all `corpseCorrectionAttempts=0`. That run tested the relay, not the correction branch. Reviewer A hypothesized that settled corpses might stop sending movement and fail the 2-second freshness gate. Source inspection of `RunLocalUpdates` and `AnimationSystem::Serialize` showed dead local actors are still serialized at the 50-ms cadence; a live follower snapshot observed a fresh authority sample roughly 0.7 s old. The stale-packet hypothesis is not the blocker in that checkpoint, although packet loss remains possible in other sessions.
- A local-only `test_displace_remote_corpse` bridge command now queues one selected dead, loaded, remote NPC displacement onto the game thread. It uses the same native `MoveTo` wrapper as production correction; [CommonLibSSE-NG's reference movement implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) confirms `SetPosition`/`MoveTo` ultimately invoke native `MoveTo_Impl`. This test command is deliberate fault injection, **not** production movement behavior. Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently reviewed it: both accepted the atomic window-to-game-thread handoff; both warned that queue acceptance alone does not prove execution, and that the 320-unit trial tests position error rather than an interior wrong-cell-only case. Reviewer B additionally warned that the unbounded offset may intersect scenery and that a just-dead ragdoll would not be a settled test target. The actual trial used a settled exterior corpse already sampled on both machines.
- Client `CBA66C2B...` built and passed 168 assertions/17 test cases. In a paired stage-160 Continue, selected corpse `0xF94B3` was at follower cell `38986`, position `(14854,-81927,8295)`, matching the owner's relayed target, with zero prior correction attempts. The one-shot follower displacement of +320 X queued successfully. About two seconds later the follower's `corpseCorrectionAttempts` was **1**, and its cell/position had returned to the host target. More than 25 seconds later the count was still 1 and the reference remained aligned; both game bridges responded. This verifies bounded recovery for one settled exterior, loaded, dead remote actor under deliberate position drift. It does **not** verify natural corpse divergence, visual smoothness during correction, cross-cell interior recovery, ragdoll bone parity, or all deaths.
- [Mutagen's Skyrim record model](https://github.com/Mutagen-Modding/Mutagen) exported installed `Skyrim.esm` scene `MQ101DragonAttackScene1` `0xD0594`. Phase 2's action 12 is a package action assigned to alias 12 (Ralof) with package `0xD76CA`; the phase has no direct player-camera action. In the paired checkpoint the host was at scene phase 3 while the follower was at phase 2, and only the follower's action 12 was incomplete. With the existing opt-in native remote-process trial enabled **only for Ralof `0x2BF9E` on the follower**, 291 native process calls over about four seconds advanced her scene to phase 3. The trial was immediately disabled. This is direct evidence that the suppressed native remote AI path held that scene action, but it is not a safe general policy to enable all remote AI: it could make NPC decisions diverge from the host.
- The same trial did **not** stop the follower's camera bob. Its 64-sample native camera trace still had a maximum per-update translation of about 0.50 game units after scene phase 3. Thus the stuck Ralof scene action and follower-only camera motion are separate observed problems in this checkpoint. [Improved Camera's first-person configuration](https://github.com/qudix/skse-improved-camera/blob/main/assets/ImprovedCamera/assets/Profiles/Default.ini) remains a useful reference for distinct head-bob/first-person states, not a demonstrated fix. Next camera work must compare the follower player's first-person graph/camera-node inputs against the host at equivalent presentation time; suppressing or teleporting the camera would mask the cause.

### Player first-person graph audit (2026-09-24)

- [CommonLibSSE-NG's graph holder](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/src/RE/I/IAnimationGraphManagerHolder.cpp) exposes named graph-variable reads and native manager updates, while [its PlayerCamera layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/main/include/RE/P/PlayerCamera.h) distinguishes camera state and camera root. [Improved Camera's profile](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/qudix/skse-improved-camera/main/assets/ImprovedCamera/assets/Profiles/Default.ini) independently configures idle/walk head-bob and camera positioning. These sources support measuring the player behavior graph separately from camera transform output; they do not identify this checkpoint's bob cause. A one-shot snapshot field now reuses the existing locked, bounded native actor-graph reader for the **local player**, recording graph state/time, pose/render checksums, and up to 256 raw variable values. It is diagnostic only and adds no per-frame graph work. Paired validation and independent review are pending.
- The first paired snapshot with client `F3E008EC...` showed both players in a 301-variable graph with a 99-bone pose and 87 readable render bones. The host's camera-update trace had zero translation while the follower's reached 0.475 units/update. Four of the first 256 graph slots differed: 54, 70, 176 and 200. Graph 0's `Master_Behavior` descriptor names these `kPitchOffset`, `kbVoiceReady`, `kVelocityZ` and `kbIsInMT`; **the names are provisional** until the sampled graph index is known. The follower's sampled slot 54 was approximately -0.396 while the host's was zero; slot 200 was false versus true. These are correlations, not proven camera causes.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently accepted the bounded one-shot reader but found it insufficient to attribute the bob. Reviewer A identified a concrete labeling risk: `TESObjectREFR::SaveAnimationVariables` deliberately chooses graph index 0 for the player, while `SampleNativeActorAnimation` chooses the manager's active index; the graph-0 descriptor cannot safely name active-graph slots if those indices differ. Both requested graph identity and render-world evidence. The snapshot now includes graph count/index and render-world checksum. Following Reviewer A's transform-chain proposal, the existing 50-ms native camera trace additionally captures root **local** translation and stable parent **world** translation before/after each native state update. This distinguishes movement written into the camera root from movement inherited from a parent. It remains read-only; no camera or graph writes are enabled. Build and paired validation of this extension are pending.
- Paired client `54F2A62C...` validated that both players have two graphs and that active graph index **1** is named `firstPerson` by the live native project data. The previous graph-0 variable labels were therefore **wrong** for those four differing slots and must not be used to choose a write. In the 64-sample trace, the host's camera world/local and parent world translations were static. The follower's camera **world and local** translation both moved up to 0.483 units/update while the parent translation was static; the parent was the same readable pointer across all samples. This pins the immediate movement to the native first-person camera update writing the camera root's local translation, not an inherited parent translation. It does not identify the state input that makes the follower update oscillate, or prove rotation/scale parity.
- The local [SageHavokEditor first-person project source](https://github.com/lennart99v/SageHavokEditor) explicitly treats Skyrim's `_1stperson` graph as separate from the third-person character graph. The installed `Skyrim - Animations.bsa` contains `_1stperson/behaviors/0_master.hkx`, extracted read-only to `C:\Users\mwalt\SkyrimResearch\firstperson-0_master.hkx` for offline variable-map research; it is not deployed to either game. The installed executable's exact graph names are still best verified from live runtime because animation mods can replace this asset.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviewed the transform trace. Both accepted the observed local/world split but flagged limits: a stable parent pointer is not full transform stability, raw parent reads can race native mutation, and the added `timeInState` could emit non-finite JSON. The follow-up guards parent reads through the existing native readable-range helper, skips trace sampling when the state does not belong to the player camera or the original update hook is missing, and sanitizes `timeInState`. These follow-up changes and the new read-only `FirstPersonState` spring/damping/pitch snapshot follow the [1.7-aware CommonLibSSE-NG FirstPersonState layout](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/2dde70e8bdf9890bbd5e648966c7d2c24e83092f/include/RE/F/FirstPersonState.h); they still require compile, reviewer and paired validation. No native camera-state mutation is enabled.
- That first safety edit called a helper that existed only inside `GameTestService.cpp`; the compiler caught it before deployment. The camera trace now uses bounded `ReadProcessMemory` for its two sampled parent points, avoiding an unchecked dereference in the native hook. Client `DABB4984...` compiled, passed 168 assertions/17 cases, and a paired Continue reached gameplay on both PCs. The native first-person state was readable on both: while speed and move/look inputs were zero, host `currentPitchOffset` and `targetPitchOffset` stayed zero, whereas follower values varied from about `-0.50` to `+0.08` across three one-shot samples, alongside a native camera-local movement peak of ~0.50 units/update. Host camera-local movement remained zero. Spring/dampening vectors also differed, but some components were nonzero on the stationary host, so they are not yet a standalone cause.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B confirmed the sampled FirstPersonState offsets match the 1.7-aware CommonLib source. Both warned that one-shot state samples cannot order cause and effect over a bob cycle; Reviewer A recommended observing the native `firstPersonCameraObj` and player 3D root as separate links in the camera chain. The snapshot now reads those two world positions with guarded native reads and validates the camera state ID through `ReadNative` rather than direct dereference. This extension is read-only and awaits build/paired validation. A future intervention should be a bounded, opt-in state trial only after the moving input is identified; continuous host-camera teleportation would hide the native cause and repeat the earlier visual snapping.
- Paired build `24CA599E...` did validate the camera-object extension. The host player 3D root and first-person camera object stayed fixed over three one-shot captures. The follower player 3D root stayed fixed, while its first-person camera object and camera root moved together by multiple game units. This distinguishes the bob from reference/root movement. The object-world measurement alone does not order its movement relative to graph and camera updates.
- Using [SageHavokEditor's HKX2 library](https://github.com/lennart99v/SageHavokEditor/tree/master/libs/HKX2Library), the installed vanilla `_1stperson/behaviors/0_master.hkx` was extracted read-only from `Skyrim - Animations.bsa` and deserialized offline. Its `hkbBehaviorGraphStringData.variableNames` contains 230 names, whereas both live players' active graph index 1 (`firstPerson`) had 301 values. The vanilla file therefore cannot label every live slot. A bounded player-only snapshot now reads the active graph's own `hkbBehaviorGraph::data` (+0x88), `hkbBehaviorGraphData::stringData` (+0x78), and `hkbBehaviorGraphStringData::variableNames` (+0x30) using the [1.7-aware CommonLibSSE-NG layouts](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/tree/2dde70e8bdf9890bbd5e648966c7d2c24e83092f/include/RE/H). Its `hkStringPtr` low-bit ownership flag is masked as in [CommonLib's implementation](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/H/hkStringPtr.cpp). Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviewed an initial reader and caught that it ran for every NPC and used per-character `VirtualQuery` while holding the graph lock. It was restricted to the player one-shot path and changed to bounded `ReadProcessMemory` calls. Reviewer A also noted that name/value ordering must not be assumed merely from the offline file; the live counts and named getter were checked next.
- Paired client `A6C0301C...` reached the shared stage-160 Continue on both PCs. Each live active first-person graph reported **301 names, 301 variable infos, and 301 values**, with byte-identical name sequences across PCs. Five of 301 raw values differed in a fresh paired capture: `TimeDelta` (53), `PitchOffset` (54), `bVoiceReady` (70), `VelocityZ` (176), and `bIsInMT` (200). The follower's slot-54 float exactly matched the separately read native `FirstPersonState::currentPitchOffset` in that one-shot capture; this is correlation, not write direction. The host's slot 54 was zero in that sample.
- [CommonLib's FirstPersonState layout](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/2dde70e8bdf9890bbd5e648966c7d2c24e83092f/include/RE/F/FirstPersonState.h) motivated a read-only before/after trace of +0x74/+0x78 pitch, +0x58 camera-object world, and camera-root local/world across the native state Update. Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviewed the patch. Both agreed the values alone cannot prove the upstream writer; Reviewer A found a non-finite JSON leak, fixed before the paired run. Reviewer B reported a ring-buffer race, but source inspection showed both producer and `GetNativeUpdateTrace()` hold `m_nativeTraceMutex`; that particular warning was not reproduced. The existing direct camera-node access and lifetime assumptions remain limitations. Build `6E94ABD8...` reached paired stage-160 Continue: all 64 follower trace samples changed pitch inside native `FirstPersonState::Update`, while all 64 host samples in that run left pitch at zero; the first-person object's world position was unchanged **within** each state update on both PCs. Across updates, the follower object still moved, and the camera root followed it. Thus the native camera update changes pitch and copies/follows an already-moving first-person object; it does not move that object within the sampled update.
- A further read-only named `GetVariableFloat("PitchOffset")` probe inside the same hook built as client `E6E76FBC...` and reached paired Continue. On both PCs, all 64 sampled named reads succeeded. For the follower, the named graph value equaled native current pitch **both before and after** each native camera update, and both changed on all 64 samples. The host's 64 samples in that run were static at zero. This confirms the live name-to-value lookup for this slot and that graph pitch and state pitch are changed in the same native update; it still does not establish whether the camera-state code writes the graph variable or consumes it. The follower first-person object's world position remained unchanged within each update but varied between updates. An additional later snapshot found the host also had a nonzero `PitchOffset` while its sampled pitch trace stayed static, so zero is not an invariant host baseline. Do not call this a fixed bob or a proven stuck intro animation. The next decisive observation is the first-person object/graph update phase before the camera update, or a bounded trial of the specific native state input after its writer is identified. External hardware write breakpoints are rejected here because an earlier breakpoint teardown caused a game crash (see above).

### Post-death host bob and movement lock (2026-09-24)

- The host reported the follower-like bob after a real combat death and get-up. A pre-restart live snapshot found both players alive at full health with stationary player 3D roots but moving first-person camera objects and native pitch. This rules out a follower-only camera-authority explanation. The code path in [TiltedEvolution PlayerService.cpp](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/client/Services/Generic/PlayerService.cpp) calls `RespawnPlayer`, then 1.5 seconds later zero-magnitude `AIProcess::KnockExplosion` as an anti-stuck ragdoll measure. [Upstream issue #878](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/issues/878) reports a distinct respawn-stuck condition when this knock does not apply. We therefore did not simply remove it; a local one-shot skip and counters were added for a controlled trial, default behavior unchanged. Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently reviewed the trial and warned that it might leave a ragdolled player stuck; the flag is consumed once and cleared when the death system disables.
- A bounded player-only first-person camera-object graph-phase trace now samples the object's local/world transforms immediately before and after native `UpdateAnimationGraphManager`, along with native camera-update sequence. Reviewer B flagged mixed camera-object lifetimes in the initial ring; a watch generation and reset guard resolved that. Reviewer A noted first-person-only coverage and the five-second watch window. Client `41CE280D...` passed 168 assertions/17 tests and reached paired Continue. At first the ring had zero samples, but with the player graph updating it filled to 64. In a settled host post-death capture, 64/64 samples changed camera-object **local** translation inside the graph update, 0/64 changed world translation inside that call, and 0/64 overlapped a native camera-state update. This places a camera-object motion writer in or below the animation graph update phase, before camera world propagation; it does not yet identify the animation state or upstream trigger. Across snapshots the object world Z continued changing with a static player root.
- Real-death baseline on client `41CE280D...`: host health crossed zero, `bleedingOut=true`, mod respawn counter rose to 1, and the extra knock attempt/applied counters rose to 1. After settling, player root/position stayed fixed at `(18242.234,-77993.51,8375.5625)` and speed 0 while first-person object Z changed `8495.861 -> 8497.548 -> 8497.706` and pitch varied; the user confirmed bob and inability to move. CameraService reported `inputGated=false`; the last sampled animation event was `GetUpBegin`, which is not proof it remained active.
- After a paired checkpoint restart, client `69E39B97...` ran the same real-death trial with only the host's one-shot extra knock skip armed. `respawns=1`, `postRespawnKnockAttempts=1`, `postRespawnKnocksApplied=0`, `lastKnockSkipped=true`. The host player root remained fixed at `(19099.31,-77881.47,8462.269)` while first-person object Z and pitch kept oscillating, so that extra knock **is not sufficient** to explain the bob. A focused game-window `W` helper returned success but the player position did not move. **Important correction:** a later DirectInput counter check found `keyboardEvents` did not rise during the helper action; the helper's success only means the Windows key injection ran, not that Skyrim consumed it. Thus this headless key test does **not** prove a movement lock. The user's first-run direct observation remains, but real held-key input must be measured. PlayerControls reported input blocking false and movement handler enabled; those flags do not prove the movement input reached the actor. The latest animation event was `IdleWalkingCameraStart`, also only a last-event record. The installed `_1stperson/behaviors/0_master.hkx` lists `IdleWalkingCameraStart` and `IdleWalkingCameraEnd` as distinct events, but the exported event-name list alone does not prove an unmatched start or that either is the current active state. The [Improved Camera head-bob profile](https://github.com/qudix/skse-improved-camera/blob/main/assets/ImprovedCamera/assets/Profiles/Default.ini) and [FP Camera Settle source](https://github.com/DCCStudios/SkyrimSE-FPCameraSettle) show camera animation/inertia is separable from reference movement; neither directly explains this respawn lock.
- A proposed synthetic health-zero bridge was compiled but **never deployed or used** and then removed. Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently identified that it would not reproduce hit/killer/ragdoll context of a real combat death and could misleadingly report success before bleedout. The two trials above used actual gameplay deaths. The next discriminating observation is whether native movement input enters `PlayerControls` during a held key and whether the actor/graph remains in an animation-driven or scripted-camera mode after `RespawnPlayer`; investigate the engine transition before adding a recovery mutation.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently reviewed the *observed* two-trial outcome and both suspected that `RespawnPlayer()` re-arms `NoBleedoutRecovery` too soon after restoring health and moving the player. That is plausible source-level reasoning, **not** validated causality. Their strongest proposed simple predicate is contradicted by the live stuck snapshot: `actorStateFlags1=0` (decoded `lifeState=0`, `knockState=0` using [CommonLibSSE-NG ActorState1 layout](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/A/ActorState.h)), `bleedingOut=false`, and live first-person graph variables `IsBleedingOut=0`, `IsBleedingOutTransition=0`, `bAnimationDriven=0`, `bMotionDriven=0`, `Speed=0`. The pre-death host snapshot also had `actorStateFlags1=0`, `actorStateFlags2=4104`. Therefore an uncleared *top-level* life/knock flag or those graph variables does not explain the lock; a nested graph state, AI process/character controller, or missed input remains possible. Both reviewers also over-read a successful 160 ms W helper as proof that movement entered PlayerControls; no during-key `MoveInputVec` sample was captured. The read-only next probe must sample that vector while held and then branch based on whether it changes.
- For the input discriminator, [Microsoft's `SendInput` documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-sendinput) and [scan-code KEYBDINPUT documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/ns-winuser-keybdinput) support a scan-code trial; Microsoft also marks the helper's old [`keybd_event` API as superseded](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-keybd_event). The local `GameTestKeyHelper` now uses a guarded, at-most-350-ms scan-code W down/up for its gameplay-only `forward` action, checks focus through the hold, retries key-up, and logs child outcome. Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently warned that the bridge acknowledges the parent process before the child sends input; `helperExit=0` is **not** evidence of delivery. They also flagged desktop focus and remapped-key limitations. Only the helper was rebuilt and backed up/replaced on the host (`GameTestKeyHelper.pre-scan-trial-20260924.exe`); the game binary/process was untouched. The first trial was rejected before injection with `Skyrim could not become foreground`; Skyrim's `keyboardEvents` and `inputPoll.forwarded` counters were unchanged. The stuck respawn and bob remain live, but no scan-code/held-key conclusion is available until the game is foreground and input is observed.
- The preserved second-run **pre-death** host snapshot was still available through `game_snapshot_at` at tick `7437582`. Comparing its 301 live named first-person graph values with a fresh stuck post-respawn snapshot found exactly five different slots: `TimeDelta`, `PitchOffset`, `bVoiceReady` (1→0), `VelocityZ`, and `bIsInMT` (1→0); top-level actor flags `0/4104` and PlayerControls enabled/blocked flags were identical. A fresh paired snapshot found the bobbing follower, which has **not** respawned (`respawns=0`), also has `bVoiceReady=0` and `bIsInMT=0`; host and follower then differed only in `TimeDelta`, `PitchOffset`, and `VelocityZ`. This supports a shared first-person graph-state signature but **disproves death/respawn as a necessary trigger for the follower**. The installed vanilla `_1stperson/behaviors/0_master.hkx` provides primary asset context: its only textual `bIsInMT == 1` expression is a wildcard transition in the `MT_LeftHandOverride` weapon state machine, not an obvious camera-bob switch. Do not force this variable to 1 without a targeted state test. `bVoiceReady` is likewise not established as a camera cause. The latest `IdleWalkingCameraStart` action remains an unproven active-state hint, and live direct input while foreground remains the next needed discriminator.
- The installed vanilla first-person behavior asset was parsed further, not just string-searched. In its own 1217-entry event table, `IdleWalkingCameraStart` is event index 1110 and `IdleWalkingCameraEnd` is 1111. The root `Master_Behavior` state machine's `Root` state (ID 0) has an event-1110 transition to state ID 49, whose state name and clip are `MT WalkingCamera` / `Animations\\MT_WalkingCamera.hkx`; that state's transition array contains event-1111 back to state ID 0. These IDs are **file-local** and state IDs are not array indices. This is a concrete candidate for an animated camera state, but the `LatestAnimation.EventName` only records the most recent action observed by our service, not the active native state; do not send an End event or mutate the graph on that basis alone.
- Source inspection of [CommonLibSSE-NG `hkbBehaviorGraph`](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/H/hkbBehaviorGraph.h), [`hkRefVariant`](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/H/hkRefVariant.h), and the installed XML reveals a probe bug: our `nativeStateId` was read from `rootGenerator` at +0x80, the serialized template, while the per-character `rootGeneratorClone` is at +0x90 and is runtime-only (`SERIALIZE_IGNORED` in the asset). Thus the previously reported state ID 0/time 0 did **not** establish that the active graph was in Root. Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently agreed on that distinction; Reviewer A could not read files outside the repo and explicitly limited its CommonLib/HKX claims, whereas Reviewer B inspected those local sources. The read-only diagnostic now models +0x90 and records both template and clone states for the player and sampled actors, guarded by graph lock, active/linked checks, a readable full state-machine range, matching template/clone vtables, and finite time/state bounds. It built and passed 168 assertions/17 tests, but was **not deployed** so it has no live result yet. The host's affected process is intentionally preserved until the requested physical-key check or a deliberate restart.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `BShkbAnimationGraph`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/B/BShkbAnimationGraph.h) embeds `hkbCharacter` at +0xC0 and also exposes a separate graph pointer at +0x208; [`hkbCharacter`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbCharacter.h) has its own `behaviorGraph` pointer at +0x58. These may name different template/instance objects. The affected host's old deployed selected-actor probe reported graph active but unlinked and template state ID 0, so the new diagnostic also records whether these two pointers match and whether the character's graph is active/linked and has a root clone. This is a read-only source change, not deployed or used to infer the active state yet. A second Reviewer A review found no blockers in the clone layout/read, while Reviewer B flagged the inherited template `Cast` guard, raw pose reads, misleading template state headline, invalid-JSON nonfinite values, and snapshot cost as issues to address before relying on broad audits. The reviewers agreed the clone must be measured, not assumed. No gameplay recovery mutation follows from this alone.
- The host's current live snapshot after the reported repeat bob shows `controls.blocked=false`, `controls.movement=true`, `moveInput=[0,0]`, first-person camera and HUD only, and a stationary player root. `remapMode=true` was **also true in the preserved pre-death snapshot**, so it does not by itself explain the change. The MQ101 dragon-attack scene is still active. The game window is not foreground and its DirectInput `keyboardEvents` counter has not advanced during the previous synthetic helper attempt. Therefore the user's report of inability to move is important, but our instrumentation cannot yet distinguish missed input from scene/animation-driven movement rejection. Requested a physical focused two-second W hold while preserving the affected process.
- The added character-vs-graph pointer diagnostic compiled into `SkyrimTogether.exe`, and the existing native tests passed (168 assertions/17 cases). This binary has **not** been deployed to either live game. The old affected host process remains intact for the focused-key discriminator; compilation is not evidence that the diagnostic works in-game.
- An old-build selected-actor sample of nearby live NPC `414971` (`DefaultMale`, 99 local pose transforms) also reports `behavior active=true`, `linked=false`, and template `Master_Behavior` state ID 0/inactive. Thus `linked=false` and template ID 0 are **not unique to the stuck host player**. The new clone reader's requirement that the graph be linked may be too restrictive for this runtime; measure pointer identity/clone presence on a restarted build before relying on a `cloneReadable=false` result.
- Independent Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviews of this bob/no-movement diagnosis agreed that `MT WalkingCamera` is a candidate rather than an established active state, and that a physical focused W hold with **during-hold** DirectInput and `MoveInputVec` sampling is the safest live discriminator. Reviewer A observed that live graph-variable deltas through `BShkbAnimationGraph+0x208` favor it being a runtime graph, but pointer identity against the character graph remains unmeasured; neither reviewer established a new camera or movement cause. Reviewer B suggested comparing new clone/pointer fields in the old live process, which is impossible because that binary has not been deployed—retain that as a future restarted-build test. Reviewer A also flagged the existing `Cast<hkbStateMachine>` template guard as a crash risk before a future deploy. No new binary was installed in this session.
- **Correction to the root-clone inference above:** [Havok's published `hkbBehaviorGraph` header](https://github.com/Bewolf2/projectanarchy/blob/master/Source/Behavior/Behavior/BehaviorGraph/hkbBehaviorGraph.h) says `m_rootGeneratorClone` points to the original root of a *cloned behavior graph*, usually the same object as `m_rootGenerator` unless the root changes. It does **not** promise the current animation state, so reading +0x90 alone cannot establish whether `MT WalkingCamera` is active. The graph's `m_activeNodes` is a separate `NodeList`; [CommonLibSSE-NG `hkbNodeInfo`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbNodeInfo.h) exposes `nodeTemplate`/`nodeClone` for each entry. A new selected-actor, read-only, bounded diagnostic samples up to 256 active-node entries and outputs up to 32 state-machine clones with name, state ID, active flag, and time; it does not send animation events or alter state. This new code compiled, but the old affected game process remains untouched and the new list has **not** been validated in-game. The earlier description of +0x90 as *the* live state should be disregarded.
- Reviewer A and Reviewer B independently reviewed the active-node reader. Reviewer A found its 0x98/0x90/+0x58/+0x60 layout consistent with the repo's older `AnimationExperiments.cpp` event loop; that local reverse-engineered loop also uses byte 0x84 as an event gate, so the new output records raw bytes 0x84/0x85 without assigning them broader semantics. Reviewer B could not access the separately installed CommonLib tree and flagged the presumed hkArray capacity word and state-machine offsets. The local [CommonLibSSE-NG `hkArray`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkArray.h), [`hkbNodeInfo`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbNodeInfo.h), [`hkbStateMachine`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbStateMachine.h), and [`hkbNode`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/H/hkbNode.h) substantiate the modeled offsets, but the older in-repo header only modeled a pointer and count, so the capacity word is now diagnostic-only, **not a gating condition**. Reviewer B's other concern is valid: filtering node vtables against the root state machine cannot enumerate state machines when the root is not a state machine; the JSON explicitly reports `stateMachineTypeScope=root-vtable` and whether that type is available. A zero count is not proof of no active state machines in those graphs. New reads still rely on `VirtualQuery` then `memcpy`, which can race a free; this is bounded, selected-actor-only, and under the graph-manager lock, but remains a runtime validation risk. No new binary has been installed.
- The revised launcher build succeeds and `TPTests.exe` still passes 168 assertions/17 cases. The affected host Skyrim process PID 50492 is still responding with the old installed binary; its DirectInput keyboard-event count remains 57 and forwarded input count 31,257 while unfocused polling continues. No focused W test has occurred yet, and no active-node runtime result exists.
- After the user performed a physical focused W press, the old live process's keyboard-event counter rose to 102 (later 106) and forwarded input polling rose above 34,000. A fresh snapshot **after** release still had player position `(19099.31,-77881.47,8462.269)`, speed 0, `MoveInputVec=[0,0]`, movement enabled, and last action `IdleWalkingCameraStart`. This proves the game received keyboard events during the interaction but does not identify their keys or sample `MoveInputVec` *during* the hold; it does not yet prove whether the movement handler accepted W. The game later went unfocused again. The prior input-delivery blocker was therefore at least partly overcome, but the movement path remains unresolved.
- An external Reviewer A brief `an internal brief` proposed a bounded causal trial: issue `player.sae IdleWalkingCameraEnd` on the **host only**, then observe bob and physical movement before touching the follower. [SKSE's vanilla console-command list](https://skse.silverlock.org/vanilla_commands.html) confirms `SendAnimEvent`/`sae` sends a named event to the selected actor's animation graph. The vanilla first-person behavior asset maps `IdleWalkingCameraEnd` to the return from `MT WalkingCamera`, but the still-active state has not been proven; this is a test, not an established fix. The host pre-test snapshot at tick `10340740` recorded the fixed position above, `controls.blocked=false`, `controls.movement=true`, `bIsInMT=0`, `bVoiceReady=0`, `bAnimationDriven=0`, `bMotionDriven=0`, `IsMoving=0`, and last action `IdleWalkingCameraStart`. The user was asked to run the console trial manually while the old process remains live. No such event has been sent by this agent yet, and the follower remains untouched.

- The user ran `player.sae IdleWalkingCameraEnd` on the stuck host. The first-person bob stopped immediately, but W still did not move the actor. It does **not** identify the script or package that initiated either restriction. The host position advanced from approximately `(19099,-77881,8462)` to `(19120,-77987,8462)` after `epc`.

Consequently all prior old-build `moveInput`, `lookInput`, `remapMode`, `povScriptMode`, `running`, and `autoMove` readings are invalid; especially `moveInput=[0,0]` is **not** proof that a physical W never entered movement handling. Handler pointers and `bBlockPlayerInput` are at separately correct offsets. The source layout was corrected and built, but not deployed to this still-running session.
- The locally installed 1.7.104 vanilla `mq101questscript.psc` defines `CameraBobStart()` as `Game.DisablePlayerControls(True, True, False, True, False, True, True, False, 0)` followed by `Game.GetPlayer().PlayIdle(IdleWalkingCameraStart)`. `CameraBobStop()` issues `IdleWalkingCameraEnd` and `Game.EnablePlayerControls(False, False, False, True, False, False, False, False, 0)`. MQ101 stage fragments independently disable/enable controls, so follower `QuestService::OnUpdate` calling `TESQuest::ScriptSetStage()` is a plausible contributor but not yet a proven source of *both* stuck effects. Directly writing stage flags would risk omitting needed vanilla side effects, so that shortcut is rejected pending trigger evidence. Installed-game script content is used only for derived identifiers, not redistributed.
- [SKSE's Papyrus/native hook patterns](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/skse64/Hooks_Papyrus.cpp) and the existing client native dispatch hook support a low-rate, name-filtered trace of `Game::DisablePlayerControls` and `Game::EnablePlayerControls`. Source now counts and logs only those calls, exposing counts and last-call time in `papyrusNativeDispatch`. This is **call telemetry, not a read of BSInputEnableManager's active layer state**; it will not catch a layer restored from a save or a different native caller. Do not claim the requested control-layer snapshot exists yet. The source build is not yet deployed or paired-tested.
- Independent local Reviewer A and Reviewer B reviews agreed this trace does **not** identify the calling script: a VM native-dispatch return address is merely the generic VM callsite, and the current stack argument is opaque. Neither reviewer validated a cause. Reviewer A additionally found an existing partial VM-vtable installation path that could overwrite a saved original with our own hook and recurse; the install now checks candidate slots before saving originals. Reviewer B flagged hot-path logging/reentrancy and camera layout brittleness. The targeted control-call logging is rare by name, but the surrounding all-native dispatch overhead still warrants measurement before a five-player performance claim. Respawn begin/end logging now records MQ101 stage, package, and actor flags to correlate with call times. A clean build and 168 unit assertions passed; paired in-game reproduction remains pending.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `BSScript::Stack`](https://ng.commonlib.dev/_stack_8h_source.html) puts its live `top` frame at +0x60; [its `StackFrame` layout](https://ng.commonlib.dev/StackFrame_8h_source.html) puts `previousFrame` at +0x08, `owningFunction` at +0x10 and instruction pointer at +0x20. `IFunction::Call` receives a **reference to `BSTSmartPointer<Stack>`**, so our hook's `apStack` ABI parameter points to that wrapper rather than directly to the stack. A bounded optional trace now reads the top and one previous frame on only the rare `Game::DisablePlayerControls`/`EnablePlayerControls` calls and logs owning object/function/source identifiers. `VirtualQuery` guards the stack/frame pointers, but this is still a new native layout and requires reviewer and live validation before deployment. The old opaque stack-pointer log was insufficient for source attribution.
- Further review corrected that design: depth 0 is the native `Game` function, while its Papyrus caller is depth 1 or deeper. The trace now walks at most five frames, records frame `self` type/raw value, rejects frames whose parent is not the live stack, checks candidate virtual-method addresses are executable before calling them, and caps full logs at 256 control calls while counters continue. This reduces but cannot eliminate native-layout risk. The local Reviewer A reviewer could not open the upstream source in its own environment; the main agent verified the offsets directly against the primary CommonLibSSE-NG pages linked above.
- [CommonLibSSE-NG `ControlMap`](https://ng.commonlib.dev/_control_map_8h_source.html) places the effective `enabledControls` mask at +0x118, with movement/looking/activate/fighting flags defined by [UserEvents](https://ng.commonlib.dev/UserEvents_8h_source.html). The project's old `BSInputEnableManager::Get()` wrapper uses AE relocation 400863, which [CommonLibSSE-NG identifies as the `ControlMap` singleton](https://ng.commonlib.dev/_offsets_8h_source.html); the wrapper name is misleading. `game_snapshot` now reads this mask separately from `PlayerControls` handler flags. This reads an effective control mask, **not** the owner/count of individual input-enable layers, and the Papyrus `IsMovementControlsEnabled` documentation warns that its return can be unreliable when movement is disabled. Validate the raw mask before/after a live lock and `epc` before using it as a recovery predicate. The code is source-only as of this note.

The source now binds a pending full snapshot to its authority epoch and cancels it on epoch change, so a new post-join capture can be requested; this diagnostic fix needs redeployment and verification. The current revision makes that epoch atomic and clears it on disconnect. Relative snapshot requests retain their shared `targetTick` (required by `Compare-TwoPcAuthority.ps1` and `Set-TwoPcPoseProbe.ps1`) but also set a monotonic local deadline; they fire on target world tick while the epoch matches, or by the wall deadline after a rebase. Explicit absolute-tick captures remain epoch-bound, and the bridge now offers `cancel_game_snapshot` plus pending status. This prevents an unreachable pre-join relative target from permanently occupying the slot without changing paired-test target selection in an established session. The common clock's plain `m_simulatedTick` cross-thread read is older technical debt and not solved by this patch; a live rebase trial must still confirm behavior.
