Held-key symptom still needs two-PC observation. |
| FRAME-21 | PollInputDevices **0x140CF95F0 / 68617** calls dispatcher **0x140CFA650 / 68655**, which reaches ProcessEvent **0x1407AD900 / 42338**. | On the first skipped poll, zero move/look and set both held-release bytes on the polling thread. |
| FRAME-05 | Calendar tick **0x140645D60 / 36291** increments DaysPassed only once per call; sleep/wait tick **0x140749560 / 40485** still counts down when that tick is suppressed. StartSleeping **0x140741370 / 40414**, StartWaiting **0x1407414C0 / 40415**, jail **0x14075A390 / 40657**, fast travel **0x140744600 / 40445**, coc **0x1401A4170 / 13328** were read. | Partly fixed: followers cannot start sleep or wait while in a party. Host skip, jail refusal with cleanup, fast-travel authority request, server date advance and follower native catch-up/DaysPassed remain. Returning early from 40657 would strand the jail state and skip inventory release, so no jail hook was installed. The existing calendar tick gate remains until those parts can be validated together in S22. |
| ITEMS-28 | Topic post **0x1403F2BB0 / 25616** sets INFO+0x3A and calls TESForm vslot +0x50 with 0x80000000, except ENAM bit 4; reset **0x1403F2E60 / 25619** clears it. | A 25616 return hook queues new said-once form IDs; the main thread maps and sends them, the server relays to party members, and receivers set +0x3A and MarkChanged. Live party replication is implemented; native reset relay and late-join replay remain. |
| QUEST-25 | Native trigger contact **0x140406D80 / 26038** calls HasKeyword vslot +0x240 on the entering reference. | Remote-player trigger filtering now tests the entering proxy. This depends on proxy keywords matching the follower and needs the paired keyword-trigger test. |

Scale in P players, A actors and B bodies: control and time hooks are O(1) per native call and add no scan. Said-once queues are event driven and drain at most 32 outgoing and 32 incoming INFOs per main frame, O(1) in P/A/B per queued item; server fan-out is O(P) per INFO. The outstanding queue can grow with unique dialogue changes during a prolonged stall, bounded by the finite INFO set in loaded plugins; five-player throughput is not measured. No new per-frame A or B scan was added.

Validation: MSVC `/Zs` passed for edited client, server and encoding translation units after forcing their existing PCHs. The new production message serializers and factories passed six assertions in one isolated protocol case. `git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check` was run. No xmake build or paired game test ran; no in-game behavior is claimed fixed. COMMON.md exempts this batch from the Reviewer A/B rule; neither assistant reviewed these changes.

## b8-carryhome follower character progress (2026-10-02)

Primary source search: [CommonLibSSE-NG `BGSSaveLoadManager.h`](https://ng.commonlib.dev/_b_g_s_save_load_manager_8h_source.html) was inspected, including `currentCharacterID` at +0xD0, the asynchronous save queue, and `Save_Impl`/`Load_Impl` signatures. It supports using the game's request queue and checking the written `.ess` afterward; the library's relocations were not copied. [Skyrim-MCP](https://github.com/maksidze/Skyrim-MCP) advertises a character blueprint, but no source implementation of its transfer path was verified, so it was not adopted. [Familiar Faces](https://github.com/Verteiron/FamiliarFaces) duplicates a character into another save but explicitly does not transfer inventory; it was rejected for the owner's carry-home requirement. The existing `CharacterSnapshots::Capture`/`Apply` source was inspected and reused: Capture excludes source-world quest item instances, and Apply changes character fields rather than quest/world state.

Native 1.7.104 evidence read with `sk.py fn` before new engine calls:

| VA / Address Library ID | Finding and decision |
|---|---|
| `0x140625850 / 35769` | `BGSSaveLoadManager::QueueSaveLoadTask` creates a save/load request in its queue. The carry-home import requests `0xF0000080` only from the main-loop quiet window, as the existing checkpoint path does. |
| `0x1406227F0 / 35727` | `Save_Impl` has separate kind 2 manual, 3 auto, 4 quick and 5 request-save handling; it rejects saving in unsafe game states. The hook observes successful saves, suppresses ordinary follower saves during the party, and checks a finished new `.ess` before retiring the carry-home file. |
| `0x140623DA0 / 35749` | Save name generation uses `Save` for request-save kind 5, a save counter and a time field, rather than `Autosave` or `Quicksave`. The import still verifies a distinct new file in the original playthrough before retirement. |
| `0x140622F30 / 35728` | `Load_Impl` queues a load by file name. The after-load step uses that name and the load-complete callback to compare the carry-home timestamp with the loaded ordinary save. |
| `0x140A253E0 / 55490` | `Game.QuitToMainMenu` starts the fade-to-title callback. A follower capture is requested before that callback can unload the character. |
| `0x140991A30 / 52933` | `SendHUDMessage::ShowHUDMessage` queues a HUD message; used only after the new save is observed and the carry-home record is retired. |

No protocol fields changed. The carry-home path is local to each follower and indexed by the ordinary save playthrough key. `SavedCharacters` checks one carry-home record per ordinary character during an explicit lobby scan; no new per-frame directory scan is added. Main-loop import polling checks one pending record and at most the save folder after a requested save. CPU cost is O(1) in party players P, actors A and bodies B per frame except those event-triggered file operations. Five-player performance and two-PC correctness are not yet measured.

### b8 carry-home adversarial fix, round 1 (2026-10-02)

Primary source rechecked: [CommonLibSSE-NG `BGSSaveLoadManager.cpp`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/B/BGSSaveLoadManager.cpp) forwards the optional file name to `Save_Impl` and calls `Load_Impl` with a file name. The 1.7.104 executable corpus shows `Save_Impl` **0x1406227F0 / ID 35727** calls save naming **0x140623DA0 / ID 35749** when its name argument is null. The naming function writes a wide filename into the supplied buffer; kind 5 uses the `Save` stem. `Save_Impl` then creates a save operation request, so its true return and the hook's serial do not establish disk completion. Queued task **0x140625850 / ID 35769** feeds the manager queue; its consumer **0x140625B90** calls `Save_Impl` for save tasks. The fix records the generated filename at 35749 for the pending kind-5 import request and rejects a name already present on disk. It then checks only that `.ess`: nonzero size, modification after request, unchanged size for at least 500 ms, readable header and matching ordinary playthrough key. A newest-save time-window scan was rejected because a different save can satisfy it. The generated filename is only accepted on the recorded main-loop thread; if it cannot be observed, the carry record stays unretired after timeout. This is source logic, not an observed game result.

The native load path **0x140622F30 / ID 35728** is followed by the existing `LoadGame` result hook (**0x140618970 / ID 35600**) in `AchievementsWithMods.cpp`. A false handler result now clears the pending name/state. The Loading Menu is only a wait condition; it cannot promote an unconfirmed load. `Load_Impl` refusal also clears that state, so a refused load cannot trigger this path's carry apply or missing `.snap` capture. A new load cancels the previous import confirmation. `SSC_*` paths now use `ssc:name:<player>` keys, disjoint from legacy own-save `name:<player>` and ordinary hex keys; `SavedCharacters` applies the same key to `.ess` and `.snap`, so an SSC snapshot cannot be selected as an own-save fallback.

Cost correction: while a checkpoint is pending, `Poll` visits the S entries in the save folder at most once every 500 ms, **O(S) per scan** and **O(1) per intervening frame**. Import confirmation stats one exact path per frame, **O(1)** in save count S and players P; it performs one header read when stable. The existing snapshot-after-save path still performs one O(S) scan per completed save, and explicit lobby scans remain O(S). Four followers in a five-player party may each perform their own checkpoint scan at that bounded rate. No five-player profile, changed-build run, or paired in-game result exists for this fix.

Independent review: Reviewer A completed a read-only review and identified four high-severity source issues. A one-player auto-host party was incorrectly excluding solo import; the import now excludes only a started shared session or a party with another player. A queued write could run after the next load; it now carries a load generation and is attempted once. Follower checkpoint staging saves remained ordinary and could be chosen by solo Continue; the follower path now moves the finished staging `.ess`, `.skse` and `.snap` into `SSC_` rather than copying, with the `.ess` moved last. A lobby choice alone did not prove the chosen character was in the shared world; the chosen snapshot is now applied after that world loads, and carry-home is enabled only after a successful apply. Reviewer A also noted promotion, stale drop-in identity, failed loads and file sharing; the paths now write for promoted followers, clear identity on new drop-in, require successful `LoadGame`, and retry an atomic replace after flushing the temp file. Reviewer B was started independently but its run was limited to a partial inspection of `CheckpointSaves.cpp` and its header; it confirmed the quit timing concern and suggested stricter save acceptance, but did not review the whole batch. Its suggestion to defer the quit write even when on the main loop was rejected: the write is a local snapshot file, not an engine `Save_Impl` call, and the character must be captured before menu teardown. An off-thread quit is deferred to the main-loop window. Its unrelated findings about the inherited death-reload guard, checkpoint ID collisions and `WriteReceived` were not changed here because they predate this batch and need separate validation. Reviewer agreement is not treated as game validation.

Validation so far: cached-flag MSVC `/Zs` passed for the seven edited client translation units, with no xmake build. `git diff --check` passed. The changed build has not been exercised in a two-PC game, so no in-game result is claimed. The main risk is the first shared-world apply timing and whether the native request save always produces a distinct ordinary save under every menu/game state; the carry record remains available if that save is not observed. The existing follower checkpoint mechanism still creates a temporary ordinary `Save*` in the shared world's playthrough before the completed file is moved to `SSC_*`; no follower home playthrough save is used or overwritten. A crash during that short staging window can leave that temporary save in the picker. A direct named `SSC_*` save would avoid the window but would change the proven checkpoint engine path and needs a paired test before adoption.

### b9 checkpoint player positions (2026-10-02)

Historical first-pass notes below are superseded by the ov-b9 revision 1 entry. In particular, UpdateEvent is not the main thread, player MoveTo is queued, and loading an exterior cell record does not establish a party loading barrier. Do not use those first-pass claims as validation.

Primary source search and inspection: [CommonLibSSE-NG `TESObjectREFR.cpp`](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) calls `MoveTo_Impl` with the target cell, worldspace, position and rotation; this supports using the existing mod `TESObjectREFR::MoveTo` wrapper for a one-time join placement on the main frame. [skymp `MpActor.cpp`](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/master/skymp5-server/cpp/server_guest_lib/MpActor.cpp) keeps position, angle and a plugin-aware cell/world descriptor in `LocationalData`, and updates those with a teleport. We adopted its separation of location identity from coordinates, but not its always-authoritative teleport: this fork loads each player's own checkpoint on Continue and uses a cell barrier for party travel. The existing fork `CharacterService::MoveActorNow` loads exterior cells via `TESWorldSpace::LoadCell`; b9 reuses that exterior load path and refuses unloaded or mismatched interiors/worldspaces. A plain raw form ID in the roster was rejected because load orders can differ.

The 1.7.104 executable corpus confirms `TESObjectREFR::MoveTo_Impl` at **0x140A5D370 / Address Library ID 56626** calls `TESObjectREFR::SetPosition`, then `FUN_1402F1E50` (**0x1402F1E50 / ID 19799**) for cell/world placement. `TESWorldSpace::LoadCell` is **0x14030D0E0 / ID 20460** and reads or loads the exterior grid cell before returning it. `BGSSaveLoadManager::Save_Impl` remains **0x1406227F0 / ID 35727**; b9 observes its existing checkpoint event and does not call it. The mod's `MoveTo` wrapper is used only from `DropInService::OnUpdate` on the main thread after the host save loads and the chosen character applies. A roster destination in another worldspace or unattached interior falls back to the existing placement beside the leader. The heading is applied after the move. Downed state is stored; b9 does not alter the game's loaded life state.

The roster is one report per party member per leader checkpoint. Each report contains the Steam account ID reported at authentication, own-save playthrough key and character name, plugin-aware `GameId` worldspace and cell, XYZ, heading and downed status. The server binds reports to the authenticated connection's reported Steam ID, stores them in `checkpoint_roster` in the campaign SQLite ledger, and sends a full roster snapshot to the leader for an atomic `SSC_<id>.roster` JSON sidecar. A drop-in join looks up the newest matching Steam ID, key and name, including a checkpoint before the player's absence, and passes that position on the save's `Done` message. A different character cannot match. The wire marker is bumped to `SSM/2` because authentication and drop-in packet layouts changed while the Git commit hash is unchanged in this uncommitted tree. The Steam ID is a client claim carried over the existing direct-IP transport; the server does not independently verify Steam ownership. Steam's shipped Skyrim DLL exports `SteamAPI_SteamUser_v021` and `SteamAPI_ISteamUser_GetSteamID`; v023 and v022 were absent, so the client accessor tries all three in that order.

`PartyService::OnNotifyLeaderControl` previously gathered followers to the leader on the first free-control notice, even after loading their own Continue checkpoint. Continue now skips that gather. CharacterService's post-load `ReplayToPlayer` spawns remote actor copies and does not move the local `PlayerCharacter`.

Cost: report and ledger write are O(1) per member at save time; sending each growing full roster to the leader is O(P�) data and work per checkpoint, bounded to 16 records on the wire (five players: at most 15 serialized records across five notifications). Character lookup scans S files in the save directory per checkpoint on that PC and keeps at most 200 character summaries, not per frame. Drop-in lookup uses the `(steam_id,character_key,saved_at_ms)` SQLite index; placement loads at most one exterior cell. No new per-frame actor, body or cell scan was added. Five-player CPU, memory and network measurements and paired in-game observations have not yet been made.

Verification: isolated MSVC `/Zs` checks passed for the edited client, server, campaign and encoding test translation units; isolated protocol tests passed 17 assertions in 3 cases, and campaign roster persistence/matching passed 7 assertions. The additive SQLite schema check passed 6 cases. No xmake build, deployment or changed-build two-PC test was run. Required paired observation: Rex walks away from the leader within one cell, leader saves, `roster_status` on leader shows both identities and Rex's coordinates, Rex leaves and rejoins with the same character, then her position/heading is compared to the roster and the leader. Repeat with a different character and with an unreachable cell to confirm the leader fallback, and Continue Together to confirm no gather. Reviewer A/B review was not performed for this batch: the task-specific `C:\Tools\skyrim_re\agent\COMMON.md` explicitly exempts this agent from that rule and says Reviewer B cannot start in this sandbox. Their prior b8 reviews do not cover b9.

### ov-b9 revision 1: reviewer resolution and native placement conclusion (2026-10-02)

Conclusion: initial b9 had real thread, heading, identity and cell-boundary defects. Revision 1 corrects those source paths. A matching player can be placed at saved coordinates and heading within the attached current exterior or interior cell. A different cell/worldspace falls back at the host's loaded location; an individual return never initiates party travel. Continue retains each PC's own save position. These are high-confidence source conclusions, not an observed changed-build game result. The paired acceptance remains unverified. No xmake build, deployment, commit or live probe was performed.

Primary-source research was repeated before editing. Searched and inspected [CommonLibSSE-NG TESObjectREFR.cpp](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp), including MoveTo_Impl's relocation ID 56626. Inspected local C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG/include/RE/B/BGSSaveLoadManager.h:167, which names currentCharacterID at +0xD0 (remote header fetch failed; local primary source was available). The earlier skymp plugin-aware location representation remains the reason for storing GameId plus coordinates. Rejected MoveTo as a synchronous player-placement primitive after inspecting its native implementation. Rejected bare CharacterService::MoveActorNow/LoadCell as proof of the party barrier: DoorVoteService's production barrier uses voted door activation; its separate RequestTestCell is a leader-only, whole-party test operation, not individual drop-in travel.

Native corpus, read with sk.cmd fn before the edits:

| Address Library ID / VA | Finding and decision |
| --- | --- |
| 56626 / 0x140A5D370 | MoveTo_Impl tests the player singleton separately. Nonactors can disable/enable; nonplayer actors use ForceState. The PLAYER builds a transition with supplied position/heading and calls 40442 / 0x140743E50. The former wrapper passed the old heading, then SetRotation changed it before the queued transition could reapply it. The initial report described the wrong branch. |
| 37309 / 0x1406770A0 | Actor virtual SetPosition (A9) calls reference SetPosition and, with true, updates controller and root translation. Adopt for one current-cell placement. |
| 19799 / 0x1402F1E50 | Reconciles cell/world; null interior plus world derives exterior cell from floor(position/4096). For interiors pass current interior and null world. This can process unloaded references, so it is not a substitute for reachability guards. |
| 19826 / 0x1402F4360 | Havok-moved path uses SetPosition and reconciliation (ref,nullptr,worldspace) when destination exists. Adopt this call shape after current-cell identity/grid checks; no raw coordinate-only mutation. |
| 20460 / 0x14030D0E0 | Reads/loads exterior cell records through IOManager/TESFile. A returned record is not proof of attachment or a party load barrier. Removed b9's use; no new LoadCell remains in DropInService. |
| 36564 / 0x140658870 | Existing HookMainLoop calls World::UpdateMainFrame before the native main loop. Added one service drain there. SkyrimVM64.cpp:35-52 and World.cpp:262-268 show UpdateEvent instead comes from the VM update path. |
| 35749 / 0x140623DA0 | Save-name generator reads manager+0xD0 for the eight-digit hexadecimal playthrough field. CurrentCharacterKey now uses it after an explicitly selected carry-home key, rather than the newest unrelated save. The decompiler renders narrow %08X bytes as wide glyphs; surrounding format and primary-source member agree. |

DropInService now queues one mutex-protected position copy, attempt and epoch, drains it on the main frame, then lets the VM send Loaded only after a result. It checks connection, epoch, attached world, menus and pending door vote; cancellation/timeout fails the join. Same-cell interior placement updates XYZ/heading and is not a no-op unless coordinates already match. A different interior is deliberately unreachable for this individual path: a roster supplies no approved door context. Fallback stays inside the exterior grid; at an edge it keeps the host spot. The bridge uses the same drain and checks current identity. PartyService retains this PC's latest reported entry for the bridge. A restored drop-in suppresses its later leader-gather for that epoch; Continue's existing b9 gather exclusion remains. Fresh New Game intro gather is retained.

FindLatestRoster and incoming Done use Steam ID plus playthrough key. Display name remains metadata and may differ after rename. Equal-millisecond ledger entries use rowid as a deterministic tiebreak. Rename matching is tested across reopening SQLite. Name-only legacy keys cannot supply rename-stable identity; ordinary native save keys are the supported stable identity.

The cost objection is disproved by server PartyService.cpp:500-515: every report rereads/sends the growing full roster ONLY to the leader. There are P reports and P notifications, not P squared messages or an all-member broadcast. They serialize P(P+1)/2 outgoing roster records plus P incoming records: 15+5 records in 10 messages at five players; 55+10 in 20 at ten. Thus roster serialization/read/write volume is O(P squared) once per checkpoint (wire cap 16). New idle-frame work is one bounded queue check, with no scan over actors A or bodies B. CurrentCharacterKey is now O(1); snapshot selection retains the save-directory scan at join time. Five/ten wire tests are correctness tests, not performance measurements.

AuthenticationRequest.cpp:7,28 does carry SteamId, contrary to verify0's read, but no roster XYZ/key/name. TransportService.cpp:195 supplies it and GameServer.cpp:1172 stores it. Correct reporting: authentication binds the account claim; CheckpointRoster and DropIn Done carry full position. Direct IP still does not verify Steam ownership.

Wire provenance: b9.log:168050-168052 records the original b9 diff SSM/1 to SSM/2; git show 25fa030d:Code/common/BuildInfo.h.in predates either marker. SteamLobbyService.cpp:769 rejects unequal complete markers before joining; GameServer.cpp:1021 rejects a decoded unequal Version. Identical SSM/2 release/commit/layouts are compatible; SSM/1 and official Together are unsupported. Old direct-IP auth layout can fail decoding before Version, so a friendly version error is not guaranteed. Revision 1 changes semantics, not packet layout. Coordinator must regenerate BuildInfo/deploy matching binaries: uncommitted source revisions otherwise share a marker.

Validation repeated: 21 assertions in 4 checkpoint/protocol cases, including five/ten interior players; 10 assertions in reopened-ledger roster case; 6 schema tests. Isolated MSVC /Zs passed b9 client/server/campaign/protocol units, then World, DropInService, PartyService and CheckpointSaves again after final edits. Evidence: C:/Tools/skyrim_re/agent/overnight/b9-r1-protocol.log, b9-r1-campaign.log, b9-r1-syntax.log, b9-r1-final-check.log and b9-r1-final-check.py. Toolchain setup prints a nonfatal missing vswhere warning, but compiler/linker/tests succeed; existing u8path deprecation remains. git diff --check passed. No new diagnostic probe was used.

Regression scope: compared relevant hunks against 25fa030d; DropInService does not exist at that commit. This round changes b9 paths plus one World drain call, leaving corpse ragdoll, execution head/body, cart steering, trigger gate and door-vote implementation untouched. This is a source audit, not paired regression proof. Server CharacterService::ReplayToPlayer:908 emits remote spawns, not local-player teleport. Continue guard is client PartyService.cpp:1197. No broad claim is made about quest/script-driven movement after load.

Remaining acceptance/risks: coordinator's matching build must be exercised on both PCs, with authorized diagnostics armed, observing saved/returned XYZ and heading in one interior and one exterior; repeat after rename, with a different playthrough, through Continue, and at unavailable destination (fallback). Capture past the next leader-free-control heartbeat. Ledger restart persistence is tested by reopen, not all game identity/mod-map transitions. Sidecar is an export, not an importer to reconstruct a lost database. roster_status reads the leader's current sidecar; a follower without it shows an empty players array. roster_place_now uses an in-memory own checkpoint report or received return entry, so after process restart it needs a save/join first. Capture precedes completion of a queued follower save, so exact equality with every asynchronous save frame is not demonstrated. These limitations prevent claiming the entire original acceptance is complete. COMMON exempts this batch from additional Reviewer A/B launches; no independent review of this revision is claimed.

## 2026-10-02: b10-skipcreator, follower keeps the chosen character in New Game Together

**Historical candidate, refuted.** The no-early-level, rebuild-completion, epoch-retention and constant-cost claims below are superseded by the ov-b10 round-1 correction at the end of this file. This entry is not acceptance evidence.

Research before implementation:
- Read [CommonLibSSE-NG `RaceSexMenu.h`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/R/RaceSexMenu.h), [`UIMessageQueue.cpp`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/U/UIMessageQueue.cpp), and [`UserEvents.h`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/U/UserEvents.h), plus the corresponding local source in `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG`. They establish a paused native RaceSex menu, its named show/hide queue, and the fighting control bit. Adopted the existing native close/release path and the native control query. Rejected suppressing the menu's show message outright because that would skip its close/camera cleanup without evidence.
- Searched prior open-source Skyrim/SKSE mods and inspected `C:/Tools/ref/caelvanost/TiltedEvolution/Code/client` for creator hooks. That older multiplayer source has no `ShowRaceMenu` or `RaceSex Menu` implementation to reuse. Alternate-start mods bypass MQ101 entirely, so their quest skip is inappropriate for a party that still plays the vanilla intro together.
- Read the installed MQ101 PEX decompilation at `C:/Tools/ref/mq101-audit/psc/qf_mq101_0003372b.psc` and its `sf_mq101scene4_000cd68e.psc` (the locally installed `Skyrim.esm` stage mapping was already recorded in `docs/reverse-engineering/INTRO_MQ101_MULTIPLAYER.md`). MQ101 stage 75 `Fragment_13` calls `Game.ShowRaceMenu()`. Scene 4 `Fragment_33` sets stage 80; stage 80 `Fragment_11` exits chargen and adds race spells. Stage 160 `Fragment_316` releases the outdoor restraint but leaves fighting unavailable. In the Keep, stage 240 `Fragment_74` enables all controls, shows first-person geometry, and removes `PrisonerCuffsPlayer`; unlike stage 160, this is the actual hands-untied/equipment handoff. The fragment has no latent wait. An earlier stage-10 fragment strips all items, so restoring before stage 240 is wrong.

Executable contracts read before the change (SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104): `RaceSexMenu::ProcessMessage` VA `0x140968010`, ID **52345**, handles native creator callbacks and updates the player while the menu is open; its begin-close VA `0x14096B7D0`, ID **52388**, invokes `FadeOut`, stores the camera, and marks the menu closing. `UIMessageQueue::AddMessage` VA `0x1401B47F0`, ID **13631**, queues its final hide. `Papyrus_Game_EnablePlayerControls` VA `0x140A23FC0`, ID **55455**, calls `ControlMap::ToggleControls` VA `0x140CEFAA0`, ID **68545**; `ControlMap::GetControlsState` VA `0x140CEFB80`, ID **68548**, reads the live mask at `+0x120`, including fighting bit `0x40`. `Papyrus_Actor_SetRestrained` VA `0x140A02B20` has no Address Library ID in this corpus; it calls the actor restraint implementation and is used by stage 160, not the Keep inventory handoff. The look path uses `Actor::SwitchRace` VA `0x1406AD970`, ID **37925**, and `Actor::DoReset3D` VA `0x140739A40`, ID **40255**. The `Game.ShowRaceMenu` Papyrus native is registered in the corpus binder at VA `0x140A25A70` but its implementation has no mapped VA/ID, so the installed PEX and the menu functions are the evidence for the opening path.

Decision and implementation candidate: the host retains the existing creator path. Each follower freezes its own lobby choice by party epoch on the main thread. Only a named, readable choice opts into skipping; its native RaceSex menu is opened by MQ101 then closed via CreatorTogether's forced normal begin-close path. The selected snapshot's name, race, sex, face, and tints are applied after menu teardown, with no inventory or level change, and gameplay readiness waits for the appearance rebuild. The selected full `.snap` is applied without a second appearance rebuild after stage 240 is done, fighting control is restored, and a subsequent main frame confirms that the installed MQ101 prisoner cuffs form (`0010E039`) is absent. The cuffs read uses `TESObjectREFR::GetItemCountInInventory`, polled at most every 250 ms for five seconds; a missing unbind side effect withholds the restore. The existing full apply preserves the shared world's quest items and imports only the character snapshot's personal items; worn stacks are re-equipped by the existing inventory path. An older save without `.snap` supplies look and items from `SaveLook`, but not a trustworthy full skills/perks/spells/words record; its items-only restore is explicitly reported as partial. If its item reader found no stacks, Keep items are left in place rather than stripping them. This preserves vanilla prisoner state through the outdoor intro. Work is phase driven with a single quest stage and control read per local main frame while awaiting unbinding: O(1) per PC, O(P) across a party. After stage 240, at most 21 inventory lookups cost O(I) each for local inventory size I, with no actor scan or new network message. Existing appearance and ready messages communicate this member's Done state to the server.

`skipcreator_status` reports skip, chosen name, phase, full-state availability, and epoch. Pending two-PC acceptance: host on PRISONER must see the full creator; follower Rex on a named `.snap` must pass stage 75 without an editable creator, display her exact name/race/sex/face/tints on both PCs, retain prisoner gear through stage 160, then recover her worn gear, gold, level/XP, skills, perks, spells, shouts, and words after stage 240/cuff removal. Check quest items from Rex's former world are absent. Check both ready packets and stage 80/240 progression. The changed build has **not** been built or exercised in game in this batch, so no in-game fix or five-player performance target is claimed. `C:/Tools/skyrim_re/agent/COMMON.md` explicitly exempts this batch agent from Reviewer A/B review; no independent review is claimed.


## b18-equipment source audit and partial funnel change (2026-10-02)

Primary-source research: searched open-source Skyrim/SKSE equipment replication. [CommonLibSSE-NG ActorEquipManager.cpp](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/ActorEquipManager.cpp) was read: its EquipObject/UnequipObject wrappers pass the chosen ExtraDataList and queue/apply flags unchanged. Adopted the current fork's apply-function hooks and exact-list path; a null-list-only receiver was rejected. [CommonLibSSE-NG InventoryChanges.h](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/I/InventoryChanges.h) and its local `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG/src/RE/I/InventoryChanges.cpp` were read for owner at +0x08, suppression byte at +0x19, and RemoveItem's signature. Searched TiltedEvolution and skymp; no source path was adopted from those projects for this funnel.

The cited 1.7.104 native functions were re-read with `sk.py fn id:<ID>`: equip apply 0x1406B2570 / ID 38001 and unequip apply 0x1406B2E50 / ID 38002; inventory AddItem 0x14022C310 / ID 16053 and AddWorldReference 0x14022B990 / ID 16052; RemoveItem 0x14022DA70 / ID 16059; stack selection 0x140231280 / ID 16066; EquipObject 0x1406DC310 / ID 38894; worn reset 0x1403C4440 / ID 418622 and worn-parts refresh 0x1402EEAA0 / ID 19743. The AddItem decompilation reads its owner from InventoryChanges+0x08, checks +0x19, accepts item, list, count and old owner, and sends a container change; its extra-list input may be consumed/merged. Therefore the new hook serializes the input before calling native and reports after it returns. It skips replica overrides and direct calls already represented by an outer add/pickup hook on the same thread. No new address was guessed.

Source audit against MOD_FIXES.md: ITEMS-04 is already covered in EquipManager.cpp by apply hooks and removal of worker sends. ITEMS-02 is already covered in TESObjectREFR::AddOrRemoveItem by an unworn add and fingerprint re-find. ITEMS-03 has stack-bearing equip messages and exact weapon guard repair, but unequip still chooses the first worn list for the form and an ambiguous owner-side equip cannot identify the exact stack; it remains partial. ITEMS-06 has return hooks on 418622/19743, a bounded dirty queue, exact-list guard repair and NpcLootService clearing received snapshots after load so queries restart; paired game validation remains pending. ITEMS-09 remains partial: this batch adds ID 16053 capture only. ID 16052 world-reference adds, ID 16059 removals, the soul-gem ExtraSoul split, and complete outfit/load suppression remain to be implemented after validating funnel argument lifetimes and event ordering.

Cost: new add capture is O(1) in players P, actors A and bodies B per native call, plus the existing extra-list serialization cost for that changed stack. It introduces no frame loop or actor scan. Outer-hook depth is thread_local; jobs cannot duplicate a nested outer add. Five-player throughput and two-PC correctness are unmeasured. No xmake build or changed-build in-game run was performed, so none of the owner-visible symptoms is claimed resolved.
`nValidation: isolated MSVC /Zs checks passed for TESObjectREFR.cpp and Actor.cpp using the original read-only dependency manifests and Libraries; git diff --check passed. MSVC emitted only the pre-existing signed/unsigned warning at TESObjectREFR.cpp:799. The setup printed a nonfatal missing vswhere warning. No protocol wire format changed, so no protocol test was added. COMMON.md explicitly exempts this batch from Reviewer A/B; neither reviewed this change.

(2026-10-02 coordinator: the ov-b18 ID 16053 InventoryChanges::AddItem hook above was NOT merged: it runs on JobThread workers and during cell-load loot fills; it waits for MTD stage 1e (ITEMS-05) and an authority/flood check. ITEMS-02, 04, 06 were found already fixed; ITEMS-03 partly.)

## b20-doors source research (2026-10-02)

Rechecked ACTOR-02, QUEST-19, QUEST-20 and QUEST-23 against the current private batch copy and `SkyrimAtlas/for-coop/MOD_FIXES.md`. The prior b4 actor edits already blocked remote NPC activation in `HookActivate`, skipped remote pending-door activation in `HookRunPendingDoor`, dropped remote load-door replay in `ObjectService::OnActivateNotify`, and cleared a stale door opener before world-state apply. This batch keeps the quest-door vote, first-player trigger and barrier paths as they were. It closes the unresolved-handle corner in ACTOR-02, adds event-driven replica fade cleanup for QUEST-20, and observes the actual action-bit transition for QUEST-23. QUEST-19 required no additional replay policy change.

Primary open-source code searched and inspected: [CommonLibSSE-NG `TESObjectREFR.cpp`](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/src/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.cpp) maps `ActivateRef` to AE relocation 19796 and exposes reference/teleport methods. Its native boundary and handle-lifetime pattern were adopted, while its wrappers were not taken as proof of door travel semantics. [CommonLibSSE-NG `BGSOpenCloseForm.h`](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/B/BGSOpenCloseForm.h) declares `GetOpenState` and `SetOpenState`; the declaration was useful for the API shape, but cannot explain the return-value behavior, which was taken from the exact executable. The [TiltedEvolution-rwf `ObjectService.cpp`](https://github.com/rfortier/TiltedEvolution-rwf/blob/master/Code/client/Services/Generic/ObjectService.cpp) source replays a received door activation using the remote actor after comparing pre-open states; that approach was rejected for load doors because the native activates/travels that actor. No reference mod supplied a complete replica fade-state reset.

Exact `SkyrimSE.exe` 1.7.104 functions checked with `sk.py fn id:<ID>` (the `fn` parser needs `id:` rather than a bare decimal ID):

| Item | Function VA / Address Library ID | Finding and decision |
|---|---|---|
| ACTOR-02 | `0x1406F6E40 / 39408`, `0x1406F6E10 / 39407`, `0x140786780 / 41412`, `0x1406A81D0 / 37878`, `0x1407CE180 / 42866`, `0x1402F11B0 / 19796`, process demotions `0x140696060 / 37624`, `0x1406959F0 / 37623`, `0x1406951A0 / 37622`, package tick `0x140704C70 / 39637` | Native runner resolves High+0xD8 before clearing it and can activate a non-player. The setter dereferences a handle pointer. The remote branch now clears High+0xD8 and +0x470 before trying to resolve the door, including an unresolved handle. The activation hook continues to block the idle/animation direct paths. |
| QUEST-19 | `0x140283210 / 17922`, `0x140301090 / 20216`, `0x1406FE1F0 / 39457` | A load door can request fade state 4 and later move/activate its actor. The existing received-message guard checks Teleport extra data and skips replay with any remote actor, including a proxy on the host. No event-only TESActivateEvent emulation was added: the cited recipe marks it inferred and untried. |
| QUEST-20 | `0x1406FE0B0 / 39454`, `0x1406FE050 / 39453`, `0x1406FE1F0 / 39457`, `0x1406FE650 / 39460`, `0x1406F6E10 / 39407`, `0x1406F6E40 / 39408` | RequestFade with a trigger and teleport=true stores fade state 4 and the trigger; UpdateFade later reactivates it. FadeIn clears state 4 and trigger, while SetPendingDoor needs a pointer to a copied null handle. 39460 resets the state without clearing the trigger, so it was rejected. The new request hook declines only door-triggered teleport fades for remote non-player actors; door events queue an ID, and the X.00 main-thread drain uses FadeIn only for state 4 and clears the pending-door fields. The new hook does no scene/engine calls on the worker. An already stale state 4 without a subsequent intercepted door event or ownership/spawn event remains unhandled; a lifecycle dirty event is needed for full coverage. |
| QUEST-23 | `0x1401D9EB0 / 14287`, `0x1401DA110 / 14288`, `0x1402E7A20 / 19682`, `0x140169130 / 11801`, `0x140169170 / 11802`, door HandleOpen `0x140283F00 / 17926`, Papyrus Lock `0x140A46370 / 56198`, ReferenceReset `0x1402D2CB0 / 19286` | SetOpenState can flip the action bit and return false, and a true return can mean a queued task. Papyrus Lock can call it to snap a door closed. HookWorldOpen now publishes an immediate bit change regardless of return and waits for an observed transition after a queued success. The already present Apply path reads open state, respects a local opener while moving, clears extra 0x68 with 11802 and a null-handle copy, and preserves the no-restart check. Pending checks expire after one second; a task completing later would need a later baseline or native call to publish. |

Scale with P players, A replica actors and B bodies: the new activation/fade hooks and enqueue operations cost expected O(1) per native door event; pending replica storage is O(A) distinct actor IDs. Each X.00 frame drains at most 32 changed replica IDs and 32 queued door-open checks, O(min(32,A) + min(32,D)) work, where D is pending doors, with no new full actor, body or per-player scan and no packet schema change. These are bounds, not measured five-player performance. The queued open checks read only the current door by form ID and expire after one second. Worker hooks enqueue immutable IDs; native fade/pending-door cleanup runs before `Main::Update` starts the frame's job lists.

MSVC `/Zs` passed for `TESObjectREFR.cpp`, `ObjectService.cpp` and `SkyrimVM64.cpp` with the original read-only dependency manifests and Libraries, and source/includes from this batch copy. The compiler emitted an existing TESObjectREFR.cpp signedness warning; vcvars printed a nonfatal missing-vswhere warning. `git diff --check` passed. No xmake build, plugin link, two-PC run or five-player profile was performed. Test S07 with a replica near an opened load door and Ralof/Hadvar leaving Helgen keep; assert no replica `ActivateRequest`, second travel or local fade. Test S12 with a follower using a free load door; its proxy must not travel locally on the host. For QUEST-23, script a door closed or locked while unloaded and later attach it on the follower; also open the same door locally while a host update arrives and confirm the local player does not travel because of a stale opener. These are required observations, not reported successes. COMMON.md exempts this batch from Reviewer A/B; neither reviewer was invoked.

# b11 foundation research (2026-10-02)

Before changing the party epoch, opcode table, or Papyrus call wrapper, I inspected the original [TiltedEvolution source](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution) in the local `C:/Tools/ref/TiltedEvolution-upstream` snapshot: `Code/server/Services/PartyService.cpp`, `Code/client/SkyrimVM64.cpp`, `Code/client/Services/PapyrusService.h`, and `Code/encoding/Opcodes.h`. Its party leader is reassigned without a generation and its Papyrus wrapper passes stack ID 0. Those are useful compatibility baselines but cannot distinguish a leader that returns within one StartEpoch or safely call latent natives. This batch adds a server-owned generation and uses `UINT32_MAX` for wrapper calls. The existing `LatentPapyrusFunction` and explicit calls in this fork already use that value. I also inspected [PrismaUI's MainThreadQueue source](https://github.com/PrismaUI-SKSE/framework/blob/main/src/Utils/MainThreadQueue.cpp), which swaps a mutex-protected vector into a main-thread pump. It is a useful thread-affinity example but was not adopted for M0: the integrated design needs bounded lanes, per-key order, and epoch-based stale-record rejection. No M0 queue or detour was installed in this batch.

Offline executable checks: `sk.py fn 0x1401A6540` (Address Library 13363) shows the loading byte `0x14219E5F6` set on start and cleared on end, but also early returns; that is why an M0 LoadSerial hook must use edges rather than every call. `sk.py fn 0x140658870` (36564) and `sk.py fn 0x1409D7450` (53926) confirm the main-loop and VM-update entry points. `sk.py fn 0x1414A9C10` (N104651) shows the native call receives a stack ID and has a separate latent path; the wrapper's zero ID was rejected in favor of `UINT32_MAX`. SkyrimAtlas `papyrus/natives.tsv` identifies 48 latent binders; the debug assertion checks their names, and the two existing GameTest AddItem calls use explicit stack IDs. `sk.py fn 0x140646BB0` (36317) confirms the resync entry point, but the post-load consumer detour remains for stage 0c. No diagnostic counter or live probe was added, and no in-game result is claimed.

Opcode baseline in this private copy before reservation: `kRequestCheckpointRoster=85`, `kNotifyCheckpointRoster=83`. C1-C12 now occupy 86-97 and S1-S14 occupy 84-97, leaving both Max sentinels at 98. These are reserved names only; no factory or handler dispatch is registered before each message's owning stage. `Code/tests/foundation_protocol.cpp` locks the numbers and round-trips the new `NotifyPartyInfo` generation. The SP trailing blocks and their old/new reader checks belong to stage 3d, so X-T11 is only partially enabled here. At five or ten players, the epoch adds four bytes to each `NotifyPartyInfo` recipient: 20 or 40 bytes per broadcast, O(P); the opcode reservation has no per-frame or per-message cost, and the stack-ID change is O(1) per mod Papyrus call. These are estimates, not measured throughput.

Validation in this batch: an isolated MSVC build linked the edited `NotifyPartyInfo.cpp` and `ServerMessageFactory.cpp` with the new test; `[encoding.foundation]` passed 35 assertions in two cases. `/Zs` syntax checks passed the edited client/server PartyService translation units, PapyrusService, GameTestService and the new test. The compiler environment emitted a nonfatal `vswhere.exe` warning. `git diff --check` passed. This is protocol/source validation only; no changed client/server build or paired in-game run was performed.


## b11b-slots stage 0c source research (2026-10-02)

Primary open-source implementations inspected before this cut:

* [TiltedEvolution `SkyrimVM64.cpp`](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/client/SkyrimVM64.cpp) has the original main-loop and VM detours. The shared fork already had X.00 work; I retained its engine-hook ownership and split that work into the integration design's named slots. The upstream VM detour calls the app on every active entry, which does not distinguish the two permitted return sites and was rejected for the MTD gate.
* [PrismaUI `MainThreadQueue.cpp`](https://github.com/PrismaUI-SKSE/framework/blob/main/src/Utils/MainThreadQueue.cpp) posts `std::function` objects to a mutex-protected vector, swaps the vector on main, and invokes the tasks. It confirms the main-thread pump pattern. Its heap-backed, unbounded posts and single lane were rejected for engine-lock callbacks and per-key holds; `MainQueue.h` instead uses five bounded MPSC rings and inline records.
* [CommonLibSSE-NG `TaskInterface::AddTask`](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/SKSE/Interfaces.cpp) allocates a task delegate before handing it to SKSE. This is suitable for ordinary plugin tasks but was rejected for the fixed-capacity, allocation-free hook/sink push requirement. No source from these projects was copied.

Exact-runtime executable evidence, checked with `sk.py fn <VA>` and `sk.py id <ID>`:

| VA / Address Library ID | Observed contract and use |
|---|---|
| `0x140658870` / 36564 | `Main::Update` contains the normal VM call at return `0x140658DBA`; the mod's HookMainLoop still calls the original after X.00. |
| `0x1409D7450` / 53926, `0x1406E57D0` / 39074 | VM update runs its timer and script body. Job_VM_update calls it at return `0x1406E57F3`; only that return and 36564's M.19 return run `App::Update`. Other active entries increment `AppLaneNested` and still execute the native VM body. |
| `0x1401A6540` / 13363; byte `0x14219E5F6` / 400336 | The start path has early returns; successful start writes the byte and asks JobListManager to start serving. The end path clears the byte. The hook samples both byte edges and JLM state; only a 0-to-1 edge with post-call state 2 raises `LoadSerial`. A start without the serving loop is `InFrameLoad` and is counted without raising the serial. |
| `0x14060E990` / 35406; pointer `0x143220A90` / 403107 | The native reads JobListManager state at pointer +0x6C. MTD reads that field without calling a function that assigns a worker mask bit. |
| `0x140646BB0` / 36317 | Calendar resync writes raw days from GameDaysPassed. The return detour raises `LoadGeneration`; `loadGenOrphans` counts a resync without a fresh `LoadSerial`. Its callers are `0x14061CFB0`, `0x14061DE80`, and `0x14061E230`; a paired run must establish which load paths reach them. |
| `0x1411B1BE0` / 90256; `0x1411B3E10` / 90293 | The first wraps the movie loop; the second executes a movie step. Counter-only entry/return detours maintain `BinkDepth`, including nested calls. The watchdog ignores a stalled frame count while depth is nonzero. |
| `0x140651300` / 36544; byte `0x1420B58C0` / 380768 | WinMain tests focus and `bAlwaysActive` before calling the main loop. Renderer init and slot 0a write one byte to keep X.00 available while unfocused. |

Implemented this cut: named slots 0a through 7, X.01 and X.02; existing pumps retained in their named slots, with the clock sample moved before slot 3.2 as INTEGRATION section 2 requires. `MarkMainThread` retains its existing save/load work until cut M6, so a load can still occur in slot 0a and DoorVote can still start one in slot 3.2. MainQueue's five capacities are Window 16, Ui 64, World 512, Script 256 and Terminal 4. Script is the reserved SAM ApplyQueue lane; there are no producers yet. The queue test checks saturation, per-key hold ordering, other-key progress and two concurrent producers. The queue owner callback will supply epoch, deadline, gate, apply and cancel policy when each service migrates. MT has a single main id, TLS scopes, 64-sample slot timings, load and connection serials, the VM return gate, Bink depth and a dedicated watchdog. `mt_status` exposes these counters and timings. The checker reports field is currently zero because step 9 did not land; there is no claim that it covers unsafe writes.

Step 9 was stopped: report-only wrappers and engine probes for the later MTD cuts require coordinated edits to UI, renderer, actor, quest, transport and registry paths, plus the six native probe owners. A partial checker would underreport. Stage 0c is therefore incomplete even though steps 1-8 compile. The loaded queue has no new network messages or protocol changes, so no client/server encoding tests were added.

Cost: every slot timer is O(1) and records one of 64 recent samples; status sorts at most 64 samples per slot only when requested. Queue push is bounded O(1) expected CAS work with no allocation; drain is O(C) expected for a lane of capacity C (fixed capacities above), and records occupy fixed memory independent of players P, actors A and bodies B. The watchdog wakes once per second, O(1). At five and ten players these are structural bounds, not measured latency or throughput; MTD-T19 still must measure p95 slot times, queue carry and end-to-end behavior.

Validation: isolated MSVC `/Zs` passed the six edited client translation units, and the standalone MainQueue test passed; `git diff --check` passed. `vswhere.exe` printed a nonfatal environment warning and existing Calendar/Transport narrowing warnings remain. No xmake build, link, game launch or two-PC observation was run. X-T12 is enabled as a **diagnostic** of load phase/serial/generation only; its slot-7 expectation cannot pass until M6. MTD-T8 (Bink), T10 (unfocused title), T13 (watchdog), T20 (nested VM) and T21 (in-frame load) now have counters or probes to observe, but their full acceptance criteria depend on later cuts and paired runs. `COMMON.md` exempts this batch from Reviewer A/B; neither reviewer was invoked.


## b17 crime and experience source check (2026-10-02)

Primary source search preceded the changes. [CommonLibSSE-NG Actor.cpp](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/A/Actor.cpp) was inspected for `GetCrimeFaction`, `KillImmediate`, actor handles and native actor wrappers; its names support the API mapping, while 1.7.104 behavior comes from the executable. [CommonLibSSE-NG Crime.h](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/C/Crime.h) was inspected in the local `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG` clone: it identifies the witness array at +0x28 but mislabels the gold at +0x58 and the lock at +0x68, so those offsets were taken from the executable instead. [CommonLibSSE-NG PlayerCharacter.h](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/P/PlayerCharacter.h) was inspected for the skill API and crime gold virtuals. Reusing the existing player-combat relay was adopted for guard response because it already authenticates the party sender, actor ownership and cell proximity. Calling native `StartCombat` 38561 directly from the message handler was rejected: this fork's existing `Actor::DrainStartCombatNative` uses the Papyrus wrapper after observed null combat-group crashes from direct 38561 calls. Replaying the alarm on the host was rejected because it would charge the host player through the teammate swap.

Scale: the new hook does O(min(W,512)) work per charged crime, where witnesses W are at most A loaded high actors; the two queues hold at most 64 events and drain 8 per frame. The host processes at most the bounded witness list per report, and its preexisting combat queue drains 32 targets per main frame. Murder attribution adds O(1) per hit/death and drains at most 8 notifications per frame. No new per-frame scan of P players, A actors or B bodies was added. Server party validation costs O(P) per reported death or crime; recipient selection already scans the party. Five-player CPU, memory and network cost is not yet profiled.

### b19 combat copies: knock, down, flee and mounts (2026-10-02)

Primary source search and inspection: [activeragdoll `src/utils.cpp`](https://github.com/adamhynek/activeragdoll/blob/master/src/utils.cpp) reads actor knock bits 25-27 and distinguishes GetUp; this supports streaming the state rather than inferring it from body motion. Its VR offsets and physical-animation controller were rejected for this 1.7.104 build. [skymp `MpActor.cpp`](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/skymp5-server/cpp/server_guest_lib/MpActor.cpp) sends an explicit death decision and stores health percentages together; the owner-decision pattern was adopted, but its server respawn/teleport model does not provide this mod's essential bleedout or animation transitions. [Precision `ContactListener.cpp`](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/Havok/ContactListener.cpp) filters collision callbacks before hit processing; no local collision observation was adopted as authority for knock, flee or death. The existing `CorpseRagdollRequest` owner stream and `Movement` owner snapshots were extended instead. These source paths were inspected, not only repository descriptions.

The 1.7.104 executable corpus was queried with `sk.py fn id:<ID>` before editing:

| Address Library ID / VA | Engine contract and use |
| --- | --- |
| 39895 / 0x1407237C0 | `AIProcess::KnockExplosion` enters state 2 only on its guarded branch and calls StopInteractingQuick for a seated rider. Existing next-frame state check and bounded retry for ACTOR-10 were retained. Owner dismount is now sent before the first body sample; the copy logs a still-mounted knock. Pending mount relations pause during a local knock without being erased. |
| 39896 / 0x140723B60; 40150 / 0x140735C20; 39897 / 0x140723FF0; 38085 / 0x1406B7C40 | Local knock processing can call StartGetUp from the rest/timer path. StartGetUp is gated for a living remote NPC while the owner's knock state is 1-4 or 7. At owner get-up/terminal state the ragdoll binding is retired before the gate opens. A blocked native attempt is left to native retry; if none was blocked, a main-frame StartGetUp uses the graph's native 38085 result. A direct jump to owner state 0 uses native knock end. Raw actor knock bits are never written. |
| 37624 / 0x140696060; 37612 / 0x140693200 | Process-level demotion can clear state 7. The existing ACTOR-06 ApplyPeers recheck was retained. New NPC down-state replay uses SetLifeState at the main-frame drain and rechecks only streamed down actors, bounded to 32 per frame. |
| 38631 / 0x1406CEF50; 40004 / 0x140727E30 | Health damage can enter local NPC bleedout; the existing remote-NPC 38631 hook declines it and existing kill hook refuses local death. 40004 writes the local bleedout timer; it is not used on copies because `kNoBleedoutRecovery` stays set while the owner reports down and the owner state transition, not a local countdown, ends it. This is a deliberate difference in local timer storage, not an observed game result. |
| 37577 / 0x140691AA0 | IsFleeing can read combat-controller, MiddleHigh or package state. The hook now returns the streamed owner value for remote NPCs. Raw CombatState readers are not covered or claimed to be synchronized. |
| 42884 / 0x1407CEE40; 38702 / 0x1406D32C0 | The rider attachment path resolves mount roots; GetMount takes an extra-data read lock. Mounted placement is therefore queried on the main frame and cached for the world update, never queried from its worker thread. Only native-mounted sit-state-3 remote riders skip ForcePosition; sit-state-1/2 and cart passengers retain their existing paths. |

Source status: ACTOR-10 and ACTOR-06 were present before this batch. ACTOR-09, ACTOR-15, ACTOR-05's NPC life-state half, ACTOR-22 and ACTOR-14 have source changes in this private copy. The NPC movement snapshot now adds 6 bits (4 life, 1 essential, 1 flee) per sent actor movement and relays them through the server. The wire marker moved from SSM/2 to SSM/3; all peers must use matching binaries. State application visits at most 32 queued/down NPCs and 32 native mount candidates per main frame, O(min(A,32)) each, independent of bodies B; the existing movement fanout is O(P*A) per update and the ragdoll body stream remains O(P*B) while active. There is no new full actor or body scan. Five-player CPU/network measurements are pending.

### b10b native in-game Journal CO-OP and OPTIONS (2026-10-02)

Primary sources searched and inspected before editing: [SkyUI VR's Quest_Journal.as](https://github.com/Odie/skyui-vr/blob/master/src/PauseMenu/Quest_Journal.as) routes keyboard and controller through the page's `handleInput` and lets the System page own its list, supporting a native list rather than a cursor-only overlay. [SkyUI BasicList.as](https://github.com/schlangster/skyui/blob/master/src/Common/skyui/components/list/BasicList.as) dispatches `itemPress` with both `entry` and `index`, supporting action properties on each entry. [SkyUI SKI_ConfigBase.psc](https://github.com/schlangster/skyui/blob/master/dist/Data/Scripts/Source/SKI_ConfigBase.psc) identifies the engine's menu as `Journal Menu`; its custom ConfigPanel path was rejected because the owner has no SkyUI and the target is the vanilla movie. The installed 1.7.104 `Skyrim - Interface.bsa` was decompiled locally with BSArch and JPEXS: `SystemPage.as` appends the vanilla eight entries in `onLoad`, uses fixed positions for `SetSaveDisabled`, handles item presses in `onCategoryButtonPress`, and already has `PCQuitPanel` and `ConfirmPanel` with gamepad focus. New entries are appended after QUIT and intercepted before the vanilla index switch; the existing list and confirmation panels are reused. Inserting above SAVE, LOAD, or QUIT was rejected because it would shift Bethesda's fixed indexes and permission arguments. The `PCQuitPanel` opening frame (`DefineSprite_652/frame_10/DoAction.as`) hardcodes state 8, so the patch changes that one frame to select state 19 only for CO-OP; its closing frame remains native. The `ConfirmPanel` opening and closing frames use `confirmType` and `returnState`, so its existing quit confirmation state can return to the new submenu. The source SWF SHA-256 is `840508308251DC8E5960755743216B39962333C923F527EF198898C0FEE3E012`.

Engine corpus: `sk.cmd id 55490` resolves to **0x140A253E0 / Address Library ID 55490**. `sk.cmd fn 0x140A253E0` shows the function creates a `FadeThenMainMenuCallback` and invokes the fade path at 0x14098DDD0. The existing `CheckpointSaves` hook targets this exact ID; the in-game full leave requests it from `LobbyService::OnMainFrame` only after party/carry-home and Steam/transport cleanup have been queued and drained. It does not call a save routine. The quit-to-main-menu result, Journal panel transitions, and controller selection still require a changed-build game run; the source and SWF preview are not in-game validation.

The current client `OverlayService::PollControllerNavigation` already forwards XInput D-pad and left stick as directions, A/B as accept/back, and the Angular `GamepadNavigationService` owns focus and routes B to Escape. The SETTINGS and COOP_LOBBY components have navigation scopes and Escape handlers, so a second controller path was rejected. The new Journal flags are read only while `Journal Menu` is open; member names/role are checked every 250 ms, at O(P) for up to 16 members during the open menu, and published only when changed so selection does not jump. Idle frames do no member scan. The END PARTY wire uses the existing leader-only `PartyKickRequest` opcode with reserved player ID zero and adds a forced bit to `NotifyPartyLeft` so recipients can distinguish host removal from voluntary leave. The host keeps its local server running while its own session is recreated so already queued forced-leave notices can drain to followers; immediate server termination was rejected as a packet-loss risk. The protocol marker changes to SSM/3; matching client and server binaries are required. END PARTY itself sends O(P squared) intermediate party-info records as members are removed, once per request, with no per-frame cost. Five-player performance and paired behavior are not yet measured.

Independent review: Reviewer A completed a read-only source review. Its high findings were the `PCQuitPanel` frame-10 hardcoded state (fixed and round-tripped), immediate host-server termination before guest notification (removed), and a frozen Journal after Yes if the action role changed (the native Journal is now closed for either confirmed action before dispatch). Its medium findings led to preserving the existing password across native open/close via `PartySessionSettingsRequest::KeepPassword`, retaining the session-state guard for forced carry-home, guarding `World::Get` before World construction, and deferring ID 55490 while the Loading Menu is open. Its member-list and bridge-state findings were corrected: a changed member roster refreshes the chooser, and the bridge accepts nested actions only in the CO-OP state. The legacy overlay button now confirms before forwarding to the same full-leave path. Reviewer A's host promotion concern remains a deployment risk: logical party leadership can move, but the local server process still runs on the original owner's PC and dies if that PC exits the game. The native list still offers MAKE HOST as requested; no server-process migration is claimed. Reviewer A suggested a server-level fanout test; only wire round trips were run, so three-player forced-delivery behavior remains unverified. Reviewer B could not start: its session writer lease failed with Windows access denied and it reported an unreadable settings file. The task-specific COMMON.md exempts this batch from requiring the two reviews, and no Reviewer B conclusion is claimed.

Validation: the Journal patch compiled in preview mode from the installed BSA without writing the game directory; the resulting `quest_journal.swf` SHA-256 was `BA54A3C2A320841A30B6E4ECB8ECCBB60A1CAE169A64B7926DBDBB7091967D29`. MSVC `/Zs` passed for eight affected C++ translation units using the original tree's read-only Libraries/build flags. Angular `ngc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` passed. The isolated protocol executable passed 14 assertions in two cases, including forced-leave and password-intent wire round trips; `git diff --check` passed. No xmake build, game-directory install, changed-build run, or paired two-PC observation was performed. In particular, the System list's visible capacity and focus behavior, the order of the game's leave/fade events, and END PARTY delivery to all connected followers remain in-game acceptance items.


### b10b-fix1 Journal list capacity and test bridge (2026-10-02)

The owner observed only PARTY and INVITE FRIEND in the deployed host pause menu. The installed 1.7.104 `quest_journal.swf` was decompiled and its `SystemPage.as`, `Quest_Journal.as`, `Shared/BSScrollingList.as`, and panel frame scripts inspected. `PCQuitPanel.List_mc` has only the two vanilla Quit row clips, so assigning six host actions or up to 16 member names to `PCQuitList.entryList` cannot render the rest. `CategoryList` already owns the System tab's longer scrolling list and its `itemPress`/keyboard/controller/mouse handling. The patched state 19 now maps to the System page's `PanelRect` and keeps `CategoryList` focus. The source array is saved by reference, including the appended CO-OP/OPTIONS rows and a later `$CREATIONS` insertion; BACK, Cancel, tab switch, Journal close, and unload restore it. Selection is cleared before each array swap because `BSScrollingList.doSetSelectedIndex` reads the former entry. `InvalidateData` recalculates scroll limits for the 16-name picker. Bethesda's positional permission callbacks are kept off the temporary co-op array. `QUIT_CONFIRM_STATE` and all action numbers remain in place. Reusing `HelpList` was rejected because the existing System list provides enough row clips and requires no Help panel transition.

Primary source cross-check: [SkyUI VR `Quest_Journal.as`](https://github.com/Odie/skyui-vr/blob/master/src/PauseMenu/Quest_Journal.as) routes page input and tab selection through the Journal movie, and [SkyUI `BasicList.as`](https://github.com/schlangster/skyui/blob/master/src/Common/skyui/components/list/BasicList.as) uses per-entry `itemPress` data. Both were inspected as related open-source implementations; the installed vanilla movie supplied the exact patch text and panel behavior. The executable corpus confirms `UIMessageQueue::CreateUIMessageData` at VA **0x14116E1B0**, Address Library ID **82169**, and `UIMessageQueue::AddMessage` at VA **0x1401B47F0**, ID **13631**. `JournalMenu::ProcessMessage` at VA **0x1409ABE30**, ID **53315**, processes the Journal show path only with message data. The `pause_menu_open` bridge therefore queues a data-bearing show message from `GameTestService::OnGameThread` (called by `World::UpdateMainFrame`) and, after movie initialization, invokes patched `Quest_Journal.SkyrimSeamlessShowSystem` to end the previous page and select System through the vanilla page and button methods. Calling the existing null-data `ShowNativeMenu`, assigning `iCurrentTab` alone, or re-running `RestoreSavedSettings` on an already open other tab was rejected because those paths skip Journal initialization, tab focus/depth work, or previous-page cleanup.

Independent Reviewer A flagged the old list's selected-index dereference, the `onCategoryListPress` back path, missing close restoration, permission indexes, `$CREATIONS` insertion, and role updates during confirmation. The patch now clears selection before swapping, handles co-op list cancel separately, restores on close, guards positional permission calls, adjusts the saved index on later `$CREATIONS`, and defers role-stamp consumption until co-op state is active. Reviewer B identified the required data-bearing native show message and the window-thread/main-thread split for `GameTestService`; the bridge queues work to the main frame. Their findings were checked against the decompiled movie and executable, not treated as validation by agreement.

`Patch-NativeJournalMenu.ps1 -PreviewOnly -WorkPath .\\journal-preview` decompiled and compiled the installed BSA without touching the game directory: vanilla SHA-256 `840508308251DC8E5960755743216B39962333C923F527EF198898C0FEE3E012`; patched SHA-256 `A022B0D68F9F63B7D2D66E4B70C0BF7682152EB440B96E87FD036A986089D47A`. JPEXS printed a logging-file access warning but produced the output and exited successfully. MSVC `/Zs` passed `GameTestService.cpp` with the original tree's read-only Libraries and flags; vcvars printed a nonfatal vswhere PATH warning. No changed build was deployed or exercised in game, so visible rows, controller focus, close restoration, and `pause_menu_open` System selection remain paired in-game acceptance checks.
