## b10c quit to lobby return (2026-10-02)

Source research before implementation: [CommonLibSSE-NG MainMenu.h](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/M/MainMenu.h) identifies the native Main Menu as an application menu and its movie/message entry points. [CommonLibSSE-NG UIMessageQueue.cpp](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/src/RE/U/UIMessageQueue.cpp) shows named menu messages are queued, so the existing `UI_AddToActiveQueue_Hook`/`OnLaunchMenuQueued` is the right boundary for hiding a newly created title before it renders. The [upstream TiltedEvolution PartyService.cpp](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/client/Services/Generic/PartyService.cpp) and [OverlayClient.cpp](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/client/Services/Generic/OverlayClient.cpp) were inspected locally in `C:/Tools/ref/TiltedEvolution-upstream`: `LeaveParty` sends a request and the overlay calls it directly; neither returns Skyrim to its title. Retained the request and added a main-frame native quit for a running session. A direct load of the campfire from a party callback was rejected because the callback can be off the main thread and would bypass Skyrim's title reset.

Exact 1.7.104 executable functions read with `sk.py fn id:<ID>`: `Game.QuitToMainMenu` **0x140A253E0 / 55490** requests a fade callback; callback **0x140A2E9A0 / 55646** marks `Main+0x11` for the title reset. The UI load transition **0x1401A6540 / 13363** closes menus, queues UI messages, and enters a loading wait; the hook continues to set flags only inside it and posts its menu restoration on the later main frame. `UIMessageQueue::AddMessage` **0x1401B47F0 / 13631** queues the named message; it is not a safe replacement for the existing menu-queue interception. Main Menu show **0x140965710 / 52316** can request the Mist model and queue a show message, so the return must arm before the native quit and clear the model as the menu is queued. Mist model setter **0x140965490 / 52313** owns the model request. Title sequence start **0x140985190 / 52654** drives the existing splash. `ControlMap::PopInputContext` **0x140CEF980 / 68544** removes the TFC context from the live stack, so the staging path pops any stale context before the next `tfc` and then locks controls as it did at launch.

The implementation re-arms the launch title one-shot on a requested return, with a queued-menu fallback for other engine title returns. The native journal quit still owns its fade, while leaving a live party or losing its connection asks for the same native quit on the main frame. The lobby rescans saves with a scan generation so a slower previous scan cannot publish stale entries, and resets presentation caches before the fresh Main Menu movie. No protocol message changed. New per-frame work is a constant number of UI/player checks, O(1) in players P, actors A, and bodies B; party seats still use their existing bounded five-slot path. This is a design cost estimate, not a five-player measurement.

Validation: MSVC `/Zs` passed the four edited client translation units (`LobbyService.cpp`, `CheckpointSaves.cpp`, `PartyService.cpp`, `GameTestService.cpp`) with the private copy's headers, original read-only Libraries and cached flags. `CheckpointSaves.cpp` emitted its existing C++20 `u8path` deprecation warning. `git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check` passed. No xmake build, plugin link, paired in-game test, or five-player profile was run. Acceptance still requires journal Quit to Main Menu on both PCs, Rex leaving the party in game, a disconnect and no-reload death return, no vanilla menu/dragon frame, a fresh character list and seats, working Continue/New Game Together after return, and journal Quit to Desktop still exiting. `lobby_return_status` exposes the reason and phase. `COMMON.md` exempts this batch from Reviewer A/B; neither reviewed this change.

## 2026-10-02: ov-b10 round 1, reviewer refutation accepted

Conclusion: the original b10 completion claim was false. The four concrete defects are supported by source, not explained away by missing captures. This round repairs their source mechanisms, but the complete owner request is still NOT accepted: legacy progression, trustworthy words in old snapshots, complete loaded-character coverage, bounded full restoration and paired runtime acceptance remain outstanding. Confidence is high in that negative conclusion; runtime confidence in this revision is unestablished.

Research and decisions:
- Searched the web for CommonLib NPC loading/word types and open-source character transfer implementations. Read the primary [CommonLib TESNPC header](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/T/TESNPC.h) and [FormTypes header](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/main/include/RE/F/FormTypes.h), including their local copies. Adopted the explicit face/gender/race flag set and WordOfPower 0x76. A PROTEUS repository search result was discovery only, not implementation evidence; no claim of inspecting or reusing its source.
- Read native NPC SaveGame **24777 / 0x1403C7640**, NPC LoadGame **24778 / 0x1403C7B10**, actor-base LoadGame **24388 / 0x1403B05D0**, and base-data loader **14411 / 0x1401DDAF0**. The last loads 0x18 bytes into actor-base data when flag 0x2 is present. The NPC loader also consumes skills under 0x200 and the base spell list through its parent. Therefore changing only the input flag mask is WRONG: it changes interpretation without removing those bytes. Adopted explicit masked serialization for new captures and scratch-NPC decode with ORIGINAL flags followed by reserialization with **0x03000800** before touching the local player. Existing SaveLook::BuildLook uses the same scratch/explicit-save-mask approach. The scratch is reused, not created every frame. Original race is normalized as in the existing character import policy. This isolates native NPC data; it does not prove race-switch side effects preserve every live actor value.
- Read word constructor **23407 / 0x140388AE0**: writes **0x76**, confirming 118. Corrected the one constant used by capture, resolve validation and whole-word-array restore. **120 is EquipSlot**. Correcting the reader cannot recreate word records that earlier snapshots never captured. Empty Words in snapshot version 1 cannot distinguish a legitimately unlearned character from a broken capture. An original source save and a corrected fresh capture or a complete save decoder are required for reliable recovery; no guessed words were added.
- Re-read **52388 / 0x14096B7D0** and **68548 / 0x140CEFB80**; native closing and live control-mask reads are as the reviewer described. Read installed `C:/Tools/ref/mq101-audit/psc/qf_mq101_0003372b.psc`: Fragment_13 lines 1505-1507 opens ShowRaceMenu; Fragment_11 lines 1026-1032 exits chargen/adds race spells; Fragment_74 lines 1034-1044 enables controls and removes cuffs. Existing stage mapping remains 75/80/240. No new offsets, probes or quest exceptions.
- Read **40255 / 0x140739A40**. Reset3D can queue work; a clock timeout is not evidence that the new head exists. Strict intro apply now requires a changed head pointer, existing BoundPoseKeeper graph/model readiness, equality of the explicitly serialized appearance and name, and all current/snapshot tint layers matched. If an actually observed bound pose preceded the rebuild, the existing BoundPoseKeeper repair must accept the replay. Rebuild start is gated by CanRebuildNow with a 20-second deadline. This is a conservative data/readiness check, NOT proof of rendered parity: pointer reuse or normalized native data can cause a false failure, and final textures/graphs still require observations. Retained older lobby callers' timeout behavior to avoid an unrelated preview change.

Lifecycle corrections in the existing task files:
- CreatorTogether freezes eligibility, snapshot, name and carry-home key at the campaign epoch. Promotion does not reset them. Epoch/party reset cancels the specific apply; loading during an unfinished skip fails with readiness withheld.
- Every snapshot queue submission advances an operation ID. Intro callers retain that ID; stale status publication and another caller's done result cannot satisfy their barrier. Cancellation drops queued work immediately and the main-frame pump drops an invalidated running rebuild before subsequent writes. Already executed native mutations cannot be rolled back. This is frame-boundary cancellation, not a transactional engine load.
- Party metadata is copied from the client update under the existing skip-status mutex. Appearance publication now runs on the client-update registry owner, at most 40 attempts per epoch, at least 250 ms apart, with a ten-second main-frame deadline. No SendLookNow registry traversal remains in TickSkipCreator.
- Continued sessions before MQ101 stage 80 can use a selected named follower's snapshot, including session state 3. Already-created continued characters do not enter this transfer path. Host exclusion remains unchanged. The broader case of an arbitrary live named character unrelated to the lobby choice is still not implemented; it must not be represented as covered.
- PartyService also withholds state-2 readiness when an outstanding skip in a continued session has not passed its look check. Bridge status adds the apply operation ID.

Every reviewer limit retained:
- SaveLook::BuildLook still constructs a default CharacterSnapshot and fills look/items/tints, not skills/perks/spells/words. Existing snapshot fullState means fields are structurally available, not verified parity or repaired old word capture. Legacy items-only completion remains explicitly partial. No raw PlayerCharacter change-form load was added: it also imports the source world, controls and objectives. Automatic source-save loading is not a proven substitute and can hit missing-content prompts (SaveLook.h documents that existing constraint).
- Exact face/body/name/tint/equipment/progression/quest-item parity, crash freedom, engine load interruption, promotion in a live party, and GPU completion remain unverified. The retained fade-in workaround and old EVIDENCE captures are not acceptance evidence. The supplied run5 host and follower logs contain no b10 skip/apply acceptance markers. No new runtime capture was taken.
- This revision adds no Havok body writes, changes no checked native ABI, and removes no baseline cart/ragdoll/trigger/door behavior. Changed code postdates the working 25fa030d baseline. No independent review of this revision is claimed; the complete supplied review was read and accepted, and COMMON.md exempts this batch from launching Reviewer A/B.

Correct cost accounting (P players, A registered actors, B bodies, F all FormId entities, I inventory entries, K perks, W words, S spells/shouts, T tint layers): idle skip checks O(1) per local frame, O(P) across PCs, with no B scan. Cuffs cost up to 21 O(I) lookups after the gate. Publishing still calls existing BuildLook, O(F), now at most 40 attempts per skipping PC per epoch; it is NOT O(1). One successful new appearance per follower can produce (P-1)^2 recipient deliveries, 16 at five players and 81 at ten. Apply performs O(K+W+S+I^2) work in the worst case including inventory collection, plus engine side effects; it remains synchronous and lacks a frame budget. Snapshot copies/status strings/serialization allocate. Tint fallback can cost O(T^2); verification serializes once per completed attempt. The old creator per-frame collections predate b10. No measured CPU, frame-time, memory or bandwidth result supports a five/ten-player target. Full-state application is therefore NOT cleared against the scale rule.

Validation: MSVC /Zs syntax checks passed for CharacterSnapshots.cpp, CreatorTogether.cpp and PartyService.cpp using the existing compiler manifest (no xmake/build/link). A temporary C++ harness compiled the actual queue/status and TickSkipCreator function text with mocked engine/lobby services; host/PRISONER exclusion, promotion through unbinding with frozen return key, stale completion, cross-caller supersession, epoch cancellation, same-epoch loading, continue before/after creator and publication timeout passed. These are control-flow tests, not native ABI, rendering, protocol integration or paired gameplay tests. Targeted git diff --check passed. The coordinator must build the revision before the specified paired acceptance run; until then the appropriate batch status is NOT COMPLETE.

## 2026-10-03: ov-b10 round 2, verify1 adjudication

**Conclusion: b10 does not meet the complete preservation contract.** High confidence applies to this source-level counterexample: `SaveLook::BuildLook` creates a default snapshot and fills appearance/items/tints; `ReadChosenCharacterForNewGame` returns it with `fullState=false`; the actual `TickSkipCreator` then queues `Items`, not `State`. No code in that branch can restore the omitted XP/skills/perks/spells/words. The retained harness executes that branch. This conclusion does not depend on assuming a rendered result or native ABI works. Runtime confidence in the revised build is unestablished. No changed-build or five/ten-player acceptance is claimed.

Read the complete `C:/Tools/skyrim_re/agent/conductor/ov-b10-verify1.log`. Evidence and reproducible commands are retained under `C:/Tools/skyrim_re/agent/overnight/b10-r2-evidence/`; `report-b10.md` in its parent adjudicates every review point. COMMON.md exempts this batch from launching Reviewer A/B; neither reviewed round 2.

Research before changes: web search for related CommonLib/Skyrim Together appearance serialization, then inspected the primary [CommonLib TESNPC header](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/a898f469851c464d05137bb74b069dd234897643/include/RE/T/TESNPC.h), local clone at `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG/include/RE/T/TESNPC.h:44`, and this fork's `Games/Forms.cpp:25`. Adopted explicit face/gender/race flags. Rejected equating that mask with proof of all runtime statistics or rendered appearance: native race switching has side effects and the engine save omits body weight/height; this fork carries those in a trailer. Rejected masking an existing stream without rewriting it: field order follows the ORIGINAL flags.

Native functions re-read, with complete query output retained in `native.txt`:

| Address Library ID | VA | Decisive evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 24777 | 0x1403C7640 | NPC save separates skills (bit 9), class (10), race (25), face (11), gender (24), outfits (12/13). Face includes head form IDs and morph values, not raw pointer addresses. |
| 24778 | 0x1403C7B10 | NPC load reads with original flags; absent head-data/morph presence bytes can leave previous values untouched. A reused scratch NPC is not a clean decode target. |
| 24388 | 0x1403B05D0 | Actor-base load calls 14411 before the NPC-specific fields. |
| 14411 | 0x1401DDAF0 | Bit 1 loads the 0x18-byte base-data block, including level. `0x03000800` excludes it. |
| 23407 | 0x140388AE0 | WordOfPower constructor writes type 0x76 = 118. |
| 37925 | 0x1406AD970 | Actor::SwitchRace calls actor-value/magic/3D and interaction paths; narrow stream flags alone do not prove unchanged live statistics. |
| 40255 | 0x140739A40 | DoReset3D may enqueue work; returning is not a render fence. |
| 40696 | 0x14075CAE0 | Rebuilds tint templates, reusing allocation when counts match. |
| 40699 | 0x14075CF20 | Finds the face shader and invokes CreateTints; matched tint count is not GPU completion evidence. |
| 52388 | 0x14096B7D0 | Starts FadeOut and sets closing byte +0x1A0; not a full Done/readiness acknowledgement by itself. |
| 68548 | 0x140CEFB80 | Reads live controls at +0x120 and stored controls at +0x124. |
| 24648 | 0x1403BB570 | Factory allocates 0x268 bytes and invokes the NPC constructor, which initializes defaults. |
| 24888 | 0x1403CBCA0 | NPC virtual deleting destructor performs cleanup and engine deallocation when flag bit 0 is set. |
| 24660 | 0x1403BBF50 | Releases NPC-owned head/morph allocations during destruction. |

Source changes this round:
- `CharacterSnapshots::IsolateAppearance`: one native factory-created NPC per conversion, owned by `unique_ptr`, instead of a static scratch across characters/loads. Do not call the mod's `Initialize()`, which borrows the current player's defaults; require all appearance flags and let the native factory initialize a clean NPC. The native loader's omitted-field behavior motivated this change. Virtual destruction is source-checked, not exercised in Skyrim. This fixes this helper's lifetime and cross-conversion state mechanism; it does not repair the separate pre-existing SaveLook scratch or recover missing source data.
- `PartyService::OnUpdate` home apply: a continued pre-creator checkpoint previously queued full state after three seconds while CreatorTogether waited for session state 2. It could import level/gear early and supersede the intro operation. Once the loaded world is available, a Continue before MQ101 stage 80 now releases the home-apply gate and defers to CreatorTogether. Post-creator Continue retains the existing full apply. No new quest-specific rule was added beyond the task's own MQ101 creator boundary.
- `CharacterSnapshots.h`: correct misleading `QueueApplyLook` comment. It is a lobby preview that writes name, level and items. The skip uses `Appearance`, not `Look`. Narrow the skip comment to the actual writes, with no promise about native race effects.

Promotion question settled by current server source, not an assumed mocked epoch: `OnPartyChangeLeader` (server PartyService.cpp:330) changes leader ID/generation without changing StartEpoch or SessionState. `RemovePlayerFromParty` (:814) also keeps StartEpoch and advances only surviving barriers via `AfterMemberLeft`. `BroadcastPartyInfo` sends that epoch (:923); client `OnPartyInfo` stores it directly. Therefore neither ordinary manual promotion nor leader disconnect triggers the epoch-reset branch. Actual new starts/checkpoint reloads do change the epoch and intentionally cancel pending work. This is a source property, not paired promotion acceptance.

The supplied review could not access the round-1 test evidence, but it exists: `ov-b10-r1.log:10114` and `:20199` report syntax success; `:14314` onward contains the harness source, and `:15018-15019` its passing output. Round 2 saves a rerunnable `verify.py`, generated harness C++, source hashes, and `verification.log`: three MSVC `/Zs` units and three extracted-production-function harnesses pass (lifecycle including the legacy counterexample, scratch lifetime/omitted fields, Continue dispatch). Native calls are mocks. A nonfatal missing-vswhere setup warning precedes the successful compiler runs. No protocol integration test, plugin build/link, deployment or in-game test occurred.

MQ101 stages were independently re-queried from the prebuilt plugin index (`mq101-fragments.json`) and checked against installed decompiled PSC: 75/Fragment_13 opens ShowRaceMenu; 80/Fragment_11 exits chargen/adds race spells; 240/Fragment_74 enables controls then removes cuffs. Stage 160 is not the inventory handoff. `run5-coverage.json` records hashes and searches the last sessions: host lines 25841-28012, follower .1 lines 26009-42713, follower current lines 38361-44603 contain zero b10 markers. This establishes absence of supplied b10 acceptance evidence, not a gameplay failure measurement.

Limits retained as failures of the full contract, not future diagnostic questions: legacy progression decoding and old-word recovery are absent; v1 snapshots have no provenance distinguishing a correct empty Words vector from one captured with the wrong type; arbitrary loaded-named-character capture is not implemented; full restore remains synchronous without a frame budget. Serialized equality can establish only serialized data, not the visible result. Head-pointer reuse can conservatively reject a valid rebuild; it cannot bypass the strict head-inequality predicate. Runtime fade, pose, load interruption, tint/gear/progression/quest-item parity and crash freedom are unproven.

Cost correction: the new scratch is allocated once per conversion, with O(D) payload work and no retained per-player/body storage. The new Continue check runs once after the existing world wait and scans the single MQ101 stage list O(Q). Existing waiting stage checks also scan that list, so blanket O(1) waiting is too strong. Full apply still has O(K+W+S+I^2) worst-case work, plus engine effects, without a frame budget. Publication remains O(F) per attempt, up to 40; fanout across skipping followers can be (P-1)^2, 16 at five and 81 at ten. No new all-actor/all-body scan was added (F includes registered actors and other forms; B is not traversed by these two changes). No timing or bandwidth claim is made. Saved `baseline.diff` is the entire selected-file workspace difference against 25fa030d, including other engineers' work, not this round's authorship or proof of runtime non-regression.


## 2026-10-03: b10-fix3, skip stays in the shared creator

Owner's two-PC observation at 00:58: Rex's old skip forced RaceSex to close before applying her chosen appearance, and her display remained black while the host edited. This is the observed old-build failure, not evidence that the changed build works. The old phase 1 set `s_forceReleaseRequested` and `s_releaseRequested`; the real begin-closing path then ran before `QueueApplyAppearance` and `FadeOutGame` attempted to compensate afterward.

Prior open-source source inspection before this patch:

- [CommonLibSSE-NG RaceSexMenu declaration](https://github.com/alandtse/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/ng/include/RE/R/RaceSexMenu.h) exposes `ChangeName`, the menu's update and input methods, and the 0x1A8 menu layout. Adopted the existing native ChangeName entry in this fork so the skip follows the ordinary name/Done event path. A declaration alone does not establish fade timing; the executable does.
- [RaceMenu PresetInterface.cpp](https://github.com/expired6978/SKSE64Plugins/blob/master/skee64/PresetInterface.cpp) `ApplyPresetData` (around source lines 2382-2538) changes NPC head parts, morphs and player tint masks in the creator. It confirms that a live creator can display an applied look. Rejected importing its preset logic: it uses RaceMenu-specific preset and override data, while this fork already has native snapshot deserialization, race switching, tint restoration and strict verification.
- This fork's `CharacterSnapshots::IsolateAppearance` and `BeginAppearance` are the adopted apply path: a prepared, reused scratch NPC isolates the appearance record; the local player receives the native NPC load, race/skeleton switch, 3D rebuild and tint verification. `CreatorTogether::OnMainFrame` already applies remote looks and provides the other-player viewer, so the skip uses its held Done state instead of a separate camera or fade.

Executable checks with `C:/Tools/skyrim_re/sk.cmd` for SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104:

| Address Library ID | VA | Evidence used here |
|---|---|---|
| 52388 | 0x14096B7D0 | RaceSex begin-closing invokes Scaleform `FadeOut` and sets menu +0x1A0 to 1. `HookBeginClosing` intercepts before both actions while holding. |
| 52345 | 0x140968010 | RaceSex per-frame processing branches on +0x1A0; entering closing stops the normal open-menu character update. The skip must stay out of this branch until joint release. |
| 52415 | 0x1409718D0 | `RaceSexMenu::ChangeName` writes the player's name, marks NPC name change 0x20, updates Scaleform name text and invokes ID 52388. Invoked only after strict apply and publication while the party holds the menu. |
| 52391 | 0x14096BCF0 | RaceSex rebuild calls `Actor::DoReset3D(true)` during normal creation; the imported-look path already uses this same reset. |
| 37925 | 0x1406AD970 | `Actor::SwitchRace` performs the full actor transition used by `BeginAppearance`. |
| 40255 | 0x140739A40 | `Actor::DoReset3D` can queue work, so only `CharacterSnapshots::ApplyStatus == done` counts as an applied look. |

`CreatorTogether::TickSkipCreator` now queues `QueueApplyAppearance` when stage 75 has opened RaceSex and the party is holding it. It keeps the menu open, locks editing immediately, and waits for strict appearance completion (serialized look/name, head rebuild, tints and bound pose). It then asks the existing bounded client-update publisher to call `CharacterService::SendLookNow`. After publication it invokes native `ChangeName`; `HookBeginClosing` records `s_done` and the held menu before the native fade. `s_skipReady` becomes true after that, preserving the PartyService readiness gate. The existing release path later calls the real begin-closing for the held menu when all players are ready. The old force-release and compensating fade were removed from skip phases; force release for separate party-state transitions remains untouched. `skipcreator_status` stages now describe open-menu apply/publication/held Done and expose `done`, `ready`, and `editingLocked` for paired observation.

The skip-specific editor lock hides the GFx panels and rejects its Scaleform/user events from the first import frame; it also prevents Backspace/View from clearing Done. The existing bracket and L3/R3 viewer poll remains active and can select the host or another player's character. The lock clears on epoch change, interrupted load, or after this creator closes. A failed apply or timed-out publication keeps the gameplay barrier held; it does not claim a usable imported character.

Independent reviews: Reviewer A supported native `ChangeName` under the existing begin-closing hook and noted that its name event dispatcher may be needed for ordinary Done semantics. Reviewer B preferred the existing snapshot name write plus direct held-Done transition because `ChangeName` calls begin-closing. The executable shows the hook intercepts that call before fade/+0x1A0, and the code guards on an open menu, party hold, and no release; native `ChangeName` was selected to retain its normal event path. Both reviewers independently identified the asynchronous editing window and Backspace readiness withdrawal; both are locked. Their source reviews do not validate the runtime result.

Validation here: `CreatorTogether.cpp` passed MSVC `/Zs` with the cached compiler manifest and read-only original Libraries; no xmake, link, deployment or changed-build two-PC test ran. `git diff --check` passed. The next paired acceptance must show Rex's named imported race/sex/face/tints on her own open creator and on the host, `skipcreator_status` reporting held Done and ready, Rex viewing the host while the host edits, both menus closing together after the host presses Done, and no black screen. Then check stage 80/240, Keep unbinding gear/progression, and the 20 s character-list wait. A successful two-PC run would still not establish five-player performance. The imported look may still be overwritten by native per-frame creator work or take up to the existing 20 s rebuild deadline; visible result and frame/network costs require measurement. Earlier b10 legacy snapshots still cannot restore unavailable progression fields.

## b10d host handoff through the lobby (2026-10-03, incomplete)

Primary source research: [Steamworks ISteamMatchmaking](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/api/ISteamMatchmaking) documents asynchronous lobby creation, lobby metadata, owner lookup and invites. A Steam owner migration does not move this mod's local game server, so the successor must create a new server and lobby. The original [Skyrim Together PartyService source](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/server/Services/PartyService.cpp) was inspected: its leader-change handler changes the party leader on the existing server; that approach was rejected for this owner's PC-host migration. The local `CampaignLedger.h/.cpp` was read: roster is one of several durable tables; metadata, journal, checkpoints and quest items remain in the old host's SQLite database. Sending only roster rows and a checkpoint ID cannot faithfully seed that ledger. The b10b in-game leader request and b10c `RequestReturnToLobby` paths were present and reused.

Further source inspection found the server database path is selected in `World.cpp` before `QuestItemService` and other services are constructed. `SteamLobbyService::StartLocalServer` starts a fresh process from the game directory, and `CommandSettingsProvider.cpp` supports a `Campaign:sDatabasePath` override. A complete implementation should transfer a consistent SQLite snapshot to each checkpoint sidecar, stage the selected common snapshot at a separate campaign-specific path before launching the successor's server, and pass that path in the server command line. Importing roster rows after the world is already constructed leaves service caches stale and may mix the successor PC's existing campaign database with the old host's. The current batch does not implement this transfer/staging.

Native 1.7.104 functions inspected with the offline executable corpus before changing quit/save behavior: `Game.QuitToMainMenu` **55490 / 0x140A253E0** queues a fade-to-title callback, so a planned leader quit must be held until the checkpoint barrier; save/load queue function **35769 / 0x140625850** and save-name path **35749 / 0x140623DA0** show that queuing a save is not evidence the `.ess` exists; `SendHUDMessage` **52933 / 0x140991A30** is the notification path used at the main-frame Steam transition. The position move **19799 / 0x1402F1E50** was identified but not changed; this batch relies on the existing b9 DropInService roster placement and each local Continue save. No offset guess or new live probe was added.

Position path inspection: `PartyService::LaunchCheckpoint` calls `CheckpointSaves::Load` on each PC for the chosen ID, so Continue restores that PC's local saved position. `DropInService::OnMainFrame` checks identity and loaded cell before `TryPlaceReturnPosition` performs a same-cell placement; a cross-cell roster entry does not load a cell. `DropInService::OnMessage` queues the local checkpoint load for a joining member. None of those paths was changed or exercised with a rehosted build, so two-PC position preservation remains an acceptance test rather than a proven result.

Current source changes attempt a planned save/roster/carry-home acknowledgment barrier, an ordered successor, new Steam lobby discovery, automatic rejoin, common checkpoint intersection, and handoff bridge commands. Source review caught and repaired several races: repeated save announcements no longer replace the selected handoff ID, acknowledgment is resent until ready, checkpoint IDs are monotonic at microsecond scale, a lobby missing metadata is left for retry, and a scoped handoff admission bypasses the optional ordinary auto-party-join setting. A 20-second join window prevents a two-person subset of a larger party from starting immediately; after it, the common checkpoint is chosen from currently connected survivors. Candidate IDs are constrained to the seed's epoch prefix and no newer than the seed. This is still weaker than a persisted campaign identifier; repeated epochs across different servers can alias. The new lobby publishes the old lobby ID and the join checks both origin and owner, but that marker is not an authenticated nonce.

Independent Reviewer A and Reviewer B both found **blocking ledger continuity**: the first `RequestHandoffSeed` implementation wrote only roster rows and called `CreateCheckpoint` with the ID as its snapshot. It did not transfer journal entries, quest items or original campaign metadata. That partial database mutation has been removed. `OnPartyStart` now fails closed for a seeded handoff until an actual ledger import sets `HandoffLedgerReady`; this batch has no such import, so Continue Together after a handoff is intentionally unavailable. They also found the initial two-player solo-Continue gate, subset start, acknowledgment reset, lobby metadata retry, missing carry-home source, old-host exclusion after a local server crash, and stale timeout state; the source revisions above address those specific paths. Their agreement is not runtime validation. No changed client/server build or paired in-game observation exists. In particular, no claim that planned swap, quit, kill, position recovery or quest continuity works is justified.

Cost model: one checkpoint fans a roster of P positions to P clients, O(P²) position deliveries (25 positions at five, 100 at ten), with up to 16 records per current wire message. Each local checkpoint list is bounded at 32 IDs; intersecting all connected members is O(32P²) with vector lookup, at most about 800 membership comparisons at five and 3200 at ten per availability event. Rehosting uses one 20-second membership gate and a 90-second once-per-second discovery retry; no new all-actor, body or per-frame party scan is intended. The fire UI still has five seat marks and therefore does not visually cover ten players. These are source estimates, not a five- or ten-player profile.



## 2026-10-03: b14 stage 1a server clock

Before editing, searched primary Skyrim Together and CommonLib sources. The upstream [CalendarService source](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/server/Services/CalendarService.cpp) and its client counterpart use a float DateTime and a per-frame client hard set, matching the local fork. [Upstream PR #897](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/pull/897) implements a sleep/wait advance with a bounded request and reports paired-gameplay results; its own author notes minute-scale drift and limited coverage. Adopted its central server authority and bounded serialization test idea. Rejected its post-sleep delta applied directly to GameDaysPassed: the reconciled TS design requires exact integer GameMs, ordered samples, and native 36291 calls for small forward corrections. CommonLibSSE-NG's [source tree](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG) was also checked; its declarations do not settle this executable's calendar call sites.

Executable evidence, queried with `sk.py fn <VA>` against SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104:

| Address Library ID | VA | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 36291 | 0x140645D60 | `Calendar::Update` adds `realSeconds * TimeScale / 3600` to GameHour, rolls whole days with an event, then writes GameDaysPassed from raw days and hour. R1 therefore calls it for a small positive difference and hard sets only for large differences or load resync. |
| 36317 | 0x140646BB0 | Raw-day resync takes the floor of GameDaysPassed and writes +0x3C; the post-load clock set writes that field and leaves +0x38 alone. |
| 40485 | 0x140749560 | Caller at 0x1407495DD is the native sleep/wait tick; stage 1a swallows it until stage 2b owns an executor. |
| 40445 | 0x140744600 | Fast travel calls 36291 and later performs world work; stage 1a swallows its clock call until Flow B Report is wired in stage 2b. |

Stage 1a now keeps `m_gameMs` and a double fractional carry on the server, derives the float model on send, pairs a joiner's hour and days, sends sequenced nonce-keyed samples every ten seconds, and appends their fields to ServerTimeSettings. On the client, the App-lane handler stores a sample; slot 1 applies it, writes TimeScale, and no longer hard sets the calendar every frame. The 36291 hook follows a small positive difference through the original and uses the no-event hard set beyond two hours. `SSC_TS_CLOCK=0` selects the old client path for an A/B run. The otherwise unused TS `Exec`, `WorldOnly`, pending and ahead-credit cases default to swallow or zero until stage 2b wires Flow A/B; no time-skip request is emitted by this stage. The Q19 live check logs the return address and count of 36317 calls at the next slot 1, allowing a two-PC run to distinguish load and new-game callers without adding an offset guess.

`server_clock_protocol.cpp` round-trips the extended sample. MSVC `/Zs` passed for its test and encoding source, the server and client CalendarService, and TimeManager.cpp, using read-only package headers and build dependency manifests from the original checkout. No xmake, link or changed-build game run was done. The new clock therefore has no paired in-game acceptance; the clock-only parts of TS-T16 and TS-T17 can be exercised, while full phase A2/A3 skip tests remain blocked on stage 2b. At five players, the ten-second sample fanout is five messages per interval; at ten it is ten. Server advancement and each client R1 call are O(1), with no actor or body scan. A measured frame cost is pending. The requested QSP stages 1b and 2a are still absent; the earlier QUEST-01/QUEST-07 code in QuestService.cpp was not rewritten into a partial protocol.


## b12b main-thread cuts, stages 0d and 1d (2026-10-03)

Primary source research before editing: [TiltedEvolution's renderer hook](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/client/Games/Skyrim/BSGraphics/BSGraphicsRenderer.cpp) calls the full overlay render from the engine End hook. Its local `C:\Tools\ref\caelvanost\TiltedEvolution\Code\client\Games\Skyrim\BSGraphics\BSGraphicsRenderer.cpp:71-96` implementation has no thread split; it is evidence of the hook's origin, not a safe M2 implementation. [CommonLibSSE-NG's task interface](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/master/include/SKSE/Interfaces.h) `:347-394` offers queued main and UI tasks but allocates a `Task` for `std::function` work and does not specify this mod's X.00 ordering. The existing fixed slot skeleton and deferred-window atomics were used for M1 and M6, rather than adding a second queue or importing these task wrappers. The current tree's `PartyService::LaunchCheckpoint` hand-off check was kept in the execution function, so a queued Continue still refuses a missing common checkpoint.

Offline executable reads used `sk.py fn <VA>` and `sk.py id <ID>` on SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104. Main loop **0x140658870 / 36564** runs the frame in which the mod's X.00 wrapper precedes the original. Load-screen mode **0x1401A6540 / 13363** raises byte `0x14219E5F6` on entry and clears it on exit, supporting the quiet/load gate. `PlayerCharacter::CenterOnCell_Impl` **0x140742DF0 / 40437** can call `TESWorldSpace::LoadCell`, so DoorVote's test cell operation belongs last in slot 7. Native quit **0x140A253E0 / 55490** queues a fade-to-title callback, supporting terminal placement. Save/load queue **0x140625850 / 35769** allocates and enqueues a task, so the deferred save remains in 5s before terminal work. Renderer resize **0x1410097F0 / 77239** reads the HWND and changes presentation state; WndProc now records its request and slot 0b applies it. Renderer End **0x141009F70 / 77246** presents through the swap chain, but the overlay's full poll still runs there; M2 needs a safe front/back draw buffer and remains open. The corpus labels 77239 as `BSPointerHandleManager::IsValid`; its body and the mod call site identify the resize operation, so the body rather than that label was used.

The implemented M1 route moves GameTest/settings wakes and size/placement callbacks into slot 0b. M6 moves the native respawn and restart fade into 3.3, the changed-form and deferred-save family into 5s, and queued quit, checkpoint launch, queued load, DoorVote cell load and Steam lobby quit into slot 7. `CutM1` and `CutM6` default on and read `harness.ini` once at 0a. `mt_status` exposes `m1WrongSlot`, `m6WrongSlot`, and `cutMask`. A flag-off run restores the old 0a ordering of quit, respawn, save work, queued load, and deferred save. The newly moved calls are client local and add O(1) routing per frame, plus the existing O(C) changed-form pass for C queued forms; there is no new per-player loop or wire message. At five and ten players this is still O(1 + C) per client per frame, with no measured p95, network cost change or five/ten-player performance claim.

Limits: the DropIn World/Ui hold of M6 is not wired because those MainQueue lanes have no producers yet; M6 is therefore partial. M2's overlay buffer/title pump and all of M3/M4a remain. No changed build was linked or exercised on two PCs, so the named crashes are not claimed fixed. The local Reviewer A and Reviewer B assistants were unavailable as callable tools for this batch; their independent review has not happened. The required acceptance is a paired run of MTD-T7 (resize/F11 through and after a load, zero `m1WrongSlot`) and the routing parts of MTD-T12 (door vote, checkpoint restart, GameTest load, drop-in, one terminal action per frame, zero `m6WrongSlot`); full MTD-T12 and MTD-T14 also require the DropIn hold and paired observations. MSVC `/Zs` and `git diff --check` are source checks only.

The recent LobbyService input block, Journal movie pump and stale free-camera cleanup already ran from its slot-3.2 OnMainFrame in this tree; this batch adds the `MT::Loading()` guard around those three paths. Its Steam quit previously called native 55490 from slot 3.2 and now drains at slot 7. The requested CharacterService load-hold migration was not attempted as a separate partial move: its `OnUpdate` also consumes `m_nextFrameSpawns` and calls actor creation, which needs the full M5 actor-family migration to keep owner ordering. InventoryService's worn watcher likewise remains on its existing UpdateEvent path pending that migration.

It belongs in the X.00 slot 3.2 weather pump. **ContainerMenu::OpenMenu 0x140911FB0 / 51140** checks the UI pause state, sets the target handle, and calls **UIMessageQueue::AddMessage 0x1401B47F0 / 13631**. **BarterMenu::OpenMenu 0x140901CC0 / 50955** also sets the target handle and posts `kShow`. **0x1411696E0 / 82076** reads the pause-disabled field; **0x14116E0D0 / 82168** searches pending UI messages. If the Ui pool is full, that lease replay still runs through the existing slot-3.2 path. `CutM3a` defaults on, and `mt_status` reports `m3aWrongSlot` and the cut-mask bit. With the cut off, an off-slot direct fallback increments that counter for the negative control. There is no new wire message, and each client drains at most 32 weather and 8 Ui records per X.00: O(1) bounded work independent of P, A and B, with an O(P) host weather fanout already present. ## b16-stage3l hand-off protocol and barrier review (2026-10-03)

Prior source inspected before editing: the original [Skyrim Together server PartyService](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/blob/dev/Code/server/Services/PartyService.cpp) changes gameplay leader inside a running server; it does not transfer this fork's SQLite campaign state, so it cannot seed a new server run. [Steamworks ISteamMatchmaking](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/api/ISteamMatchmaking) says the Steam lobby owner changes when its current owner leaves, and `GetLobbyOwner` requires membership. Lobby ownership alone cannot stand in for the old server's ledger or a verified 5-second host absence. The local `Code/campaign/CampaignLedger.cpp` schema was inspected: `checkpoint_roster` and `quest_items` exist, but the required `checkpoints_meta`, campaign-scoped roster/item rows, content-profile revision, and global-row extract are not present. The [SkyMP WorldState persistence path](https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/master/skymp5-server/cpp/server_guest_lib/WorldState.cpp) was also inspected locally at `C:\Tools\ref\skymp\skymp5-server\cpp\server_guest_lib\WorldState.cpp:703-784`; its asynchronous save storage is useful evidence for separating durable state from transport, but its form upsert model does not provide this campaign's quest/roster lineage.

Decision: reserve S18 at the tail of the server opcode block and define bounded, keyed chunks, with a wire round-trip test. Do not set `HandoffLedgerReady` until an actual extract and atomic import exist. The old server's hand-off barrier now expires after 180 seconds; it refuses invite, auto-join and drop-in admission while active, and rejects C18 from any member absent from the checkpoint roster. `NotifyCheckpointRoster` was already sent to all members; its stale comment was corrected. The `handoff_status` bridge now exposes whether the successor was seeded; `ledger_status` reports the selected checkpoint and whether this PC has its `.ledger` file. The S18 sender, `.ledger` sidecar, C19 import, campaign identity, C18 alignment, C20 lineage choice, server-host decision and paired game test remain open. These changes do not establish a working hand-off.

No new live probe or offset guess was added. The existing b10d corpus reads remain relevant: `Game.QuitToMainMenu` **55490 / 0x140A253E0** and `BGSSaveLoadManager::QueueSaveLoadTask` **35769 / 0x140625850** (see the b10d entry above). The present server-only barrier change does not call either function. Reviewer A and Reviewer B were not callable in this sandbox; no independent conclusion is claimed. MSVC `/Zs` covered the changed server units and wire test; it does not substitute for a linked protocol test or paired in-game observation.


## 2026-10-03: b14b quest stage Mode A, stage 1b

Primary source inspection before patching: [upstream Skyrim Together client QuestService](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/dev/Code/client/Services/Generic/QuestService.cpp) sends one update per stage-item event from its sink and applies a received stage through `ScriptSetStage`. [Upstream server QuestService](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution/dev/Code/server/Services/QuestService.cpp) relays a stage and rewrites an existing quest's Started status to StageUpdate. Those paths were rejected for this batch because a stage may have zero or several items, their per-item conditions can differ between PCs, and Startup/Shutdown bypass 25004. The local alias snapshot message and server's ledger commit were retained as the transport and durable commit path. [CommonLibSSE-NG TESQuest declarations](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/master/include/RE/T/TESQuest.h) corroborate the quest flags, stage list and objective layout; the exact runtime offsets and call sites below were checked against the 1.7.104 executable instead of taken from a library version.

Executable functions queried directly with `C:\Users\mwalt\AppData\Local\Programs\Python\Python312\python.exe C:\Tools\skyrim_re\sk.py fn <VA>` before hook changes:

| Address Library ID | VA | Evidence and decision |
|---|---|---|
| 25252 | 0x1403E19B0 | The runner sets the done bit, walks items from stage+8 and calls the item function at +0xB0 (call site 0x1403E1A60). It returns zero for an itemless stage, so F2 tests the hook's local-run counter for repeat stages. H1 wraps this entry to capture SetStage, Startup, Shutdown and CompleteAll. |
| 25004 | 0x1403D0DC0 | SetStage requires the enabled bit and a matching stage, raises +0x228, calls 25252 and queues 0x80000000. The single 25004 hook orders override, follower allowed context, suppression, alias gate and native. |
| 25000 / 25001 / 23231 | 0x1403D0B20 / 0x1403D0BB0 / 0x140382A60 | Startup and shutdown call 25252 then raise currentStage; CompleteAll calls 25252 with force 1 and does not raise currentStage. H1 classifies by the checked return sites 0x1403D0B68, 0x1403D0BF8 and 0x140382A9F. |
| 25260 / 25272 | 0x1403E1F60 / 0x1403E2720 | The item function evaluates local conditions only when force is zero; its failure branch posts a compact done event through 25272. F4 rewrites the sole 25260 call site after validating the opcode and old callee; a skipped item posts that done event so native waiters can finish. |
| 25271 | 0x1403E24E0 | Event send invokes sinks inline and stops after a nonzero sink result. H2 returns `kOk` and only sets the matching innermost H1 frame's item bit. |
| 23933 / 24991 / 24988 | 0x14039B500 / 0x1403D0630 / 0x1403D03D0 | Objective state writes at +0x1F and quest completed/failed flag setters are captured after the native call as dirty quest IDs. 24988(true) sets both completed and failed and refuses a quest already completed, so F6 writes the failed bit directly in that case. |
| 25014 / 32485 | 0x1403D1230 / 0x14053AA50 | Reset clears stage done bits, currentStage and objective states; BeginStartUpQuest calls it first. H5 excludes return site 0x14053AABA so each ordinary start does not generate a Reset record. |
| 25003 / 24987 / 40516 | 0x1403D0CB0 / 0x1403D0390 / 0x14074B6A0 | F3 uses startNow 25003 with a bounded retry, L2 uses the existing 24987-equivalent StopQuest, and F6 refreshes the player's quest target state after a native completed/failed setter. |

The 1b wire extends the existing alias snapshot with a presence bit, a per-quest sequence block, a runner mask block and objective states. `NotifyQuestAliasFills` adds Replay; no reserved opcode number was changed. The server keeps the sender's Started status for sequenced records and includes kind, sequence, run count and mask in the ledger payload. H1/H1b/H2/H3/H5 capture into one numbered queue; H4 sends at most 32 records and 32 dirty quests per App update, with one record per quest per tick after a fill change. F1 stores the mask and queues the record without engine work; F2 applies at X.00 in slot 5 with a limit of eight records and two start attempts per frame; F4 and F5 force the host item mask for stage and startup/shutdown runners. Existing bundled stage retention and the post-SetStage `IsStageDone` test are consumed by this one pipeline.

Stage 1b is **partial** against the full QSP design. Stage 2a's commit acknowledgments, durable outbox, ring replay, gap resync, snapshot, checkpoint cursor and handover are absent. In this build an overflow, disconnect gap, missing local quest or failed server commit can strand a per-quest sequence; the code logs the condition and does not claim recovery. The per-quest pending cap is 64, the global pending and host unsent caps are 4096. F3 uses the default allowlist but has no SideQuest opt-in switch. The follower's legacy alias-only repair sweep remains in the App lane pending the MTD migration. The `DropInService::Status()` gate recognizes `idle` and `joined`; a separate phase accessor would be preferable when MTD migrates this service. QSP K6c's direct state write has no paired game observation. The setter and call-site hooks have only offline syntax checks, not an installed-detour or in-game result. Stage 2c must replace the interim atomic party sample with SAM's AuthoritySnapshot, including `pendingHost`.

Offline checks: seven affected translation units and the encoding test compile under MSVC `/Zs` with the original read-only Libraries and local headers; the isolated current-source serializer executable passes 11 assertions in two cases. These verify wire round trips and invalid combinations, not the hook ABI, scene effects, two-PC parity or five-player performance. No xmake, commit, deployment or paired game run was performed. Reviewer A/B were not invoked under COMMON.md's explicit independent-review waiver; no independent review is claimed. Cost per run is one leader request plus P-1 server relays: four follower sends at P=5 and nine at P=10; payload includes 12 bytes per included alias and three bytes per objective plus the fixed header. FillsIncluded=0 avoids repeat alias bytes. Capture is event driven; H4 caps sends at 32 per App update and F2 checks at most 32 pending quest IDs per attempt with eight attempts per frame. The existing alias read path can scan entities for a dynamic reference alias; it needs profiling before a five-player target can be claimed.


## b15-stage0f whole-set stage 0f (2026-10-03)

Primary source search and inspection: [CommonLibSSE-NG ExtraDataList source](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/master/src/RE/E/ExtraDataList.cpp) uses read and write guards around presence and list access; its [BSAtomic source](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/master/src/RE/B/BSAtomic.cpp) maps read/write acquire and release to AE IDs 68233, 68234, 68239 and 68240. The local read-only mirror at `C:/Tools/ref/CommonLibSSE-NG` was inspected at these files. Adopted its lock type and engine-call mapping. The older mod's `BSRecursiveLock` implementation was rejected for `ExtraDataList+0x18`: its count 1 is a reader count to the engine, and it writes the engine owner field without the writer bit. The current project [Skyrim Together source](https://github.com/tiltedphoques/TiltedEvolution) was considered for the lobby and player cell path; its generic cell packets have no lobby-world marker, so this batch uses an empty interior cell sentinel and a server-side empty-cell branch. The server `CellIdComponent::IsInRange` now refuses empty endpoints; otherwise two lobby members with empty cells would appear in range.

Executable evidence, read with `C:/Users/mwalt/AppData/Local/Programs/Python/Python312/python.exe C:/Tools/skyrim_re/sk.py fn <VA>` on SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104:

| Address Library ID | VA | Observation and use |
|---|---|---|
| 68233 | 0x140CE3250 | LockForRead CAS increments count only without the writer bit; recursive owner also increments. `TryLockForRead` uses the same count transition and 68239 to release. |
| 68234 | 0x140CE32D0 | LockForWrite takes count from zero to `0x80000001`, then sets owner thread. `ExtraDataList::Add` uses this engine call. |
| 68239 | 0x140CE3510 | UnlockForRead atomically decrements count. |
| 68240 | 0x140CE3520 | UnlockForWrite clears owner and count at `0x80000001`, otherwise decrements the recursive count. |
| 36601 | 0x14065B6C0 | Reset entry runs 13363 and later clears Main `+0x11`; `WorldGeneration` rises on entry. |
| 52118 | 0x140959160 | New Game start sets the new-game state and starts its native flow; `WorldGeneration` rises on this call. |
| 55490 | 0x140A253E0 | Quit queues a `FadeThenMainMenuCallback`; this is why the current direct Main `+0x11` fallback still needs the M6b Fader precondition. |
| 13363 | 0x1401A6540 | Load-screen mode sets and clears byte `0x14219E5F6`, while posting UI messages; a Loading Menu kShow cannot be the first hold edge. |
| 42338 | 0x1407AD900 | PlayerControls tests remapMode at `+0x50` before dispatching gameplay input. The guard now iterates `UI.cpp`'s exact `kAllowList`. |
| 68544 | 0x140CEF980 | PopInputContext searches ControlMap stack data at `+0x108` with count at `+0x118`; the context step calls it for context 13. |

Current implementation: `ExtraDataList` uses `BSReadWriteLock` and engine read/write calls; WorldStateService `EnableParent` uses a bounded protocol try-read and counts/logs `mtd_extra_busy`. The baseline requeues that reference after a busy result. The main-loop watchdog records a main-thread `GetByType` lock address and, on a stall, reads its owner/count through `ReadProcessMemory` for `mtd_extra_lock_stall`; the reference ID is available for the `EnableParent` path. `SharedWorld` excludes the live lobby, title, reset and load; the main gate and WorldState's native-tail pump test it. `WorldGeneration` rises at native reset, native New Game, Lobby Enter / EndLobby and `LoadGeneration`. CharacterService holds remote looks, spawns and assignments by server ID until SharedWorld Idle, applies held work from slot 3.2 and does not expire the records by age. `CutM6b=0` keeps the old look/spawn deferral path; it does not restore every old side effect. The server receives an empty interior cell for a lobby member, so its range graph has no cell for that member; `CharacterService::CanReplicateTo` refuses a cell-less recipient. The slot-3.3 context step runs once per WorldGeneration when SharedWorld becomes true, popping TFC context 13 and clearing recorded lobby residue. MSVC `/Zs` passed for every changed client TU and the changed server PlayerService and CharacterService TUs with original read-only dependencies. `git diff --check` passed. Neither a linked build nor a paired in-game run has been performed, so W1, W8 and W9 are not claimed fixed live.

Enabled observations: for MTD-T22, hold engine read locks on twenty references while the changed build runs the world pump, then check that `mtd_extra_busy` only rises during held locks and no `mtd_main_stall` or orphan lock word appears. For X-T57, admit a joiner at the fire for 60 s and verify `shared_world=0 auth_cell=empty (lobby)`, the server's `no shared-world cell` line, and no range/spawn traffic for cell 976F. For the hold part of MTD-T24 and X-T60, inject look, spawn and assignment messages across reset/load/creator windows and verify `held_looks`, `held_spawns` and `held_assignments` rise, then names and copies apply once after `shared_world=1`; the terminal-kind and double-reset assertions are still unavailable. SP-T19 checks `sp_orphan_context`, `sp_lobby_residue`, stack `[0]` and attack/sheathe/sprint after New/Continue from the fire. Risks: generic main-thread `GetByType` still uses the engine's unbounded read call, not a per-caller retry contract; incoming WorldState packets may accumulate behind the lobby gate and need a bounded queue policy; the remaining M6b Terminal kinds and `mtd_reset_double` are not implemented; no five-player cost or paired-gameplay result exists. Reviewer A and Reviewer B were not callable in this sandbox, so there is no independent review result for this batch.


## b30-friendly-fire: player-only hit tolerance and the follower Threat relay (2026-10-03)

Primary sources inspected: [SKSE vanilla ObjectReference.psc](https://github.com/ianpatt/skse64/blob/master/scripts/vanilla/ObjectReference.psc) declares IgnoreFriendlyHits and IsIgnoringFriendlyHits; [CommonLibSSE-NG TESObjectREFR.h](https://github.com/CharmedBaryon/CommonLibSSE-NG/blob/master/include/RE/T/TESObjectREFR.h) names reference bit20 as IgnoreFriendlyHits. [Precision ContactListener.cpp](https://github.com/ersh1/Precision/blob/main/src/Havok/ContactListener.cpp), inspected at C:/Tools/ref/Precision/src/Havok/ContactListener.cpp:340-386, can suppress teammate and non-hostile collision. Its approach was rejected because this issue requires hits to retain their damage and reactions and the fork has a separate forced-combat relay. [No Follower Attack Collision](https://github.com/LXIV-CXXVIII/No-Follower-Attack-Collision) was found as another related source repository, but its source files were not retrievable with the available web tool; no implementation claim relies on it. Global ally-hit setting changes and setting IgnoreFriendlyHits on every ally were rejected because they affect ordinary NPC combat.

All executable bodies below were checked with C:/Users/mwalt/AppData/Local/Programs/Python/Python312/python.exe C:/Tools/skyrim_re/sk.py fn id:<ID> against SkyrimSE.exe 1.7.104.

| VA / Address Library ID | Evidence |
|---|---|
| 0x1406CCC50 / 38626 | Native hit reaction takes victim and attacker. The timed friendly-hit count branch requires attacker == player singleton, tests the victim's faction reaction to that singleton, chooses friend/ally combat or non-combat allowance, and returns before assault while the count is within that allowance. A puppet attacker skips this player branch and may reach assault body 37425. The victim's IgnoreFriendlyHits bit20 changes the allowance to 1000 inside the player branch; it does not identify a puppet as the player. |
| 0x140185180 / 12619 and 0x1401852D0 / 12621 | The hit-count record adds a timed hit and prunes old hits against fFriendHitTimer; fFriendMinimumLastHitTime limits how often hits are added. Atlas game_settings.tsv gives 10 seconds and 0.5 seconds respectively. |
| 0x1406CA640 / 38586 | Combat helper resolves the hitter from hit data and passes that hitter with the victim into 38626. |
| 0x140682ED0 / 37425 | Assault body creates a Crime for a non-guard puppet, gathers witnesses, and can spread an alarm; only a singleton attacker enters the player crime-enemy path. It does not replace teammate with player. |
| 0x1406A01C0 / 37717; 0x140681DE0 / 37422; 0x140683C70 / 37427 | SetPlayerTeammate writes Actor+0xE8 bit26. Steal and trespass replace a teammate criminal with the local player singleton. Expanding the teammate flag for friendly fire would risk charging the host for puppet crimes. |

Atlas game_settings.tsv lists iAllyHitNonCombatAllowed=3, iAllyHitCombatAllowed=1000, iFriendHitNonCombatAllowed=0, and iFriendHitCombatAllowed=3 with a Skyrim.esm override to 4. Ralof's exact reaction and live setting values remain to be observed.

The fork has independent routes. CombatService::OnHitEvent is disabled, but native hits still run. CharacterService creates a remote player Character puppet and sets IgnoreFriendlyHit on the puppet, not on Ralof. StealthService registers that puppet as a player teammate on the host. Actor.cpp::HookAuthorityDamageActor emits a Threat when the follower hits a remote NPC proxy. StealthService::OnNotify queues host StartCombat against the puppet after token/life checks, without consulting the native friendly-hit count. A separate Crime response can queue guards against a charged follower assault, and CombatService::RunTargetUpdates can queue combat for observers already seeing a party opponent or hostile puppet. Thus the report is consistent with the native player-only gate, but an isolated hook at 38626 would leave the explicit Threat and possibly Crime paths active. The exact Ralof transition has not been observed with a changed build.

Changes: Code/client/Games/Skyrim/Actor.cpp::HookHitReaction wraps ID 38626 without changing its arguments or return path, and logs player-singleton versus remote-puppet identity at native hit entry. Code/client/Services/Generic/StealthService.cpp::OnNotify logs when the host Threat path queues StartCombat, including victim/attacker IDs, sequence, damage, player-singleton and teammate flags, and victim IgnoreFriendlyHits flag. Both logs are rate-limited to at most one line per 250 ms per process at their respective sites, O(1) per event and with no scan. The native entry log records eligibility for the player branch, not the final hit-count result. The existing Crime response log reports the other relay. No behavior fix was landed: a universal correction must coordinate native tolerance, Threat, Crime, and hostile-target rules while preserving per-player crime ownership.

Required changed-build two-PC observation: in cart-free Helgen courtyard compare host and follower first and second hits on Ralof, with the new decision and existing Crime response log lines; hit a hostile bandit as a negative control; commit a shopkeeper crime and verify it charges the follower, not the host. Repeat melee, ranged, and spell impacts before calling any universal behavior fixed. There has been no changed-build in-game run or five-player performance measurement. COMMON.md expressly exempts this batch from Reviewer A/B because Reviewer B cannot start here; neither reviewed this diagnosis.

Validation for b30: MSVC /Zs passed for the edited Actor.cpp and StealthService.cpp using source/includes from this batch copy, original read-only Libraries, and the original dependency manifest flags with PCH disabled. The original PCH could not be reused across the two repository paths because its headers point at the original tree; both fresh-header syntax checks exited 0. git -c core.safecrlf=false diff --check passed. No xmake, linked build, commit, deployment, or paired game run was performed. The current Actor.cpp already has HookStealAlarm and HookTrespassAlarm guards that return for a host-side remote player puppet; the shopkeeper control verifies those guards and the follower's own charge in game rather than assuming them from code. The new native detour must be checked in a linked build and paired run before attributing a gameplay outcome to its logs.
